§ 10.01 TITLE OF CODE.
   All ordinances of a permanent and general nature of the town, as revised,
codified, rearranged, renumbered, and consolidated into component codes,
titles, chapters and sections, shall be known and designated as the “The Code
of the Town of Fort Laramie, Wyoming” for which designation “code of
ordinances”, “codified ordinances”, or “code” may be substituted. Code title,
chapter, and section headings do not constitute any part of the law as
contained in the code.
Statutory reference:
   Adoption of codes by reference, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-119
   Authority of the town to cause compilations, codifications, and
comprehensive revisions of ordinances, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103
   Manner and form of enactment of codifications or general revisions of
ordinances, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-115
   Publication of recodification of ordinances, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-116
§ 10.02 INTERPRETATION.
   Unless otherwise provided herein, or by law or implication required, the
same rules of construction, definition, and application shall govern the
interpretation of this code as those governing the interpretation of state law.
§ 10.03 APPLICATION TO FUTURE ORDINANCES.
   All provisions of this chapter compatible with future legislation shall
apply to ordinances hereafter adopted amending or supplementing this code
unless otherwise specifically provided.
§ 10.04 CONSTRUCTION OF CODE.
   (A)   This code is a codification of previously existing laws, amendments
thereto, and newly enacted laws. Any previously existing law or amendment
thereto reenacted by this code shall continue in operation and effect, as if it
had not been repealed by this code. All rules and regulations adopted under
laws reenacted in this code shall remain in full force and effect unless
repealed or amended subsequent to the enactment of this code.
   (B)   Any appropriation repealed and reenacted by this code is continued
only for the period designated in the original enactment of that appropriation.
   (C)   The numerical order and position of sections in this code does not
resolve a conflict between two or more sections.
   (D)   Any irreconcilable conflict between sections shall be resolved by
reference to the dates that the sections were originally enacted. The section
most recently enacted supersedes any conflicting section or division.
   (E)   All references within a section of this code to any section of
previously existing laws refer to the numbers in the original enactment.
   (F)   (1)   The numerical designations and descriptive headings assigned to
the various titles, chapters, subchapters, or sections of this code, as
originally enacted or as added by amendment, are not law, and may be altered by
the compilers of this or any subsequent codification, in any official
publication, to more clearly indicate its content. These descriptive headings
are for organizational purposes only and do not affect the meaning,
application, or construction of the law they precede.
      (2)   Each note following a section of this code is for reference
purposes only and is not a part of the section.
   (G)   All references to any section of this code refer to all subsequent
amendments to that section, unless otherwise provided.
§ 10.05 RULES OF INTERPRETATION; DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   Rules of interpretation. This code shall be construed by the following
rules unless the construction is plainly repugnant to the legislative intent or
context of the provision.
      (1)   Words and phrases shall be taken in their plain, ordinary, and
usual sense. Technical words and phrases having a peculiar and appropriate
meaning in law shall be understood according to their technical import.
      (2)   Where a section requires an act to be done which, by law, an agent
or deputy may perform in addition to the principal, the performance of the act
by an authorized deputy or agent is valid.
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this code of ordinances, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      CLERK/TREASURER. An appointed official responsible for managing both the
administrative records and financial operations of the town.
      COMPUTATION OF TIME. The time within which an act is to be done shall be
computed by excluding the first and including the last day unless the last day
is a Saturday, Sunday, or legal holiday, in which case the period shall run
until the end of the next day which is not a Saturday, Sunday, or legal
holiday.
      COUNCIL. The Town Council of Fort Laramie, Wyoming.
      COUNTY. Goshen County, Wyoming.
      DEPUTY CLERK. A government official who assists the Clerk/Treasurer in
performing administrative tasks, maintaining legal records, collecting monies
owed to the town and performing public services.
      GENDER. Words importing the masculine gender include the feminine and
neuter.
      GOVERNING BODY. The council constituting the elected legislative body of
the town.
      HIGHWAY. Includes bridges, roads, and streets, unless otherwise expressly
provided.
      IN THE TOWN or IN THE CORPORATE LIMITS OF THE TOWN. Includes any
territory within the corporate limits of the town, the police jurisdiction
thereof, and any other territory over which regulatory power has been conferred
on the town by general or special act.
      JOINT AUTHORITY. All words giving a JOINT AUTHORITY to three or more
persons shall be construed as giving such authority to a majority of such
persons.
      MONTH. One calendar month.
      MUNICIPAL COURT CLERK. A non-judicial official who manages the daily
administration, record handling, and procedural tasks for town court, which
handles local ordinance violations. They process citations, schedule hearings,
assist the judge with proceedings, and manage fines.
      NUMBER. Words used in the singular include the plural, and words used in
the plural include the singular.
      OATH. Includes an affirmation in all cases in which, by law, an
affirmation may be substituted for an oath, and in such cases the words “swear”
and “sworn” are equivalent to the words “affirm” and affirmed”.
      OWNER. When applied to a building or land, shall include not only the
owner of the whole but also any part owner, joint owner, tenant in common, or
joint tenant of the whole or a part of such building or land and shall include
any agent of such owner, and where such OWNER is a body corporate, it shall
include the managing agent or officer within the town.
      PERSON. Includes a firm, partnership, association of persons,
corporation, organization, or any other group acting as a unit, as well as an
individual.
      PERSONAL PROPERTY. Includes every species of property, except real
property.
      PRECEDING and FOLLOWING. Next before and next after, respectively.
      PROPERTY. Includes real and personal property.
      REAL PROPERTY. Includes lands, tenements, and hereditaments.
      RULES OF CIVIL PROCEDURE. The Wyoming Rules of Civil Procedure.
      RULES OF CRIMINAL PROCEDURE. The Wyoming Rules of Criminal Procedure.
      SHALL and MAY. SHALL is mandatory and MAY is permissive.
      SIDEWALK. Any portion of a street between the curb line and the adjacent
property line intended for the use of the pedestrians.
      STATE. The State of Wyoming.
      STATE LAW. References to STATE LAW and Wyoming Statues shall mean the
Wyoming Statutes.
      STREET. Includes public streets, avenues, highways, roads, alleys, lanes,
bridges, and the approaches thereto and all other public thoroughfares in the
town.
      TENANT or OCCUPANT. When applied to a building or land, shall mean any
person who occupies the whole or a part of such building or land, whether alone
or with others.
      TENSE. Words used in the past or present tense include the future as well
as the past and present.
      TOWN. The Town of Fort Laramie, Wyoming.
      TOWN COUNCIL. Consists of the elected or appointed Council persons and,
together with the Mayor, shall constitute the legislative body of the town.
      TOWNSHIP. The township or townships in which the town is located.
      WRITTEN or IN WRITING. Include printing, lithographing, or other modes of
representing words and letters. Where the written signature of a person is
required, the terms mean the proper handwriting of the person or the person’s
mark.
      YEAR. One calendar year, unless otherwise expressly provided.
(Prior Code, § 1-5)
Statutory reference:
   Definitions and rules of construction, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 8-1-102 and 8-1-103
   Definitions of “city or town” and “town”, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-101
   Legal holidays, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 8-4-101 and 8-4-104
   Related provisions regarding “person”, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-101
   Related provisions regarding “Town Council”, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-101
§ 10.06 SEVERABILITY.
   (A)   If any section of this code now enacted or subsequently amended or its
application to any person or circumstances is held invalid, the invalidity does
not affect other sections that can be given effect without the invalid section
or application.
   (B)   Except in the case of a section or amendment to this code containing a
nonseverability provision, each division or part of every section is severable.
If any portion or application of a section is held invalid, the invalidity does
not affect the remainder of the section unless:
      (1)   The remainder is so essentially and inseparably connected with and
so dependent upon the invalid provision or application that it cannot be
presumed that the remainder would have been enacted without the invalid
provision or application; or
      (2)   The remainder is incomplete and incapable of being executed in
accordance with the legislative intent without the invalid provision or
application.
   (C)   This section applies to every section of this code regardless of
whether a section was enacted before or after the passage of this code.
   (D)   The repeal of a section or amendment stating that the provisions of a
chapter, subchapter, or section are severable as provided in division (B) above
does not affect the operation of division (B) with respect to that chapter,
subchapter, or section.
§ 10.07 REFERENCE TO OTHER SECTIONS.
   Whenever in one section reference is made to another section hereof, the
reference shall extend and apply to the section referred to as subsequently
amended, revised, recodified, or renumbered unless the subject matter is
changed or materially altered by the amendment or revision.
§ 10.08 REFERENCE TO OFFICES; NAME DESIGNATIONS.
   (A)   Reference to offices. Reference to a public office or officer shall be
deemed to apply to any office, officer, or employee of this municipality
exercising the powers, duties, or functions contemplated in the provision,
irrespective of any transfer of functions or change in the official title of
the functionary.
   (B)   Name designations. Whenever any ordinance or resolution of the Council
refers to any board, bureau, commission, division, department, officer, agency,
authority, or instrumentality of any government, and that name designation is
incorrectly stated; on the effective date of that ordinance or following the
effective date, the rights, powers, duties, or liabilities placed with that
entity are or were transferred to a different entity; then the named board,
bureau, commission, department, division, officer, agency, authority, or
instrumentality, whether correctly named in the ordinance at its effective date
or not, means that correctly named entity, or the entity to which the duties,
liabilities, powers, and rights were transferred.
§ 10.09 ERRORS AND OMISSIONS.
   (A)   If a manifest error is discovered consisting of: the misspelling of
any words; the omission of any word or words necessary to express the intention
of the provisions affected; the use of a word or words to which no meaning can
be attached; or the use of a word or words when another word or words was
clearly intended to express the intent, the spelling shall be corrected and the
word or words supplied, omitted, or substituted as will conform with the
manifest intention, and the provisions shall have the same effect as though the
correct words were contained in the text as originally published.
   (B)   No alteration shall be made or permitted if any question exists
regarding the nature or extent of the error.
§ 10.10 REASONABLE TIME.
   In all cases where an ordinance requires an act to be done in a reasonable
time or requires reasonable notice to be given, REASONABLE TIME OR NOTICE shall
be deemed to mean the time which is necessary for a prompt performance of the
act or the giving of the notice.
§ 10.11 REPEAL OR MODIFICATION OF CODE SECTION.
   (A)   When a section of this code is repealed which repealed a former
section or law adopted prior to the enactment of this code, the former section
or law is not revived unless it so expressly provides.
   (B)   (1)   The repeal of any section shall not extinguish or release any
penalty, forfeiture, or liability incurred under the section, unless the
repealing section so expressly provides.
      (2)   The section shall be treated as still remaining in force for the
purposes of sustaining any proper action or prosecution for the enforcement of
the penalty, forfeiture, or liability.
§ 10.12 LIMITATION PERIODS.
   The running of any period of limitations or any requirement of notice
contained in any law, whether applicable to civil causes or proceedings, or to
the prosecution of offenses, or for the recovery of penalties and forfeitures,
contained in a law repealed and reenacted by this code, shall not be affected
by the repeal and reenactment; but all suits, proceedings, and prosecutions for
causes arising or acts committed prior to the effective date of this code may
be commenced and prosecuted with the same effect as if this code had not been
enacted.
§ 10.13 ORDINANCES UNAFFECTED.
   All ordinances of a temporary or special nature and all other ordinances
pertaining to subjects not embraced in this code shall remain in full force and
effect unless herein repealed expressly or by necessary implication.
§ 10.14 ORDINANCES WHICH AMEND OR SUPPLEMENT CODE.
   (A)   If the legislative body shall desire to amend any existing chapter or
section of this code, the chapter or section shall be specifically repealed and
a new chapter or section, containing the desired amendment, substituted in its
place.
   (B)   Any ordinance which is proposed to add to the existing code a new
chapter or section shall indicate, with reference to the arrangement of this
code, the proper number of the chapter or section. In addition to an indication
thereof as may appear in the text of the proposed ordinance, a caption or title
shall be shown in concise form above the ordinance.
§ 10.15 SECTION HISTORIES; STATUTORY REFERENCES.
   (A)   As histories for the code sections, the specific number and passage
date of the original ordinance, and amending ordinances, if any, are listed
following the text of the code section. Example:
      (Ord. 10, passed 5-13-1960; Ord. 15, passed 1-1-1970; Ord. 20, passed 1-
1-1980; Ord. 25, passed 1-1-1985)
   (B)   (1)   If a statutory cite is included in the history, this indicates
that the text of the section reads substantially the same as the statute.
Example:
         (Wyo. Stat. § 12-2-201)
      (2)   If a statutory cite is set forth as a “statutory reference”
following the text of the section, this indicates that the reader should refer
to that statute for further information. Example:
         § 39.01 PUBLIC RECORDS AVAILABLE.
            This municipality shall make available to any person for inspection
or copying all public records, unless otherwise exempted by state law.
         Statutory reference:
            Inspection of public records, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-2-201
   (C)   If a section of this code is derived from the previous code of
ordinances of the town published and subsequently amended, the previous code
section number shall be indicated in the history by “(Prior Code, §      )”.
§ 10.16 PRESERVATION OF PENALTIES, OFFENSES, RIGHTS AND LIABILITIES.
   All offenses committed under laws in force prior to the effective date of
this code shall be prosecuted and remain punishable as provided by those laws.
This code does not affect any rights or liabilities accrued, penalties
incurred, or proceedings begun prior to the effective date of this code. The
liabilities, proceedings, and rights are continued; punishments, penalties, or
forfeitures shall be enforced and imposed as if this code had not been enacted.
In particular, any agreement granting permission to utilize highway rights-of-
way; contracts entered into or franchises granted; the acceptance,
establishment, or vacation of any highway; and the election of corporate
officers shall remain valid in all respects, as if this code had not been
enacted.
§ 10.99 GENERAL PENALTY.
   (A)   Whenever in this code or in any ordinance, resolution, or regulation
promulgated by any officer or agency of the town under authority vested by law
or ordinance, any act is prohibited or is declared to be unlawful, an offense,
or a misdemeanor, and no specific penalty is provided therefor, the violation
of any such provision of this code or any such ordinance, resolution, or
regulation shall be punished by a fine of not more than $750; provided, that
the imposition of any such fine shall not bar institution of appropriate legal
actions or proceedings by the town to restrain, correct, or abate the
violations, nor shall the institution of such legal actions or proceedings be
deemed a bar to the imposition of such fine.
   (B)   Except as otherwise provided, each day any violation of this code or
any such ordinance, resolution, or regulation continues shall constitute a
separate offense.
   (C)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      CRIMINAL ACTIVITY. Any crime for which there is a plea of guilty, nolo
contendere, or verdict of guilty upon which a judgment of conviction may be
rendered and any other crime which is admitted by the defendant, whether or not
prosecuted.
      PECUNIARY DAMAGE. All damages which a victim could recover against the
defendant in a civil action arising out of the same facts or event, except
punitive damages and damages for pain, suffering, mental anguish, and loss of
consortium.
      RESTITUTION. Full or partial payment of pecuniary damages to a victim.
      VICTIM. A person who has suffered pecuniary damages as a result of a
defendant’s criminal activities.
   (D)   At the time of sentencing a defendant for any misdemeanor conviction,
if the court desires to require restitution, the court shall fix a reasonable
amount as restitution owed to each victim for pecuniary damages resulting from
the defendant’s criminal activity and shall include its determination as a
special finding in the judgment of conviction.
   (E)   The court may issue execution against the defendant for any assets
including wages which would be subject to attachment to satisfy the order.
   (F)   The court’s determination of the amount of restitution owed under this
section shall not be admissible as evidence in any civil action.
(Prior Code, § 1-8)
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to enforce ordinances, resolutions, and regulations by
imposition of fines or imprisonment, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103
CHAPTER 11: TOWN STANDARDS
Section
   
11.01   Passage of bylaws, ordinances, and resolutions
   
11.02   Town Seal
§ 11.01 PASSAGE OF BYLAWS, ORDINANCES, AND RESOLUTIONS.
   (A)   All enactments of law by the Town Council intended for the guidance or
government of persons or property within the said town, or the officers of said
town, shall be by ordinance.
   (B)   Any proposed ordinance may be presented at any meeting of the Town
Council and shall be presented in writing, bear the signature of the Council
members proposing the same, and shall be read at length by the designated Clerk
at the meeting at which the same is introduced.
   (C)   No ordinance shall be finally passed and adopted by the Town Council
except at a regular meeting following the meeting at which the same is
introduced; provided however, that by a vote of any four of five members of the
Council, the provisions of this section may be suspended and an emergency
declared and an ordinance finally passed and adopted at the meeting at which it
is introduced, but in that event the proposed ordinance shall recite the
emergency which is the occasion for suspending the provisions of this section.
   (D)   Except in case of emergency as provided in division (C) above, every
ordinance shall be published by posting certified copies thereof in three
public places in the town for at least ten days before the same shall take
effect.
   (E)   All ordinances passed and adopted by the Town Council shall, within
ten days after the passage thereof, be signed by the Mayor and attested by the
designated Clerk and be recorded in a book kept for that purpose, and no
ordinance of the town shall be capable of being enforced until the same shall
have been recorded and signed as herein provided.
   (F)   On the passage of any bylaw, ordinance, or resolution, a roll call
shall be had and the “ayes” and “nays” shall be taken and entered in the
journal.
(Ord. 1, passed 5-19-1925)
§ 11.02 TOWN SEAL.
   (A)   The Town Seal shall consist of the impression of two concentric
circles; between the circles shall appear the words “Town of Fort Laramie,
Wyoming”, and within the inner circle, which shall be one and one-fourth inches
in diameter, shall appear the words “Corporate Seal”.
   (B)   The Town Seal shall at all times remain in the care, custody, and
control of the Town Clerk/Treasurer or the Clerk’s designee, who shall be
responsible therefor or for its misuse upon the Clerk’s official bond.
(Prior Code, § 1-9) (Ord. 5, passed 5-19-1925)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 30.01 OFFICERS DESIGNATED.
   (A)   The officers of the town shall be:
      (1)   The Mayor and four Council members, all of whom shall be elected;
and
      (2)   The Clerk/Treasurer, Attorney, Law Enforcement Officer, and
Municipal Judge, all of whom shall be appointed.
   (B)   The Clerk/Treasurer, Attorney, Law Enforcement Officer, and Municipal
Judge shall be appointed by the Mayor with the consent of the Town Council and
may be removed by the Mayor. Appointments shall be made by the Mayor at the
first regular meeting of the governing body following election and
qualification.
   (C)   Heads of departments as specified by separate ordinances codified
herein shall be appointed by the Mayor with the consent of the Town Council and
may be removed by the Mayor.
   (D)   All other appointments, except the appointment of members of a board
or commission, and removals shall be made by the Mayor without the consent of
the Council, unless consent is required by separate statute.
   (E)   The Town Council may, by resolution, combine the offices of Clerk and
Treasurer.
(Prior Code, § 2-7) (Ord. 136, passed 7-12-1988)
§ 30.02 APPOINTIVE OFFICE; CONDITIONS.
   (A)   The appointive officers specified in §
30.01(A), with the exception of the Attorney, shall be qualified electors and
residents of the town, and before their commission shall issue to them, they
shall take the oath of office as required for county officers.
   (B)   The Clerk/Treasurer, Mayor and bank signatories shall post bonds for
the performance of their duties. Said bonds shall be from a surety company
licensed to do business in the state and shall be in an amount as determined by
the Town Council, payable to the town, and shall be filed with the Clerk/
Treasurer. The town shall pay the bond costs and any filing costs.
   (C)   The appointive officers of the town shall hold their office from the
date of their appointment and qualification until June 1 following a regular
municipal election, unless such officer is removed, resigns, or otherwise
becomes disqualified.
(Prior Code, § 2-7) (Ord. 136, passed 7-12-1988)
§ 30.03 DUTIES AND RESPONSIBILITIES.
   (A)   (1)   Council members shall attend all meetings of the governing body
and shall lend such aid and assistance in the direction and management of
government as may be necessary under the laws of the state and the ordinances
of the town.
      (2)   The members of the governing body may declare a vacancy to exist in
the office of the Council member if any Council member shall fail to attend
three consecutive regular meetings of the governing body.
   (B)   (1)   The duties of the Mayor shall be those as set forth in the
Wyoming Statutes.
      (2)   All other officers and employees of the town shall perform the
duties required of them by the administrative official, the laws of the state,
and the ordinances of the town as the same may pertain to them.
(Prior Code, § 2-7) (Ord. 136, passed 7-12-1988; Ord. 173, passed 6-4-1996)
§ 30.04 SALARIES.
   (A)   The compensation for the Mayor and each Council Member of the town is
hereby established and amended as follows:
      (1)   The Mayor shall receive $50 per month; and
      (2)   Each Council Member shall receive $25 per regular meeting actually
attended each month.
   (B)   Such compensation shall be paid monthly and shall commence upon the
start of each official’s new term of office following election or appointment,
or the after July 1, 2026, which ever comes first, notwithstanding any other
state statutory compensation restrictions.
(Prior Code, § 2-7) (Ord. 136, passed 7-12-1988; Ord. 269, passed 7-13-2016;
Ord. 270, passed 7-13-2016; Ord. 337, passed 3-11-2026)
§ 30.05 VACANCIES IN OFFICE.
   (A)   A vacancy in an appointive office shall be filled in the manner
provided for the appointment of such officer.
   (B)   A vacancy of the office of Mayor or Council member shall be deemed to
exist when a majority of the Mayor and Council members determine that the
conditions specified in Wyoming Statutes and this section exist.
   (C)   A vacancy of any appointed office shall exist if the officer is
removed, resigns, moves from the town, or otherwise becomes disqualified for
such reasons as specified by the laws of the state or by ordinances of the
town.
   (D)   A vacancy in the office of Mayor shall be filled only from the Town
Council.
   (E)   (1)   Upon the declaration of the Town Council that a vacancy exists
or will exist in the office of a Council member, the Town Council shall then
publicly solicit applications from qualified electors to fill the vacancy.
      (2)   A vacancy in the office of a Council member shall be filled by the
Town Council by appointment of a temporary successor to serve until a successor
for the remainder of the unexpired term is elected at the next general
municipal election and is qualified and takes office.
(Prior Code, § 2-7) (Ord. 136, passed 7-12-1988; Ord. 173, passed 6-4-1996)
**** MUNICIPAL MEETINGS ****
§ 30.20 OPEN TO PUBLIC; ATTENDANCE.
   (A)   All meetings of the Town Council and all other boards, commissions,
and agencies of the town shall be open to the public at all times, except as
provided in §
30.22. No action of such a body shall be taken except during a public meeting.
   (B)   A member of the public shall not be required, as a condition of
attendance at any meeting, to register his or her name, to supply information,
to complete a questionnaire, or fulfill any other condition precedent to his or
her attendance, except that a person seeking recognition may be required to
give his or her name and affiliation.
(Prior Code, § 2-1) (Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 16-4-403
§ 30.21 DISRUPTION OF COUNCIL MEETINGS.
   If any Council meeting is willfully disrupted by a person or group of
persons so as to render the orderly conduct of the meeting unfeasible, and
order cannot be restored without the removal of the person or persons who are
willfully interrupting the meeting, the Town Council may order the removal of
the person or group from the meeting room and continue in session or may recess
the meeting and reconvene at another location.
(Prior Code, § 2-2) (Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 16-4-406
§ 30.22 EXECUTIVE SESSIONS.
   Executive sessions of the Town Council and all other boards, commissions,
and agencies of the town may be held:
   (A)   With the Town Attorney, Chief of Police or their respective deputies,
or other officers of the law, on matters posing a threat to the security of
public or private property or a threat to the public’s right of access;
   (B)   To consider the appointment, employment, right to practice, or
dismissal of a public officer, professional person, or employee; or to hear
complaints or charges brought against an employee, professional person, or
officer; unless the employee, professional person, or officer request a public
hearing:
      (1)   The governing body may exclude from any public or private hearing
during the examination of a witness, any or all other witnesses in the matter
being investigated; and/or
      (2)   Following the hearing or executive session, the governing body may
deliberate on its decision in executive sessions.
   (C)   On matters concerning litigation to which the town is a party or
proposed litigation to which the town may be a party;
   (D)   When the agency is a licensing agency while preparing, administering,
or grading examinations;
   (E)   To consider the selection of a site or the purchase of real estate
when the publicity regarding the consideration would cause a likelihood of an
increase in price;
   (F)   To consider the acceptance of gifts, donations, and bequests which the
donor has requested in writing be kept confidential;
   (G)   To consider or receive any information classified as confidential by
law; and/or
   (H)   To consider accepting or tendering offers concerning wages, salaries,
benefits, and terms of employment during all such negotiations.
(Prior Code, § 2-3) (Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 16-4-405
§ 30.23 REGULAR MEETING TIMES.
   Regular public meetings of the Town Council shall be held on the second and
fourth Wednesdays of each month at 6:00 p.m.
(Prior Code, § 2-4) (Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988; Ord. 330, passed 8-14-2024)
§ 30.24 SPECIAL MEETINGS.
   Special meetings of the Town Council may be called by the Mayor by giving
notice of the meeting to each member of the Town Council and to each newspaper
of general circulation, radio, or television station requesting the notice. The
notice shall specify the time and place of the special meeting and the business
to be transacted. No other business shall be considered at such meeting.
(Prior Code, § 2-5) (Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
Statutory reference:
   Special meetings of governing body, see Wyo. Stat. § 16-4-404
§ 30.25 EMERGENCY MEETINGS.
   The Town Council may hold an emergency meeting on matters of serious and
immediate concern to take temporary action without notice. Reasonable effort
shall be made to offer a public notice. All action taken at an emergency
meeting is of a temporary nature and, in order to become permanent, shall be
reconsidered and acted upon at a regular or special meeting of the Town Council
within 48 hours.
(Prior Code, § 2-6) (Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
§ 30.26 PUBLIC RECORDS; MEETING MINUTES.
   (A)   (1)   A journal of the proceedings of the Town Council shall be kept,
said journal being a public record.
      (2)   It shall be the duty of the designated Clerk presiding over
meetings of the governing body to maintain an official journal of the minutes.
Within ten days following the adjournment of any regular or special meeting of
the Council, the designated Clerk shall post a copy of the meeting proceedings
in at least three public locations within the town, including the Town Office,
the United States Post Office, and the Senior Center. Such copy shall include
every bill presented to the Council, specifying the amount of the bill, and the
party by whom it is claimed. In addition, the Clerk’s shall be responsible for
the safekeeping of the journal and shall make it available for public
inspection upon request.
   (B)   (1)   All public records, recordings, and writings for the town shall
be open to the public for inspection under the control and guidance and
supervision of the keepers of the records.
      (2)   The keepers of the records shall include the Deputy Town Clerk, the
Clerk/Treasurer, Municipal Judge, and Chief of Police.
      (3)   Recorded minutes of Council meetings and court trials shall remain
in the Town Office building. Interviews and other police-related tapes,
reports, and the like shall be exempt from this division (B).
      (4)   Copies of documents shall be made upon request of any person at a
cost of $0.25 per page. Taped meetings may be listened to at the discretion of
the keepers of the records.
      (5)   The official custodian shall charge a reasonable fee, based on
their hourly rate, for services performed by the custodian or their deputy in
copying, printing, or reproducing records.
      (6)   At no time shall any original record, tape, recording, or report be
permitted to leave the Town Office building.
      (7)   Executive sessions are exempt from this division (B).
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988; Ord. 248, passed 1-9-2013)
§ 30.27 QUORUM.
   A majority of all the qualified members of the governing body constitutes a
quorum for the transaction of business, but any number may adjourn a meeting to
compel the attendance of and punish absent members.
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
§ 30.28 RECESS; ADJOURNED MEETINGS.
   (A)   The Town Council may recess any regular, special, or recessed regular
or special meeting to a place and at a time specified in an order of recess. A
copy of the order of recess shall be posted conspicuously on or near the door
of the place where the meeting or recessed meeting was held.
   (B)   Adjourned or recessed meetings of the Council may be held at such time
as the Council may determine.
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
§ 30.29 OFFICER DUTIES.
   The Mayor shall preside at all meetings of the Town Council provided that,
in the absence of the Mayor, such member of the Council appointed by the
Council as the Mayor Pro Tem shall preside.
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
§ 30.30 RULES OF PRACTICE.
   (A)   The rules of parliamentary practice comprised in “Robert’s Rules of
Order” shall govern the Council in all cases in which they are applicable and
in which they are not inconsistent with the rules in this subchapter.
   (B)   The Town Council may appoint such committees to function in an
advisory capacity as it from time to time desires or as may be required by law.
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
§ 30.31 RECONSIDERATION OF QUESTIONS.
   (A)   A motion to reconsider shall be made and seconded by a member of the
Council who voted with the majority, provided that where a motion is lost by
not having received a required vote of greater than a majority, a motion to
reconsider may be made and seconded by those voting with the minority.
   (B)   A vote or question may be reconsidered at any time during the same
meeting or at the first meeting held thereafter. No vote of the Council shall
be reconsidered at any meeting unless there is present as large a number of
members as were present when such vote was originally taken.
   (C)   A motion for reconsideration, being once made and decided in the
negative, shall not be reversed, nor shall a vote to reconsider be
reconsidered.
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
§ 30.32 PROCEDURE FOR TAKING VOTE.
   (A)   On all ordinances, resolutions, and appointment confirmations, a roll
call vote of the Council shall be taken. Said roll call shall rotate so one
Council member does not always vote first. On all other matters, the “ayes” and
“nays” shall be taken, and at the request of a Council member, a record of each
individual vote, together with the name of the Council member calling for a
division, shall be entered upon the journal which shall show how each Council
member voted. Any Council member can change his or her vote prior to the
announcement of the results of the vote.
   (B)   Each Council member who is present when a question is to be voted upon
shall vote thereon, unless excused by the Council or as provided in Wyo. Stat.
§ 15-1-127. Said Council member may, before the vote is called for, give his or
her reasons for not voting. If the Council member is directly interested in the
question, he or she need not vote.
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
§ 30.33 SUSPENSION OF RULES.
   (A)   The provisions of this subchapter shall constitute rules governing the
proceedings of the Town Council and shall be strictly adhered to unless
temporarily suspended by a two-thirds vote of the members present at any
meeting. The motion shall state for what purpose the suspension is to be made,
and the suspension shall exist only until the purpose named in the motion is
accomplished.
   (B)   Suspension of rules for an emergency ordinance shall require a three-
fourths vote of the qualified members of the Town Council.
(Ord. 139, passed 9-13-1988)
CHAPTER 31: MUNICIPAL COURT
Section
   
31.01   Appointments
   
31.02   Duties; jurisdiction; powers
   
31.03   Court procedure
   
31.04   Court costs
   
31.05   Witness fees
§ 31.01 APPOINTMENTS.
   The Mayor, by and with the consent of the Town Council, shall appoint a
Municipal Court Judge who shall serve at the pleasure of the Mayor.
(Prior Code, § 9-1) (Ord. 9, passed 9-7-2010)
Statutory reference:
   Appointment of Municipal Court Judges, see Wyo. Stat. § 5-6-103
§ 31.02 DUTIES; JURISDICTION; POWERS.
   (A)   The duties of the Municipal Court Judge or alternate Municipal Court
Judge shall be to preside at all regular or special sessions of the Municipal
Court.
   (B)   The Municipal Court Judge shall have exclusive jurisdiction of, and
shall hear and determine all cases arising under, this code and other
ordinances of the town. The forms, practices, and procedures before such
Municipal Court Judge in such cases, unless otherwise provided in this code or
other town ordinances, shall conform as closely as possible to the forms,
practices, and procedures before justices of the peace of the state insofar as
such practices and procedures may be applicable.
   (C)   (1)   The Municipal Court Judge shall have the power to impose fines
of no more than $750 for any one offense, to which may be added costs. The
Municipal Court Judge shall have the authority to enforce due obedience to this
code and all other town ordinances, rules, and judgments.
      (2)   Any person convicted by the Municipal Court Judge of any offense
under any town ordinance may be punished by a fine not to exceed $750, or
follow the state bond schedule. The Municipal Court Judge may also impose
imprisonment or jail time not to exceed six months, or impose both.
(Prior Code, § 9-2) (Ord. 9, passed 9-7-2010)
Statutory reference:
   Punishment powers of a Municipal Court Judge, see Wyo. Stat. § 5-6-210
§ 31.03 COURT PROCEDURE.
   Procedures in the Municipal Court shall be as provided by the State Rules of
Criminal Procedure.
(Prior Code, § 9-4)
§ 31.04 COURT COSTS.
   (A)   Upon each conviction in the Municipal Court, the Municipal Court Clerk
shall levy and collect, in addition to any fine imposed, court costs as
established by statutes or rules of court, and the fine and costs so collected
shall be remitted to the Clerk/Treasurer and deposited in the General Fund.
(Prior Code, § 9-5)
   (B)   All fines or forfeitures collected by the Municipal Court Clerk upon
conviction or upon forfeiture of bail of any person charged with any violation
or offense shall be paid to the Municipal Court and deposited in the Municipal
Court’s account. The Municipal Court Clerk shall issue a check or transfer
funds from the Municipal Court’s account to the Clerk/Treasurer monthly to be
deposited in the General Fund.
(Ord. 228, passed 8-3-2010)
Statutory reference:
   Disposition of fines, see Wyo. Stat. § 5-6-303
§ 31.05 WITNESS FEES.
   Witnesses appearing in the Municipal Court on behalf of the town shall be
entitled to such fees as are allowed to witnesses appearing in justice of the
peace courts of the state.
(Prior Code, § 9-6)
Statutory reference:
   Witness fees, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 1-14-102 and 1-14-103
CHAPTER 32: FIRE DEPARTMENT
Section
   
32.01   Establishment
   
32.02   Funds; revenue
   
32.03   Fire Chief/officer nominations
   
32.04   Member duties
   
32.05   Auxiliary/volunteer firefighters
   
32.06   Requirements for membership
   
32.07   Fire Chief duties
   
32.08   Authority
   
32.09   Reports and records; inventory
   
32.10   Equipment
   
32.11   Use of premises/apparatuses
   
32.12   False fire alarms
   
32.13   Obstructing fire escapes, exits, and the like
   32.14   Storing flammable/combustible materials
   
32.15   Fire hazards; dangerous conditions
§ 32.01 ESTABLISHMENT.
   (A)   There is created and established a Volunteer Fire Department that
shall prevent and extinguish fires, educate the public in all matters
pertaining to fires and fire prevention, care for all fire fighting apparatuses
and other municipal property in its charge, and perform such additional duties
as may be assigned to it by resolution or ordinance of the town.
   (B)   The Volunteer Fire Department shall be composed of nonpaid volunteers
and shall be governed by this chapter and the bylaws of the Fire Department as
developed by its members. Said bylaws shall be subject to acceptance and
approval of the Town Council.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.02 FUNDS; REVENUE.
   (A)   Monies for operating expenses and equipment acquisition may be
appropriated from the General Fund of the town annually through the town’s
budget process.
   (B)   The Volunteer Fire Department is authorized to raise funds and solicit
donations through social activities for the purpose of defraying expenses of
the Department, and only for Department purposes. Such funds shall be in the
care and custody of such officer or officers as specified by the bylaws of the
Department; and said officer or officers shall submit a financial statement
quarterly to the Mayor and Council.
   (C)   No member of the Department or any other person shall contract any
indebtedness on behalf of the town unless authorized to do so by the Mayor or
Town Council.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.03 FIRE CHIEF/OFFICER NOMINATIONS.
   (A)   There shall be selected a nominee for the Fire Chief and nominees for
other such officers of the Department as set forth in the bylaws of the
Department by the members of the Department. Said selection of nominees rests
solely with the members of the Department. Nominees’ names shall be forwarded
to the Mayor for appointment by the Mayor and confirmation by the Town Council.
Upon the recommendation for removal by a two-thirds majority vote of the total
number of members of the Department, or as otherwise specified by law, the
Mayor may remove the Fire Chief or other officers from office.
   (B)   Selection of nominees for appointment shall occur at least annually
and, in any case, said list of nominees shall be forwarded to the Mayor prior
to June 1 in the year of nomination.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.04 MEMBER DUTIES.
   Duties of the officers and members shall be set forth in the bylaws of the
Department and shall be in addition to any duties specified in this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.05 AUXILIARY/VOLUNTEER FIREFIGHTERS.
   In addition to the Fire Chief and officers of the Fire Department, there
shall be auxiliary firefighters who may be referred to as volunteer
firefighters. There shall be such number of positions as are from time to time
authorized and approved by the Town Council.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.06 REQUIREMENTS FOR MEMBERSHIP.
   (A)   Requirements for membership, selection of, and acceptance of members
of the Volunteer Fire Department shall be set forth in the bylaws of the
Department. However, no person under the age of 18 years shall be eligible for
membership.
   (B)   Each volunteer firefighter shall take a minimum number of hours of
regular training on such subjects and in such manner as may be prescribed by
the Fire Chief or the bylaws of the Department.
   (C)   The Chief of the Fire Department, or such person as designated by the
bylaws of the Department, shall maintain in the office of the Clerk/Treasurer
an accurate list of the members of the Fire Department and shall report
promptly any additions or withdrawals from membership.
   (D)   All persons accepted as members of the Volunteer Fire Department shall
take an oath of office to obey, support, and uphold the bylaws of the
Department, the ordinances of the town, and laws of the state as pertain to the
position and duties of volunteer firefighters.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.07 FIRE CHIEF DUTIES.
   (A)   The Chief of the Fire Department shall be responsible for the
discipline, good order, and proper conduct of the entire Department and for the
enforcement of all ordinances and regulations pertaining to it. The Chief shall
be responsible for the proper care of the fire houses, fire apparatuses, and
all other property of the Department or in its charge.
   (B)   The Fire Chief shall have command and control over all officers and
members of the Department and all houses, fire apparatuses, and all other
property of the Department or in its charge, and he or she shall likewise have
command and control of all persons present at fires.
   (C)   The Chief shall inspect or cause to be inspected by members of the
Department, at least twice annually, all fire hydrants in the town and
immediately report to the Mayor those needing attention.
   (D)   The Chief shall inspect or cause to be inspected by officers or
members of the Department, as often as necessary but not less than once each
year, all buildings, premises, and public thoroughfares (except the interiors
of private dwellings) for the purpose of ascertaining and causing to be
corrected any conditions liable to cause fire, or any violation of the
provisions or intent of this code, or any ordinance of the town affecting
safety or fire prevention. However, no member of the Fire Department shall
enter on to private property without consent of the occupant or owner except in
an emergency situation, if an immediate safety hazard exists, or with proper
court order.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.08 AUTHORITY.
   (A)   The Fire Chief shall have authority to order the immediate
discontinuance of any fire or safety hazard including but not limited to the
use of any stove, stovepipe, flue, furnace, electric wiring, or other device
which poses an imminent threat of fire.
   (B)   For the purpose of protecting persons or property, the Fire Chief or
other officer of the Department in charge of the extinguishment of any fire
shall have authority to order evacuation or abandonment of any building in
immediate danger from an imminent spreading of such fire.
   (C)   For the purpose of investigating the extent of any fire or the extent
of damage, danger, or need for evacuation, abandonment, or other action to be
taken for the protection of persons or property, the Fire Chief or other
officer in charge shall have the authority to enter any building, private
residence, or otherwise.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.09 REPORTS AND RECORDS; INVENTORY.
   (A)   The Chief shall make or cause to be made a thorough investigation and
report of each fire, giving the following information if available: cause,
location, time, date, owner, tenant, occupancy, type of building, building
loss, contents loss, how extinguished, apparatuses used, firefighters present,
adequacy of water supply and pressure, and any other information as required.
Said report shall, in addition to any other required or necessary filings, be
given to the Mayor and Town Council as its next meeting following such
incident.
   (B)   There shall be an annual inventory made by the Department of all
property or the Department or in its charge, noting the condition and
approximate value of each article. The inventory shall be submitted to the
Mayor annually by March 31.
   (C)   All recommendations for the purchase of new or additional apparatus
and equipment shall be submitted by March 31 of each year.
   (D)   Any other recommendations affecting the Fire Department shall be
submitted to the Mayor at least one week prior to any regular meeting of the
Town Council.
   (E)   There shall be kept and submitted regularly to the Town Council a
record of the activities of the Department.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.10 EQUIPMENT.
   (A)   All firefighters responding to an alarm of fire or other emergency
shall wear and use proper protective gear for the type of fire or emergency. In
no case shall any firefighter enter any burning structure without proper
protective gear.
   (B)   The insignia of the Fire Department shall be determined by a vote of
its members along with the use and wearing requirement thereof.
(Prior Code, § 2-8)
§ 32.11 USE OF PREMISES/APPARATUSES.
   (A)   No person other than members of the Department and officials the town
shall enter or be allowed to enter the fire stations unless accompanied by a
member of the Department or authorized by a member of the Department to do so.
   (B)   No person except a member of the Department shall use any of the fire
apparatuses at fires or otherwise unless acting under orders of the Fire Chief
or officer in charge, nor shall any person interfere in any way with the
Department or any member of the Department in the performance of duty.
   (C)   Fire fighting apparatuses and other town property in the charge of the
Department shall not be loaned or rented except as may be authorized in a
mutual aid agreement or contract, and then only by authorization of the Mayor.
(Prior Code, § 2-8) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 32.12 FALSE FIRE ALARMS.
   No person shall knowingly give a false alarm of fire. Alarms for test
purposes shall be given or caused to be given only by the Fire Chief.
(Prior Code, § 2-8) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 32.13 OBSTRUCTING FIRE ESCAPES, EXITS, AND THE LIKE.
   It shall be unlawful to obstruct any fire escape, stair, passageway, door,
or window so as to interfere with the egress of occupants from a building in
the case of fire or with the operations of the Fire Department.
(Prior Code, § 2-8) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 32.15 FIRE HAZARDS; DANGEROUS CONDITIONS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful to allow to exist any fire hazard or dangerous
condition as determined by the Fire Chief pursuant to applicable laws or
regulations.
   (B)   Upon determination that a fire hazard or dangerous condition exists,
the Fire Chief shall issue an order to abate and remedy such. It shall be the
responsibility of the owner of occupant to make such corrections as given in
the order within such time as given in the order.
   (C)   If the owner or occupant should fail or refuse to abate or remedy the
fire hazard or dangerous condition within the specified time, said owner or
occupant shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor. Every day the fire hazard or
dangerous condition is continued in existence beyond the date of compliance
given in the order shall constitute a separate and punishable offense.
   (D)   (1)   In such cases where the owner or occupant fails or refuses to
comply with the order to abate or remedy a fire hazard or dangerous condition,
then the Town Council may authorize said abatement or remedy to be completed.
      (2)   In such cases, in addition to any fines, the owner or occupant of
the property shall be charged an amount equal to 200% of such costs incurred to
the town to correct, abate, or remedy said fire hazard or dangerous condition.
(Prior Code, § 2-8) Penalty, see §
10.99
CHAPTER 33: ELECTIONS
Section
   
33.01   Adoption; town autonomy
   
33.02   Schedule; Mayor term
   
33.03   Poll times; locations
   
33.04   Designated Clerk to publish proclamation
   
33.05   Appointment of election employees
   
33.06   Town Canvassing Board
   
33.07   Ballots
   
33.08   Vacancies in nomination
   
33.09   Nominee application; filing fee
   
33.10   Canvassing results; tie votes
   
33.11   Certificate of election
   
33.12   Commencement of term
   
33.13   Constitutional oath
   
33.14   Expenses; cost
Editor’s note:
   All municipal elections with the town shall be conducted in accordance with
the general election laws of the state, insofar as the same may be applicable
to municipal elections, and for the purpose, the general election laws of the
state are herein adopted by reference.
§ 33.01 ADOPTION; TOWN AUTONOMY.
   (A)   This chapter is adopted pursuant to authority vested in cities and
towns of the state by:
      (1)   Article 13 § 1 of the State Constitution;
      (2)   Chapter 185 of the Sessions Laws of the State, 1975; and
      (3)   The Wyoming Statutes.
   (B)   The Town Council does elect to conduct elections pursuant to the terms
of this chapter and not in the same manner as statewide elections.
   (C)   A town ballot proposition to be voted on at an election shall be
printed on the town ballot by the designated Clerk.
   (D)   Wyo. Stat. §§ 22.320, 22.322, and 22.324 through 22.326 shall not
apply to the town.
   (E)   Except as to those sections of the Wyoming Statutes which have been
amended or modified by this chapter, all matters with respect to elections for
town office or for ballot propositions of the town shall be held in accordance
with the provisions of the State Election Code of 1973 and any amendments,
modifications, or revisions thereof.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.02 SCHEDULE; MAYOR TERM.
   (A)   A municipal election shall be held in the town on the Tuesday next
following the first Monday in the month of May every two years beginning in
2026.
   (B)   (1)   Thereafter, two Council members shall be elected in May of each
even-numbered year.
      (2)   A Mayor shall be elected in May of 2008 and serve for a term of
four years with the successive election in May of 2012 and each four-year
period thereafter.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -; Ord. 214, passed 4-1-2008; Ord.
320, passed 6-26-2024)
§ 33.03 POLL TIMES; LOCATIONS.
   (A)   Polls shall be opened at 9:00 a.m. and closed at 7:00 p.m. local time.
A Judge of Election shall proclaim aloud at 9:00 a.m. at the polls that the
polls are opened.
   (B)   The designated Clerk shall designate the polling place for municipal
elections within the town and shall also be responsible for determining if a
person seeking nomination is a qualified elector of the town.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.04 DESIGNATED CLERK TO PUBLISH PROCLAMATION.
   Between 95 and 45 days before each town election, the designated Clerk shall
publish, once in a legal newspaper of general circulation in the town and post
conspicuously in not less than three public places around town, a proclamation
setting forth the date of the forthcoming election, the titles and terms of the
officers to be filled at the election, the number of persons required by law to
fill the offices, the requirements for filing statements of campaign receipts
and expenditures, and, in addition before the election, the legislative
description of each proposed ballot proposition submitted to the voters of the
town, if any.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -; Ord. 320, passed 2-28-2024)
§ 33.05 APPOINTMENT OF ELECTION EMPLOYEES.
   (A)   The Mayor shall, by and with the advice of the Town Council, appoint
the Judges and Clerk of Election; there shall be appointed two capable and
discreet persons, possessing the qualifications of electors of the town to act
as Judges of Election, and one possessing the qualifications to act as a Clerk
of such municipal election.
   (B)   Such Judges and Clerk of Election shall take the oath of office
prescribed and governed by general election laws of the municipal election and
shall receive such salary as may be prescribed by the laws of the state for
Judges and Clerk in general elections conducted by count of state.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -; Ord. 200, passed 3-6-2004)
§ 33.06 TOWN CANVASSING BOARD.
   Wyo. Stat. § 22.1-223 is amended to read as follows:
      Section 22.1-223, Town canvassing board; compositions; compensation.
      The town canvassing board is the designated clerk, and two electors of
different political parties resident in the town appointed by the town council.
The two electors shall received the same compensation as election judges for
statewide elections.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.07 BALLOTS.
   (A)   Wyo. Stat § 22.1-319 is amended to read as follows:
      Section 22.1-319, Certifications of ballot propositions.
      A town ballot proposition to be voted on at an election shall be printed
on the town ballot by the designated clerk.
   (B)   Wyo. Stat. § 22.1-328 is amended to read as follows:
      Section 22.1-328, Preparation of ballots—form; cost.
      The designated clerk shall prepare ballots which shall be in
substantially the same form as the statewide general election non-partisan
ballot. The name of every candidate legally qualified to appear on the ballot
and all town ballot propositions to be voted on at the election shall be
printed thereon.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.08 VACANCIES IN NOMINATION.
   Wyo. Stat. § 22.1-327 is amended to read as follows:
      Section 22.1-327, Vacancies in nomination.
      A vacancy in nomination for a town office to be filled in a town election
occurs if at the time of the town election there are no nomination applications
for the office of mayor, or there are less than two nomination applications for
councilman. A vacancy in nomination shall be filled by the town council.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.09 NOMINEE APPLICATION; FILING FEE.
   Not more than 95 days and not less than 35 days preceding the town election,
each candidate for a town office shall pay the prescribed filing fee and file
with the designated Clerk an application in the form found below:
STATE OF WYOMING
TOWN OF FORT LARAMIE
I,                                  , the undersigned, being              years
of age, a qualified elector of the Town of Fort Laramie and the State of
Wyoming, do hereby petition and request that my name be printed upon the
Official Ballot at the next town election as a candidate for the office of
                                                 .

Signature of candidate
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -; Ord. 320, passed 2-28-2024)
§ 33.10 CANVASSING RESULTS; TIE VOTES.
   Wyo. Stat. § 22.1-331 is amended to read as follows:
      Section 22-1-331, Canvassing results; certification; votes.
      The returns of the town election shall be canvassed by the town
canvassing board which shall certify the results in writing to the Clerk/
Treasurer. A tie vote shall be broken by lots cast by the town canvassing
board.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.11 CERTIFICATE OF ELECTION.
   Wyo. Stat. § 22.1-332 is amended as follows:
      Section 22.1-332, Certificate of Election.
      As soon as the official result of the town election is received from the
town canvassing board, the designated clerk shall issue certificates of
election to the persons receiving the highest number of votes for each office
to be filled at the election.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.12 COMMENCEMENT OF TERM.
   Wyo. Stat. § 22.1-333 is amended to read as follows:
      Section 22.1-333, Commencement of term of office.
      The term of office of a person elected at the town election commences on
June 1st following the election.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.13 CONSTITUTIONAL OATH.
   Wyo. Stat. § 22.1-334 is amended to read as follows:
      Section 22.1-334, Constitutional oath required.
      Before entering his duties, a person elected to a town office shall sign
and file with the office of the clerk the same constitutional oath of office as
county officers.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
§ 33.14 EXPENSES; COST.
   The town shall bear the expense of any election held pursuant to this
chapter.
(Prior Code, § 5-1) (Charter Ord. passed - -)
CHAPTER 34: TOWN POLICIES
Section
   
34.01   Confiscated or abandoned property
   
34.02   Annual appropriations
§ 34.01 CONFISCATED OR ABANDONED PROPERTY.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      ABANDONED PROPERTY. Any item of personal property coming into the
possession of the town, its officers, or employees, which property remains
unclaimed by the owner for a period of 90 days.
      CONFISCATED PROPERTY. Any item of personal property taken by any police
officer of the town from any person when, under the circumstances, the
possession by such person was a violation of the laws of the state.
(Prior Code, § 6-1)
   (B)   Any abandoned property in the possession of the town shall be sold by
the Clerk/Treasurer in conformity with the laws of the state and the proceeds
therefrom placed in the General Fund.
      (1)   Any confiscated property shall be exhibited to the Law Enforcement
Officer who shall examine such property, record a description of the
confiscated items in the docket, and note the date that such property was
exhibited. Upon final disposition of the case in the event of a conviction, the
Law Enforcement Officer shall, as part of the judgment, order the confiscated
property turned over to the Clerk/Treasurer for sale or disposal.
      (2)   At least once during a calendar year, the Clerk/Treasurer shall
sell all items confiscated during the preceding year except alcoholic and malt
beverages, weapons, and controlled substances. The property shall be sold at
public auction for cash to the highest bidder after notice published by the
Clerk/Treasurer in the official newspaper of the town one time at least ten
days prior to the date of the sale. The notice shall describe the items to be
sold and the time and place of the sale. All proceeds from the sale shall be
placed in the General Fund.
      (3)   Any alcoholic and malt beverages, weapons, or controlled substances
confiscated shall, immediately after the court order becomes final, be
destroyed under the supervision of the Clerk/Treasurer and a law enforcement
representative.
(Prior Code, § 6-2)
§ 34.02 ANNUAL APPROPRIATIONS.
   (A)   The portion of Wyo. Stat. § 15-2-201(a) that reads “No further
appropriation may be made at any other time within the fiscal year, unless the
proposition to make each appropriation has been first sanctioned by a majority
of the qualified electors of such town, either by a petition signed by them or
at a general or special election duly called for that purpose”, shall not apply
to the town.
   (B)   The fiscal year for the town and its agencies shall begin July 1 and
end July 30. All claims and encumbrances allowed by the governing body at its
meeting beginning in July for the expense incurred in the preceding fiscal year
shall be charged against the budget for said preceding year.
   (C)   The governing body by resolution may amend the annual appropriation as
the need therefor may arise, but the total amount appropriated shall not exceed
the probable amount of revenue that will be collected during the fiscal year.
   (D)   The provisions of the ordinance codified herein shall apply to the
fiscal year first commencing July 1, 1977, and all subsequent years thereafter.
(Prior Code, § 5-2) (Charter Ord. 105, passed 12-7-1977)

Editor’s note:
Case holding that a service charge imposed for paying for the construction of a
sewerage disposal plant is not a tax, see Laverents v. City of Cheyenne, 67
Who. 187, P. 2d 877 (1950)
Case holding that service charges may be imposed for three purposes, namely to
pay for the operation and maintenance of the system, to provide an adequate
depreciation fund, and to pay the principal and interest on bonds, see Antlers
Hotel, Inc. v. Town of City of Newcastle, 80 Wyo. 294.341 P. 2d 951 (1959)
Case holding that ordinances setting flat water rates for some consumers and
metered rates for others on which sewerage rates were based is not per see
illegal, arbitrary, discriminatory, or unjust, see Antlers Hotel, Inc. v. Town
of City of Newcastle and Ira Baldwin v. Town of City of Newcastle, 80 Wyo. 294,
641 P. 2d 951 (1959)

   Joint Powers Act, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 16-1-102 to 16-1-109

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 50.01 OWNER RESPONSIBILITY.
   (A)   The owner of the property furnished electricity, water, sewer, and
sanitation (trash removal) by the town shall be liable for the utility charges
incurred for such property; therefore, liability shall be imposed on the owner
of the property for any charges incurred by his or her tenants or agents.
   (B)   A violation of this section is a misdemeanor.
(Ord. 261, passed 6-10-2015) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 50.02 WATER AND SEWER COMMISSIONER.
   (A)   (1)   There is created the office of Water and Sewer Commissioner. The
Water and Sewer Commissioner shall be appointed by the Mayor by and with the
consent of the Town Council.
      (2)   He or she shall have such experience as shall be necessary for the
faithful performance of the duties of his or her office.
(Prior Code, § 12-22)
   (B)   The Water and Sewer Commissioner shall be the Superintendent of
Sewers, and as such Superintendent, he or she shall have charge and management
of the sewerage system of the town, and all property belonging or pertaining
thereto, and shall see that the system is kept properly cleaned and in good
working order and repair. He or she shall perform all such duties in connection
with such system as shall be required of him or her by the Town Council.
(Prior Code, § 12-23)
§ 50.03 TOWN AUTHORITY.
   It is declared to be the public policy of the town to preempt the exclusive
power and authority to provide, furnish, and distribute unto the citizens and
occupants of the town and residing in or maintaining a place of business within
the corporate limits of the town: all electrical power, all natural gas
service, water service, television service, radio service, telephone and
telegraph service, transportation, and other utility services used by the
citizens of the town, required by the citizens or subsequently furnished to the
citizens now or in the future, except such services that shall be provided by
persons who have been granted a franchise from the town.
(Prior Code, § 12-1)
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to grant utility franchises, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103
§ 50.04 FURNISHING SERVICES.
   No person shall sell, provide, distribute, or otherwise furnish electrical
power, natural gas service, propane service, telephone service, telegraph
service, water for domestic use and service, radio service, television service,
or other public utility service within the incorporated limits of the town,
unless such person or public utility shall have first been granted a franchise
and authority therefor to so do by the governing body.
(Prior Code, § 12-2) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 50.05 NON-FRANCHISED PURCHASES.
   No person shall purchase, obtain, receive, use, or consume any electrical
power, any natural gas, telephone service, telegraph service, water for
domestic use and service, radio service, television service, or other public
utility service within the corporate limits of the town from any source other
than the town or a person or company who shall have first been granted a
franchise and authority to so furnish such service by the town.
(Prior Code, § 12-3) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 50.06 ACQUIRING PROPERTY OF NON-FRANCHISED SERVICES.
   The town shall have the right to acquire by eminent domain all poles, lines,
fixtures, pipelines, mains, and appurtenances of whatever nature of any
electric company, telegraph company, telephone company, gas company, water
company, television distributing company, radio distributing company,
transportation company, and of any other public utility company, association,
or corporation so providing or undertaking to provide and furnish any of town
when such public utility, company, association, corporation, or otherwise is
not then and there the holder of a franchise granted by the town to so provide
such services.
(Prior Code, § 12-4)
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to exercise power of eminent domain, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-
1-103
**** BILLING; FEES ****
§ 50.20 DEPOSITS.
   (A)   (1)   The governing body shall from time to time establish the dollar
amount which shall be assessed and collected by the office of the Clerk, prior
to the time any utility services are provided by the town, as a deposit for
each commercial service and for each residential service; such deposit shall be
retained by the town until such time as all charges for utilities are paid in
full.
      (2)   Each utility account shall require a deposit in the amount of $100
per utility service, excluding refuse services, to be paid in full prior to the
commencement of service by the town.
   (B)   Any established utility account without a deposit shall be deemed
grandfathered. Any change in the name of the account holder shall be required
to submit a new utility application and provide the appropriate payment of any
applicable deposit.
   (C)   All deposits shall be returned upon termination of all utility service
and payment of all charges due the town for anything whatsoever. In the event
any amounts are due and owing to the town for anything whatsoever, the charges
shall first be deducted from any deposit so held by the town and the balance
refunded to the consumer.
(Prior Code, § 12-5) (Article 1, passed 5-10-2023)
§ 50.21 BILLING PROCEDURES.
   (A)   Charges for utility services provided by the town shall be as set,
from time to time, by the Town Council. A copy of each ordinance establishing
such charges shall be maintained on file in the office of the Clerk of the town
where it shall be available for inspection by the public during the normal
office hours.
   (B)   Billing and collection procedures shall be as established by the Town
Council.
(Prior Code, § 12-6)
§ 50.22 DUE DATES; DELINQUENT ACCOUNTS; RECONNECTION FEES.
   (A)   (1)   All charges for water, sanitation, sewer, and electrical
services and all related charges shall be due and payable at the office of the
Clerk on the fifteenth day after the date of mailing utility bills. Any account
not paid in full by the fifteenth day after mailing shall be deemed delinquent,
assessed a $25 late fee, mailed a letter detailing timelines to pay the account
as well as offered the opportunity to enter into a payment plan with the town,
and given an additional ten days to pay the amount owing in full.
      (2)   If payment arrangements are not made or the entire amount is not
paid in full by the end of said ten-day period, the utility services shall be
shut off. Utility services will not be shut off the day before holidays or on
Fridays. In the event of termination of any services, the user shall pay a
reconnection fee of $50 plus a nonrefundable penalty fee of $300 to the town
before any services are resumed. The previous utility charges shall be paid in
full prior to services being resumed.
   (B)   For any utility user that is currently delinquent on utility bills or
that cannot pay the utility bill described in division (A) above, in an effort
to avoid utility services being terminated, can enter into a payment plan with
the town on the following terms.
      (1)   The customer must pay for all future utility bills, beginning the
next immediate month of entering the payment plan, in full for the duration of
the payment plan.
      (2)   Upon entering into a payment plan, the customer shall pay $200 or
20%, whichever is less, of the arrears in addition to the current utility bill
until paid in full.
      (3)   A payment plan is null and void if divisions (B)(1) and (B)(2)
above are not fulfilled by the due date, and all utilities will immediately be
shut off.
(Prior Code, § 12-7) (Ord. 244A, passed 8-9-2023)
§ 50.23 INSPECTION FEES.
   The governing body shall from time to time establish the fee to be charged
for electrical inspection and plumbing inspection for each domestic residence,
and for every other type of construction shall be paid to the town at the time
a building permit is secured for any construction, remodeling, or other
activity for which any electrical and plumbing inspection is required.
(Prior Code, § 12-8)
§ 50.24 CONNECTION FEES.
   (A)   The following fees shall be paid for each connection of the electrical
service system of the town when the connection is for single-phase service or
for connection to the sewer system of the town:
      (1)   A connection fee of $300 plus the cost of time and materials, for
any electrical or sewer system connection for any other type of usage not
otherwise specified herein; and/or
      (2)   An amount set by the governing body from time to time plus an
amount equal to the difference in total cost of construction between single-
phase electrical service systems of the town for three-phase electrical
service.
(Prior Code, § 12-9)
   (B)   All fees shall be paid prior to the time any connection is made to the
service systems, and in the event of new construction where the facilities are
not present, all fees shall be paid prior to the commencement of any
construction of the facilities which will be required to provide the services.
(Prior Code, § 12-10)
   (C)   All connection fees for electrical services to be provided for all
lots, tracts, or other parcels in subdivisions shall be paid prior to the
commencement of any construction of the facilities which will be required to
provide the service.
      (1)   In lieu of payment of all electrical service connection fees in
advance of any construction, a subdivision may provide collateral acceptable to
the Town Council, guaranteeing payment of the electrical service connection
fees for all lots, tracts, or other parcels in the entire subdivision.
      (2)   The Town Council shall have the option of demanding payment in full
in advance of construction or of accepting a guarantee in lieu thereof.
(Prior Code, § 12-11)
   (D)   Connections to the sewer service system shall include connections to
the primary sewer service system of the town and connections to all feeder
lines connected thereto, regardless of ownership of the lines.
(Prior Code, § 12-12)
   (E)   (2)   An amount set by the governing body from time to time, plus and
amount equal to the difference in total cost of construction between single-
phase electrical service for each connection to the electrical service system
of the town for three-phase electrical service.
(Ord. 186, passed 8-1-2000) Penalty, see §
10.99
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to provide and regulate electrical power, see Wyo. Stat.
§§ 15-1-103, 15-7-101, and 15-7-201 through 15-7-209

§ 51.01 RESPONSIBILITY FOR CONNECTIONS.
   (A)   All connections to the main water system of the town and all shall be
made by a person duly employed by the town or under a town employee’s
supervision.
(Prior Code, § 12-30)
   (B)   (1)   Water mains shall be maintained and repaired by the town at its
cost, unless damage was caused by others.
      (2)   Service lines shall be maintained and replaced as needed by the
town at its cost from the main to the water meter, with the entity or person
served by such service line responsible for maintaining and replacing, at his
or her cost, such service line from the water meter to wherever the service
line continues thereafter.
      (3)   When leaking occurs on the customer’s service line, the service
line from the water meter to the point of use shall be replaced within 14
business days using materials that meet or exceed town standards, WDEQ, and the
International Plumbing Code. If a replacement of the service line is not made
within 14 business days, water service to the property will be terminated until
replacement of the service line has been completed.
(Ord. 331, passed 4-9-2025) Penalty, see §
10.99

Residential, commercial, and out-of-town $2 per 1,000 gallons over the base
