(Ord. 336, passed 9-8-2025)
§ 51.03 WATER USERS OUTSIDE TOWN LIMITS.
   (A)   (1)   Each water user residing outside the corporate limits of the
town using water furnished through the mains of the town or using water
supplied by the town shall pay an amount which the governing body shall
establish from time to time.
      (2)   From and after the effective date of passage of the ordinance
codified herein, as required by law, no town water or sewer services will be
extended to consumers located outside the town boundaries and not already
receiving such services.
(Prior Code, § 12-24)
   (B)   All users of water from the town residing outside the corporate limits
shall be deemed to have subscribed and agreed to all provisions of this chapter
applicable to water usage.
(Prior Code, § 12-25)
(Ord. 332, passed 5-14-2025) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 51.04 PERMIT.
   No person shall tap or connect to any part of the water system of the town
or use any water therefrom without first obtaining a permit to do so from the
town.
(Prior Code, § 12-26) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 51.05 APPLICATION.
   Any person desiring to make a connection to the water system or to use water
therefrom shall make written application to the town for a permit. The
application for a permit to make any connection to the water system shall state
the name of the person to whom the permit is to be issued, the size of the tap,
the location thereof, the premises upon which the water is to be used, and the
purpose for which the water is to be used. All permits issued shall state the
name of the person to whom the permit is issued, the date of the permit, the
size of the tap, the premises upon which the water is to be used, and the
purpose for which the water is to be used.
(Prior Code, § 12-27) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 51.06 WATER LINE INSTALLATION; FEES.
   (A)   An installation fee of $300 for each water line connected to the main
water distribution line of the town, plus the cost of time and materials.
   (B)   For any installation larger than three-fourth’s of an inch, there
shall be assessed and collected a fee equal to the greater amount in division
(A) above or the actual cost of the materials and labor for any installation
larger than one inch connected to the main water distribution line of the town.
(Prior Code, § 12-28) (Ord. 197, passed 10-7-2003)
§ 51.07 BILLING; PAYMENTS.
   A $300 connection fee is to be paid up front. Time and materials will be due
30 days after completion.
Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 51.08 IRRIGATION WATER.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      IRRIGATION WATER. The water acquired by the town from the Pathfinder
Irrigation District and the Lingle Water Users Association.
      LATERALS. All ditches, pipes, or other conveyances of irrigation water
including headgates (head gates), diversion boxes, and other appurtenances.
      SEASON. The period of time during the calendar year that irrigation water
is available for delivery.
      SUBSCRIBER. Any person requesting delivery of irrigation water and paying
the established rate.
   (B)   The town may deliver water to lots designated by paid subscribers
where delivery is practical and available. The maximum delivery will be one and
one-half cubic foot per second of water per lot for a period of one hour. Town
employees shall be responsible for setting all headgates, diversion boxes, and
water controls for delivery. The Mayor and town employees responsible for
delivery shall establish a schedule of delivery at the beginning of each
season. The delivery schedule will be based on the number and geographic
location of subscribers.
   (C)   All costs associated with the time and labor incurred by residents
seeking to obtain irrigation water access to their property shall be paid in
full as a condition prior to irrigation water access.
   (D)   Any person desiring delivery of irrigation water shall subscribe for
such delivery with the office of the Clerk prior to each season and submit such
fees, rates, or tariffs per lot as may be set by the Town Council. Set rates
are $25 per lot. These fees or tariffs shall be paid before irrigation water is
delivered.
   (E)   Each subscriber shall be responsible for the irrigation water when it
is delivered. Each subscriber shall take all precautions and care that the
irrigation water does not flow from his or her property onto town property,
streets or alleys, or onto adjoining private property. Any subscriber not
desiring water at the scheduled delivery time shall notify the Town Office 24
hours in advance.
   (F)   In the case of an emergency or for the general health, welfare, and
safety of the town, the town reserves the right to alter or suspend the
delivery schedule or to suspend all delivery of irrigation water.
   (G)   No subscriber will be delivered irrigation water unless the subscriber
agrees to all the rules and regulations pertaining to irrigation water as set
forth in this section or by resolution.
   (H)   No person shall in any way damage, alter, change, or interfere with
any laterals, headgates, diversion boxes, or other appurtenances of the town’s
irrigation water system, nor shall any person make a connection to any lateral
without permission from the town.
(Prior Code, § 12-31) (Ord. passed 6-14-2017) Penalty, see §
10.99

§ 52.01 RATES FOR USAGE.
   (A)   The following rates are established for use of sewers within the town:
 
Installed sewer rate $40 per month
Unoccupied lot rate  $15 per month
Vacant lot rate      $0 per month
 
   (B)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      UNOCCUPIED LOT. Any parcel of real property that is not currently
occupied by tenant, owner, or person for a period of 30 or more days. For the
purpose of this section, “real property” shall be deemed to be an UNOCCUPIED
LOT for such time the tenant, owner, or real person remains absent from the
real property, and shall continue until the tenant, owner, or person returns to
the real property, at which point the real property shall no longer be deemed
unoccupied and the base rate shall apply. An UNOCCUPIED LOT does have a sewer
pit and further has sewer services available.
      VACANT LOT. Any real parcel of real property that is not improved with a
complete, permitted, and permanent structure. For the purpose of this section,
“real property” shall be deemed a VACANT LOT unless a structure with a fixed
foundation is constructed upon the real property or water, sewer, or electric
are provided to any structure, building, or portion of the real property. A
VACANT LOT does not have a sewer pit and does not have sewers available.
(Ord. 335, passed 9-8-2025)
§ 52.02 SEWER SERVICE CHARGES; DISCHARGES.
    (A)   The purpose of this section shall be to generate sufficient revenue
to pay all costs for the operation and maintenance of the complete wastewater
system. The costs shall be distributed to all users of the system in proportion
to each user’s contribution to the total loading of the treatment works.
Factors such as strength of biochemical oxygen demand and total suspended
solids (BOD and TSS), volume, and delivery flow rate characteristics shall be
considered and included as the basis for the user’s contribution to ensure a
proportional distribution of operation and maintenance costs to each user or
user class.
   (B)   The governing body of the town, or its engineer, shall determine the
total annual costs of operation and maintenance of the wastewater system which
are necessary to maintain the capacity and performance, during the service life
of the treatment works, for which such works were designed and constructed. The
total annual cost of operation and maintenance shall include, but need not be
limited to: labor, repairs, equipment replacement, maintenance, necessary
modifications, power, sampling, laboratory tests, and a reasonable contingency
fund.
   (C)   (1)   The governing body of the town, or its engineer, shall determine
for each user or user class the average daily volume of wastewater discharged
to the wastewater system, which shall then be divided by the average daily
volume of all wastewater discharged to the wastewater system to determine such
user’s volume contribution percentage. The amount used as the total average
daily volume of wastewater shall exclude infiltration and inflow. The governing
body, or its engineer, shall determine for each user or user class the average
daily poundage of five-day 68°F biochemical oxygen demand (BOD) discharged to
the wastewater system which shall then be divided by the average daily poundage
of all five-day BOD discharged to the wastewater system to determine such
user’s BOD contribution percentage.
      (2)   The governing body of the town, or its engineer, shall determine
for each user or user class the average daily total suspended solids (TSS)
poundage discharged to the wastewater system, which shall then be divided by
the average daily poundage of all TSS discharged to the wastewater system, to
determine such user’s TSS contribution percentage. The volume contribution
percentage, BOD contribution percentage, and TSS contribution percentage for
each user or user class shall be multiplied by the annual operation and
maintenance costs for wastewater treatment of the total volume flow, total
five-day 68°F BOD, and TSS, respectively.
   (D)   (1)   The governing body of the town, or its engineer, will assess a
surcharge rate for all users discharging wastes with BOD and TSS strengths
greater than the average residential user. All users exceeding the design
average per capita volume and exceeding the waste strengths (BOD and TSS) as
shown on page three of Appendix A attached to the ordinance codified herein,
shall prepare and file with the town a report that shall include pertinent data
relating to the wastewater characteristics, including the methods of sampling
and measurement to obtain this data, and this data shall be used to calculate
the user charge for that user.
      (2)   The town shall have the right to gain access to the waste stream
and take its own samples. Should the town do so, and should the results be
substantially different as determined by the town from the data submitted by
the user, the user charge for that user shall be revised for the next billing
cycle period. Such users will be assessed a surcharge sufficient to cover the
cost of treating the above-normal strength wastes.
   (E)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      COMMERCIAL USER. All retail stores, restaurants, office buildings,
laundries, home businesses, and other private business and service
establishments.
      INDUSTRIAL USER. Any nongovernmental, nonresidential user of publicly
owned treatment works which is identified in the standard Industrial
Classification Manual, 1972, Office of Management and Budget, as amended and
supplemented, under the following divisions:
         (a)   Division A: Agriculture, Forestry, and Fishing;
         (b)   Division B: Mining;
         (c)   Division D: Manufacturing;
         (d)   Division E: Transportation, Communications, Electric, Gas and
Sanitary; and
         (e)   Division I: Services.
      INSTITUTIONAL USER. Any social, charitable, religious, governmental, and
educational activities such as schools, churches, nursing homes, and similar
institutional users.
      RESIDENTIAL USER. All dwelling units such as detached, semidetached, row
houses, mobile homes, and multi-family dwellings.
   (F)   Each nonresidential user’s wastewater treatment cost contributions, as
determined in divisions (C) and (D) above, shall be added together to determine
such user’s annual wastewater service charge.
      (1)   Residential users may be considered to be one class of user, and an
equitable service charge may be determined for each user based on an estimate
of the total wastewater contribution of this class of user. The governing body
may classify industrial, institutional, commercial, and other nonresidential
establishments as a residential user, provided that the wastes from these
establishments are equivalent to the wastes from the average residential user
with respect to volume, total suspended solids, and BOD.
      (2)   Each user’s wastewater treatment cost contribution will be assessed
in accordance with the adopted rate schedule attached to the ordinance codified
herein as Appendix A.
   (G)   A reserve fund called the Wastewater Facilities Replacement Fund is
hereby established within the wastewater utility fund for the purpose of
providing sufficient funds to be expended for obtaining and installing
equipment, accessories, and appurtenances during the useful life (20 years) of
the wastewater treatment facilities necessary to maintain the capacity and
performance for which such facilities are designed and constructed.
   (H)   (1)   The town shall review the total annual cost of operation and
maintenance as well as each user’s wastewater contribution percentage not less
often than every two years, and will revise the system as necessary to assure
equity of the service charge system established herein, and to assure that
sufficient funds are obtained to adequately operate and maintain the wastewater
treatment works.
      (2)   The town shall apply excess revenues collected from a class of
users to the costs of operation and maintenance attributable to that class for
the next year and adjust the rate accordingly.
      (3)   Any user who feels the user charge is unjust and inequitable may
make written application requesting a review of the user charge. Said written
request shall, where necessary, show the actual or estimated average flow and/
or strength of his or her wastewater in comparison with the values upon which
the charge is based, including how the measurements or estimates were made.
      (4)   Review of the request shall be made by the town; and if
substantiated, the user charges for that user shall be recomputed based on the
revised flow and/or strength data, and the new charges shall be applicable to
the next billing period.
(Ord. 158, passed - -1993)
**** PUBLIC AND PRIVATE SEWERS ****
§ 52.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BIOCHEMICAL OXYGEN DEMAND (BOD). The quantity of oxygen utilized in the
biochemical oxidation of organic matter under standard laboratory procedure in
five days at 20°C, expressed in milligrams per liter.
   BUILDING DRAIN. The part of the lowest horizontal piping of a drainage
system which receives the discharge from soil, waste, and other drainage pipes
inside the walls of the building and conveys it to the building sewer,
beginning five feet (one and one-half meters) outside an inner face of the
building wall.
   BUILDING SEWER. The extension from the building drain to the public sewer or
other place of disposal. Also called HOUSE CONNECTION.
   COMBINED SEWER. A sewer intended to receive both wastewater and storm or
surface water.
   EASEMENT. An acquired legal right for the specific use of landowner by
others.
   FLOATABLE OIL. Oil, fat, or grease in a physical state such that it will
separate by gravity from wastewater by treatment in an approval pretreatment
facility. Wastewater shall be considered free of floatable fat if it is
properly pretreated and the wastewater does not interfere with the collection
system.
   GARBAGE. The animal and vegetable waste resulting from the handling,
preparation, cooking, and service of foods.
   INDUSTRIAL WASTES. The wastewater from industrial processes, trade, or
business as district from domestic or sanitary wastes.
   NATURAL OUTLET. Any outlet, including storm sewers and combined sewer
overflows, into a watercourse, pond, ditch, lake, or other body of surface or
groundwater.
   pH. The logarithm of the reciprocal of the hydrogen ion concentration. The
concentration is the weight of hydrogen ions, in grams, per liter of solution.
Neutral water, for example, has a pH value of seven and a hydrogen ion
concentration of 10-7.
   PROPERLY SHREDDED GARBAGE. The wastes from the preparation, cooking, and
dispensing of food that have been shredded to such a degree that all particles
will be carried freely under the flow conditions normally prevailing in public
sewers, with no particle greater than one-half inch (one and twenty-seven-
hundredths centimeters) in any dimension.
   PUBLIC SEWER. A common sewer controlled by a governmental agency or public
utility.
   SANITARY SEWER. A sewer that carries liquid and water-carried wastes from
residences, commercial buildings, industrial plants, and institutions together
with minor quantities of ground, storm, and surface waters that are not
admitted intentionally.
   SEWAGE. The spent water of a community. The preferred term is WASTEWATER, as
defined herein.
   SEWER. A pipe or conduit that carries wastewater or drainage water.
   SLUG. Any discharge of water or wastewater which, in concentration of any
given constituent or in quantity of flow, exceeds for any period of duration
longer than 15 minutes, more than five times the average 24-hour concentration,
or flows during normal operation and shall adversely affect the collection
system and/or performance of the wastewater treatment works.
   STORM DRAIN. A drain or sewer for conveying water, groundwater, subsurface
water, or unpolluted water from any source. Sometimes termed STORM SEWER.
   SUPERINTENDENT. The Superintendent of wastewater facilities, and/or
wastewater treatment works, and/or of water pollution control of the town; or
his or her authorized deputy, agent, or representative.
   SUSPENDED SOLIDS. Total suspended matter that either floats on the surface
of, or is in suspension in, water, wastewater, or other liquids, and that is
removable in laboratory filtering as prescribed in “Standard Methods for the
Examination of Water and Wastewater” and referred to as nonfilterable residue.
   UNPOLLUTED WATER. Water of quality equal to or better than the effluent
criteria in effect or water that would not cause violation of receiving water
quality standards and would not be benefitted by discharge to the sanitary
sewers and wastewater treatment facilities provided.
   WASTEWATER. The spent water of a community. From the standpoint of source,
it may be a combination of the liquid and water-carried wastes from residences,
commercial buildings, industrial plants, and institutions; together with any
groundwater, surface water, and stormwater that may be present.
   WASTEWATER TREATMENT WORKS. An arrangement of devices and structures for
treating wastewater, industrial wastes, and sludge. Sometimes used as
synonymous with WATER TREATMENT PLANT, or WASTEWATER TREATMENT PLANT, or WATER
POLLUTION CONTROL PLANT.
   WATERCOURSE. A natural or artificial channel for the passage of water either
continuously or intermittently.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
§ 52.16 REQUIRED USE OF TOWN SYSTEM.
   (A)   (1)   All occupied lots or parts of lots or land in the town shall be
connected by a sewer service line to the sewer main. No privy vault, septic
system, or cesspool shall be installed, maintained, or kept on any property in
the town.
      (2)   Extension of sewer mains to serve property within the town shall be
financed and paid for on such terms and conditions as the Town Council shall
provide.
(Prior Code, § 12-40)
   (B)   (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to place, deposit, or permit
to be deposited in any unsanitary manner on public or private any human or
animal excrement, garbage, or other objectionable waste.
      (2)   It shall be unlawful to discharge to any natural outlet within the
town, or in any area under the jurisdiction of the town, any sewage or other
polluted waters, except where suitable treatment has been provided in
accordance with subsequent provisions of this subchapter.
      (3)   Except as hereinafter provided, it shall be unlawful to construct
or maintain any privy, privy vault, septic tank, cesspool, or other facility
intended or used for the disposal of wastewater.
      (4)   The owner of any house, building, or property used for human
occupancy, employment, recreation, or other purposes, situated within the town
and abutting on any street, alley, or right-of-way in which there is now
located or may in the future by located a public sanitary or combined sewer of
the town, is required, at the owners expense, to install suitable toilet
facilities herein, and to connect such facilities directly with the proper
public sewer in accordance with the provisions of the requirements of the town.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.17 PERMIT; TYPES.
   (A)   No unauthorized persons shall uncover, make any connections with or
opening into, use, alter, or disturb any public sewer or appurtenance thereof
without first obtaining a written permit from the Superintendent.
(Prior Code, § 12-32)
   (B)   (1)   No unauthorized persons shall uncover, make any connections with
or opening into, use, alter, or disturb any public sewer or appurtenance
thereof without first obtaining a written permit from the Superintendent.
      (2)   There shall be two classes of building sewer permits:
         (a)   For residential and commercial service; and
         (b)   For service to establishments producing industrial wastes.
      (3)   In either case, the owners or agent shall make application on a
special form furnished by the town. The permit application shall be
supplemented by any plans, specifications, or other information considered
pertinent in the judgment of the Superintendent. A fee shall be paid as
established by the town.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.18 PERMIT APPLICATION.
   (A)   (1)   Any person desiring to open or uncover any public sewer, or lay
or cause to be laid in any public street, alley, highway, or upon any private
property any sewer line or drainage pipes, shall make application to the town
for a permit to do so.
      (2)   Such application shall be in writing, signed by the applicant or
his or her agent, shall state the name of the individual who it is desired
shall do such work, and shall be accompanied by a plan showing the course of
the drain and the size and location of any branches, fixtures, and traps to be
connected therewith.
   (B)   (1)   If the drain is to be connected with a sewer belonging to any
person other than the town, the applicant must file a written consent of the
owner of such sewer to be connected with. Upon approval of the application, the
town shall issue a permit.
      (2)   All drainage pipes shall be laid upon line and grade required by
the Water and Sewer Commissioner. All work done under any permit shall be by a
licensed plumber and shall be under the supervision of the Water and Sewer
Commissioner.
(Prior Code, § 12-33) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.19 RESPONSIBILITY OF COST/EXPENSES.
   All costs and expense incidental to the installation and connection of the
building sewer shall be borne by the owner. The owner shall indemnify the town
from any loss or damage that may directly or indirectly be occasioned by the
installation of the building sewer.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
§ 52.20 CONNECTION PER BUILDING; OLD SEWERS.
   (A)   A separate and independent building sewer shall be provided for every
building; except where one building stands at the rear of another on an
interior lot and no private sewer is available or can be constructed to the
rear building through an adjoining alley, court, yard, or driveway; the
building sewer from the front building may be extended to the rear building,
and the whole not assume any obligation or responsibility for damage caused by
or resulting from any such single connection.
   (B)   Old building sewers may be used in connection with new buildings only
when they are found, on examination and test by the Superintendent, to meet all
requirements of this subchapter.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.21 MATERIALS; CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   (1)   The size, slope, alignment, materials, or construction of all
sanitary sewers, including building sewers, and the methods to be used in
excavating, placing of the pipe, jointing, testing, and backfilling the trench,
shall all conform to the requirements of regulations of the town.
      (2)   In the absence of suitable code provisions or in amplification
thereof, the materials and procedures set forth in appropriate specifications
of the A.S.T.M. and W.P.C.F. Manual of Practice No. 9 shall apply.
   (B)   Whenever possible, the building sewer shall be brought to the building
at an elevation below the basement floor. In all buildings in which any
building drain is too low to permit gravity flow to the public sewer, sanitary
sewage carried by such building drain shall be lifted by an approved means and
discharged to the building sewer.
   (C)   No person shall make connection of roof downspouts, foundation drains,
areaway drains, or other sources of surface runoff or groundwater to a building
sewer or building drain, which in turn is connected directly or indirectly to a
public sanitary sewer unless such connection is approved by the Superintendent.
   (D)   The connection of the building sewer into the public sewer shall
conform to the requirements of the building and plumbing code or other
applicable rules and regulations of the town or the procedures set forth in
appropriate specifications of the A.S.T.M. and the W.P.C.F. Manual of Practice
No. 9. All such connections shall be made gastight and watertight and verified
by proper testing. Any deviation from the prescribed procedures and materials
must be approved by the Superintendent before installation.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.22 EXCAVATIONS.
   All excavations for building sewer installation shall be adequately guarded
with barricades and lights so as to protect the public from hazard. Streets,
sidewalks, parkways, and other public property disturbed in the course of the
work shall be restored in a manner of the Superintendent.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.23 UNPOLLUTED WATERS; STORMWATER.
   (A)   No person shall discharge or cause to be discharged any unpolluted
waters such as stormwater, surface water, groundwater, roof runoff, subsurface
drainage, or cooling water to any sewer; except stormwater runoff from limited
areas, which stormwater may be polluted at times, may be discharged to the
sanitary sewer by permission of the Superintendent.
   (B)   (1)   Stormwater, other than that exempted under division (A) above
and all other unpolluted drainage, shall be discharged to such sewer as are
specifically designated as combined sewers or storm sewers, or to natural
outlet approved by the Superintendent.
      (2)   Unpolluted industrial cooling water or process waters may be
discharged, on approval of the Superintendent, to a storm sewer, combined
sewer, or natural outlet.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.24 PROHIBITED WASTES/SUBSTANCES.
   (A)   No person shall discharge or cause to be discharged any of the
following described water wastes to any public sewers:
      (1)   Any gasoline, benzene, naphtha, fuel oil, or other flammable or
explosive liquid, solid, or gas;
      (2)   Any waters containing toxic or poisonous solids, liquids, or other
wastes, to contaminate the sludge of any sewage treatment process, constitute a
hazard in, or have an adverse effect on the waters receiving any discharge from
the works;
      (3)   Any waters or wastes having a pH lower than 5.5 or having any other
corrosive property capable of causing damage or hazard to structures,
equipment, or personnel of the wastewater works; and/or
      (4)   Solid or viscous substances in quantities or of such size capable
of causing obstruction to the flow in sewers, or other interference with the
proper operation of the wastewater facilities such as, but not limited to
ashes, cinders, sand, mud, straw, shavings, metal, glass, rags, feathers, tar,
paunch manure, hair and fleshing, entrails; and paper dishes, cups, milk
containers, and the like, either whole or ground by garbage grinders.
   (B)   Each user which discharges any toxic pollutants, which cause an
increase in the cost of managing the effluent or the sludge of the treatment
works, shall pay for such increased costs.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.25 LIMITED WASTES/SUBSTANCES.
   (A)   (1)   The following described substances, materials, waters, or wastes
shall be limited in discharges to municipal systems to concentrations or
quantities which will not harm either the sewers, the sludge, or any municipal
system, and the wastewater treatment process or equipment will not have an
adverse effect on the receiving stream, or will not otherwise endanger lives,
limb, or public property or constitute a nuisance.
      (2)   The Superintendent may set limitations lower than the limitations
established in the regulations below if, in his or her opinion, such more
severe limitations are necessary to meet the above objectives.
      (3)   In forming his or her opinion as to the acceptability, the
Superintendent will give consideration to such factors as the quantity of
subject waste in relation to flows and velocities in the sewers, materials of
construction of the sewers, the wastewater treatment process employed, capacity
of the limitations or restrictions on materials, or characteristics of waste or
wastewaters discharged to the sanitary sewer, which shall not be violated
without approval of the Superintendent, are as follows:
         (a)   Wastewater having a temperature higher than 150°F (65°C);
         (b)   Wastewater containing more than 25 milligrams per liter of
petroleum oil, non-biodegradable cutting oils, or produce of mineral-oil
origin;
         (c)   Wastewater from industrial plants containing floatable oils,
fats, or grease;
         (d)   Any garbage that has not been properly shredded;
         (e)   Garbage grinders may be connected to sanitary sewers from homes,
hotels, institutions, restaurants, catering establishments, or similar places
where garbage originates from the preparation of food in kitchens for the
purpose of consumption on the premises or when served by caterers;
         (f)   Any waters or wastes containing iron, chromium, copper, zinc,
and similar objectionable or toxic substances to such degree that any such
material received in the composite wastewater at the wastewater treatment works
exceeds the limits established by the Superintendent for such materials;
         (g)   Any waters or wastes containing odor-producing substances
exceeding limits, which may be established by the Superintendent;
         (h)   Any radioactive wastes or isotopes of such half-life or
concentration as may exceed limits established by the Superintendent in
compliance with applicable state or federal regulations;
         (i)   Quantities of flow or concentrations or both, which constitute a
slug as defined herein;
         (j)   Waters or wastes containing substances which are not amenable to
treatment or reduction by the wastewater treatment processes employed, or are
amenable to treatment only to such degree that the wastewater treatment plant
effluent cannot meet the requirements of other agencies having jurisdiction
over discharge to the receiving waters; and
         (k)   Any water or wastes which, by interaction with other water or
wastes in the public sewer system: release obnoxious gases, form suspended
solids which interfere with the collection system, or create a condition
deleterious to structures and treatment processes.
   (B)   (1)   If any waters or wastes are discharged or are proposed to be
discharged to the public sewers, which waters contain the substances or possess
the characteristics enumerated in division (A) above, and which in the judgment
of the Superintendent may have a deleterious effect upon the wastewater
facilities, processes, equipment, or receiving waters, or which otherwise
create a hazard to life or constitute a public nuisance, the Superintendent
may:
         (a)   Reject the wastes;
         (b)   Require pretreatment to an acceptable condition for discharge to
the public sewers;
         (c)   Require control over the quantities and rates of discharge; and/
or
         (d)   Require payment to cover the added cost of handling and treating
the wastes not covered by existing taxes or sewer charges.
      (2)   If the Superintendent permits the pretreatment or equalization of
waste flows, the design and installation of the plants and equipment shall be
subject to the review and approval of the Superintendent.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
§ 52.26 TREATMENT/DISPOSAL OF HARMFUL WASTES.
   (A)   (1)   Wastes detrimental to the public sewer system or detrimental to
the functioning of the sewage treatment plant shall be treated and disposed of
as found necessary and directed by the governing body or other authority having
jurisdiction.
      (2)   Sewage or other waste from a plumbing system which may be
deleterious to surface or subsurface waters shall not be discharged into the
ground or into any waterway unless it has first been rendered innocuous through
subjection to some acceptable form of treatment.
(Prior Code, § 12-35)
   (B)   (1)   Chemical or industrial liquid wastes which are likely to damage
or increase maintenance costs of the sanitary sewer system, detrimentally
affect sewage treatment, or contaminate surface or subsurface water, shall be
pretreated to render them innocuous prior to discharge into a drainage system.
      (2)   Piping conveying industrial, chemical, or process wastes from their
point of origin to sewer- connected pretreatment facilities shall be of such
material and design as to adequately perform its intended function.
(Prior Code, § 12-36)
Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.27 INTERCEPTORS; WASH RACKS.
   (A)   Interceptors (including grease, oil, sand interceptors, and the like)
shall be provided when, in the judgment of the governing body or other
authority, they are necessary for the proper handling of liquid wastes
containing grease, flammable wastes, sand, solids, acids, or alkaline
substances or other ingredients harmful to the building drainage system, the
public, or a private sewage disposal system.
(Prior Code, § 12-37)
   (B)   Every private or public wash rack and/or floor or slab used for
cleaning machinery or machine parts shall be adequately protected against storm
or surface water and shall drain or discharge into a sand and grease
interceptor of an approved design.
(Prior Code, § 12-38)
   (C)   When waste pretreatment is required, an approved-type grease
interceptor shall be installed in the waste line leading from sinks, drains,
and other fixtures or equipment in the following establishments: restaurants,
cafés, lunch counters, cafeterias, bars and clubs, hotel or school kitchens, or
other establishments where grease may be introduced into the drainage or
sewerage system in quantities that can effect line stoppage or hinder sewage
treatment.
(Prior Code, § 12-39)
   (D)   (1)   Grease, oil, and sand interceptors shall be provided when, in
the operation of the Superintendent, they are necessary for the proper handling
of liquid wastes containing floatable grease in any flammable wastes, sand, or
other harmful ingredients, except quarters or dwelling units. All interceptors
shall be of a type and capacity approved by the Superintendent and shall be
located as to be readily and easily accessible for cleaning and inspection.
      (2)   In the maintaining of these interceptors, the owner shall be
responsible for the proper removal and disposal by appropriate means of the
captivated materials and shall maintain records of the dates and means of
disposal, which are subject to review by the Superintendent. Any removal and
hauling of the collected materials not performed by the owner must be performed
by currently licensed waste disposal firms.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.28 PRETREATMENT; FLOW-EQUALIZATION FACILITIES.
   Where pretreatment or flow-equalization facilities are provided or required
for any waters or wastes, they shall be maintained continuously in satisfactory
and effective operation by the owner at his or her expense.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.29 INDUSTRIAL WASTE SAMPLING; MEASUREMENT.
   (A)   When repairing by the Superintendent, the owner of any property
serviced by a building sewer carrying industrial wastes shall install a
suitable structure, together with such necessary meters and other appurtenances
in the building sewer to facilitate observation, sampling, and measurement of
the wastes.
   (B)   Such structures, when repaired, shall be accessibly and safely located
and shall be constructed in accordance with plans approved by the
Superintendent. The structure shall be installed by the owner at his or her
expense and shall be maintained by him or her so as to be safe and accessible
at all times.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.30 USER-PROVIDED INFORMATION.
   The Superintendent may require a user of sewer services to provide
information needed to determine compliance with this subchapter. These
requirements may include:
   (A)   Wastewater’s discharge peak rate and volume over a specified time
period;
   (B)   Chemical analyses of wastewaters;
   (C)   Information on raw materials, processes, and products affecting
wastewater volume and quality;
   (D)   Quality and disposition of specific liquid, sludge, oil, solvent, or
other materials important to sewer and pretreatment facility location;
   (E)   A plot plan of sewers of the user’s property showing sewer and
pretreatment facility locations;
   (F)   Details of wastewater pretreatment facilities; and
   (G)   Details of systems to prevent and control the losses of materials
through spills to the municipal sewer.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
§ 52.31 METHOD OF MEASUREMENT, TESTS, AND ANALYSIS.
   All measurements, tests, and analysis of the characteristics of waters and
wastes to which reference is made in this subchapter shall be determined in
accordance with the latest edition of “Standard Methods of the Examination of
Water and Wastewater” published by the American Public Health Association.
Sampling methods, locations, times, durations, and frequencies are to be
determined on an individual basis subject to approval by the Superintendent.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
§ 52.32 AGREEMENTS AND ARRANGEMENTS.
   No statement contained in §§
52.23 through
52.31 shall be construed as preventing any special agreement or arrangement
between the town and any industrial concern whereby an industrial waste of
unusual strength or character may be accepted by the town for treatment.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
§ 52.33 DAMAGES TO FACILITIES.
   No person shall maliciously, willfully, or negligently break, damage,
destroy, uncover, deface, or tamper with any structure, appurtenance, or
equipment, which is a part of the wastewater facilities.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 52.34 AUTHORIZED PERSONS; EMPLOYEE SAFETY.
   (A)   The Superintendent and other duly authorized employees of the town
shall be permitted to enter all properties for the purposes of inspection,
observation, measurement, sampling, and testing pertinent to discharge to the
community system in accordance with the provisions of this subchapter.
   (B)   The Superintendent or other duly authorized employees are authorized
to obtain information concerning industrial processes, which have a direct
bearing on the kind and source of discharge to the wastewater collection
system. The industry may withhold information considered confidential. The
industry must establish that the revelation to the public of the information in
question might result in an advantage to competitors.
   (C)   While performing the necessary work on private properties, the
Superintendent or duly authorized employees of the town shall observe all
safety rules applicable to the premises established by the company, and the
company shall be held harmless for injury or death to the town employees, and
the town shall indemnify the company against loss or damage to its property by
the town employees and against liability claims and demands for personal injury
or property damage asserted against the company growing out of the gauging and
sampling operation, except as such may be caused by negligence or failure of
the company to maintain safe conditions as required in §
52.29.
   (D)   The Superintendent and other duly authorized employees of the town
shall be permitted to enter all private properties through which the town holds
an easement for the purpose of, but not limited to, inspection, observation,
measurement, sampling, repair, and maintenance of any portion of the waste
water facilities lying within said easement. All entry and subsequent work, if
any, on said easement shall be done in full accordance with the terms of the
easement pertaining to the private property involved.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
§ 52.35 VIOLATIONS.
   Any person violating any of the provisions of this subchapter shall be
guilty of a misdemeanor.
(Ord. 160, passed 10-5-1993)
CHAPTER 53: ELECTRICITY
Section
   
53.01   Rates
   
53.02   Requirements for electrical work
   
53.03   Temporary service
   
53.04   Inspections
   
53.05   Pole/wire placement
   
53.06   Meter installations
   
53.07   Liability
   
53.08   Unlawful connections
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to provide and regulate electrical power, see Wyo. Stat.
§§ 15-1-103, 15-7-101, and 15-7-201 through 15-7-209
§ 53.01 RATES.
   (A)   The following rates are established for use of electricity within the
town:
 
Installed electrical rate $0.15 per kW in addition to a $15 base rate
Unoccupied lot rate       $15 per month
Vacant lot rate           $0 per month
 
   (B)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      UNOCCUPIED LOT. Any parcel of real property that is not currently
occupied by a tenant, owner, or person for a period of 30 or more days. For the
purposes of this section, “real property” shall be deemed to be an UNOCCUPIED
LOT for such time the tenant, owner, or person remains absent from the real
property and shall continue until the tenant, owner, or person returns to the
real property, at which point the real property shall not longer be deemed
unoccupied, and the base rates shall apply. An UNOCCUPIED LOT has an electrical
meter on the premises.
      VACANT LOT. Any parcel of real property that is not improved with a
complete, permitted, and permanent structure. For the purposes of this section,
“real property” shall be deemed a VACANT LOT unless a structure with a fixed
foundation is constructed upon the real property or water, sewer, or electrical
services are provided to any structure, building, or portion of the real
property. A VACANT LOT does not have an electrical meter on the premises.
(Ord. 305, passed 5-10-2023)
§ 53.02 REQUIREMENTS FOR ELECTRICAL WORK.
   (A)   No person shall make any connection to the town electrical system,
construct or install any electrical wiring in any building or structure, or
conduct any electrical work in any addition to any building or structure
without first obtaining a permit to do so. No connection shall be made to the
town electrical system without prior inspection and approval by the electrical
inspector.
(Prior Code, § 12-14)
   (B)   No person, except an licensed electrician, shall construct, repair,
install, or interfere with, in any manner, any electrical wiring or apparatus
in the town except on or within property owned or controlled by the person so
doing the construction, repair, or installation.
(Prior Code, § 12-15)
Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 53.03 TEMPORARY SERVICE.
   Any person desiring temporary service during construction of any building
shall pay a fee equal to the cost of equipment and labor upon application for
such service. The town shall provide all material for any temporary connection.
(Prior Code, § 12-16)
§ 53.04 INSPECTIONS.
   (A)   An agent of the town shall have reasonable access to all building and
other structures at reasonable times for the purpose of determining the amount
of electricity consumed and for the inspection of all electrical wiring,
connections, and installations.
   (B)   No person shall deny any authorized agent of the town access for any
reasonable inspection.
(Prior Code, § 12-17) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 53.05 POLE/WIRE PLACEMENT.
   The town shall have the power and supervision over the placing, erecting,
and wiring of all electrical poles and wires in the town. No pole or wire shall
be placed so as to create any unsafe or hazardous condition.
(Prior Code, § 12-18) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 53.06 METER INSTALLATIONS.
   (A)   The town shall install a meter socket furnished by the town at a
location prescribed by the town. The town shall make all connections from the
transformer to the meter socket. All multiple-family dwellings shall have all
meters located in a central area, and each meter shall be equipped with a
separate disconnect.
   (B)   (1)   All electrical service installed for buildings, except buildings
used for residential purposes or private garages, located within the fire
limits of the town shall be in conduit, equipped at outer end with approved
service head or conduit fitting, and extending, without outlet or junction box,
into service cabinets of approved steel construction.
      (2)   All cabinets shall be located inside the walls of the building in
the nearest accessible place to the point where the wires enter the building.
Cabinets shall be arranged so that the handle of any service switch is not more
than seven feet above a permanent floor. All conduit and cabinets shall be
securely and firmly bonded and grounded.
(Prior Code, § 12-19) (Ord. 184, passed 4-4-2000)
§ 53.07 LIABILITY.
   The town shall control and be responsible for all service to the outside
disconnect meter base and the consumer shall be responsible for all service
thereafter.
(Prior Code, § 12-20)
§ 53.08 UNLAWFUL CONNECTIONS.
   (A)   No person shall connect or change any wire, other instrument, device,
or contrivance which transmits or supplies or is intended to transmit or supply
electricity to any house, mobile home, store or other building, without the
express knowledge and consent of the electrical inspector of the town or his or
her authorized agent.
   (B)   No person shall connect or change any wire, other instrument, device,
or contrivance with any wire transmitting or supplying or intended to transmit
or supply electricity to any house, mobile home, store or other building in
such a manner as to transmit or supply any electricity to any house, mobile
home, store, or other building around or without passing through the meter
provided for measuring or registering the amount or quantity of electricity
passing through it without the knowledge or consent of the electrical inspector
or his or her authorized agent.
   (C)   No person shall willfully injure, alter, or interfere with or obstruct
the action or operation of any meter measuring electricity passing through it.
(Prior Code, § 12-21) Penalty, see §
10.99

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 54.01 OWNER RESPONSIBILITY.
   The owner of the property furnished trash removal by the town shall be
liable for the trash charges incurred for such property; therefore, liability
shall be imposed on the owner of the property for any charges incurred by his
or her tenants or agents.
(Prior Code, § 13-9)
§ 54.02 COLLECTION RATES.
   (A)   The following rates are established for use of trash removal with the
town:
 
Large commercial $66.15 per month
Residential      $27.75 per month
Small commercial $36.95 per month
 
   (B)   The town and contracted trash removal entity reserves the right to
reach consensus on the charges to commercial entities based solely on the
volume of trash the commercial entity is producing.
(Prior Code, § 13-6; Prior Code, § 13-10) (Ord. 321, passed 3-13-2024)
**** COLLECTION SERVICES; REGULATIONS ****
§ 54.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COMMERCIAL ESTABLISHMENT. All buildings or premises not included in the
definition of a residence.
   GARBAGE. Wastes resulting from the handling, preparation, cooking, or
consumption of foods; wastes from the handling, storage, and sale of produce;
and/or any other matter whatsoever that may decompose and become foul,
offensive, unsanitary, or dangerous to health.
   HAZARDOUS WASTE. Any waste or combination of wastes which are nondegradable
or persistent in nature, can be biologically magnified, or can be lethal and
which pose a substantial present or potential hazard.
   REFUSE. Combustible and noncombustible discarded materials including but not
limited to: paper, wood, glass, metal and cloth products, yard trimmings, tree
branches, furniture, bedding, building materials, leaves, ashes, and solid
wastes resulting from residential activities and industrial and manufacturing
processes.
   RESIDENCE. Any living quarters not connected to a business or commercial
establishment with kitchen facilities, including mobile homes, apartments, and
churches.
   RURAL RESIDENCES. Those residences not in the corporate limits of the town
but are within a five-mile radius of the town or within the geographic limits
of the town’s Rural Fire District.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987)
§ 54.16 COLLECTION BY TOWN.
   (A)   (1)   All garbage and refuse accumulated in the town shall be
collected, conveyed, and disposed of by the town. No person shall be collect or
convey over any of the streets or alleys of the town or dispose of any garbage
and refuse accumulated in the town.
      (2)   This subchapter shall not prohibit the actual producers of garbage
and refuse, or the owners of premises upon which garbage and refuse has
accumulated, from collecting conveying and disposing of such garbage and
refuse, providing such producers or owners comply with the provisions of this
subchapter and with any other governing laws or ordinances.
   (B)   The town will provide garbage and refuse collection in the alleys
within the corporate limits of the town and in streets where alley collection
is not practical, from approved or town-supplied containers. Frequency of
service will be determined by the Mayor and Town Council.
   (C)   The town will provide for the collection of leaves, tree branches, and
yard trimmings on a frequency determined by the seasonal needs and volume
generally along the same routes as garbage and refuse collection.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987) Penalty, see §
54.99
§ 54.17 PROHIBITED PRACTICES.
   (A)   No person shall dump, place, or bury in any lot, land, or street
within the town any garbage, refuse, hazard waste, other waste matter, or any
other deleterious or offensive substance.
   (B)   No person shall place in containers for regular collection any highly
inflammable or explosive materials, nor any burning material, hot coals, or
ashes.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987) Penalty, see §
54.99
§ 54.18 GARBAGE CONTAINER DESIGN/PLACEMENT.
   (A)   All garbage and refuse cans shall be constructed of galvanized iron,
metal, or other material approved by the town and shall not exceed 35 gallons
in capacity or 75 pounds in content.
   (B)   All garbage and refuse cans shall be kept at some proper and
accessible place within easy reach of the collector. Such cans shall be secured
in such a manner as to provide reasonable security against being overturned by
animals, wind, or other causes.
   (C)   Property owners or tenants of property fronting alleys will deposit
refuse in the alley in a manner that will prevent blowing and spreading.
Property owners or tenants not having access to alleys will use suitable
containers or place refuse in such a manner that will prevent blowing and
spreading, will not create a nuisance, or will not create an eyesore.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987) Penalty, see §
54.99
§ 54.19 PREPARING MATERIALS FOR COLLECTION.
   (A)   Tree limbs, branches, and wood used in construction shall be cut to
lengths not exceeding six feet. Rock, stone, brick, concrete, dirt, and other
building materials shall be placed in such a manner as to make collection
practical and within easy reach of the collector.
   (B)   Appliances, furniture, and other bulky wastes or refuse will be
collected upon special request and arrangement with the town and shall be
placed near the garbage or refuse cans in such a manner as to make collection
practical and within easy reach of the collector.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987) Penalty, see §
54.99
§ 54.20 BILLING; FEES.
   (A)   The Town Council shall establish fees, rates, or tariffs for garbage
and refuse collection by resolution and may from time to time adjust, by
resolution, such fees, rates, or tariffs consistent with the costs of providing
such services.
   (B)   (1)   All accounts shall be considered delinquent if not paid by the
tenth of the month following the month for which the service was rendered. All
delinquent accounts are subject to stoppage of service if not paid within 30
days of notice of delinquency unless otherwise directed by the Town Council.
Service shall be resumed thereafter only on payment of the accumulated fees for
the period of collection and the period of non-collection unless the Town
Council directs otherwise.
      (2)   The stoppage of services hereinbefore authorized for non-payment of
collection charges shall be in addition to the right of the town to proceed for
collection of such unpaid charges in any manner provided by law for the
collection of a municipal claim.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987)
§ 54.21 LANDFILLS.
   (A)   (1)   There is established a municipal sanitary landfill for the
municipality to be used for the disposal of all garbage, refuse, ashes, and
other waste matter or materials.
      (2)   The sanitary landfill site shall be fenced with locked gates. Only
the town personnel and landfill attendant shall have access to keys for the
sanitary landfill.
   (B)   The sanitary landfill will be open and attended for a period of eight
hours at least two days per week, one of which shall be Saturday. The specific
hours shall be established by the Town Council according to seasonal and volume
needs.
      (1)   Use of the sanitary landfill by permit holders or other eligible
users as determined by the Town Council shall be during the time the facility
is open and attended.
      (2)   The town shall have printed and issue at no extra charge landfill-
use permits to all persons who pay collection service fees. Rural residents
shall have the opportunity to purchase landfill-use permits from the town at a
fee as may be established by the Town Council.
      (3)   Any person who has had stoppage of collection due to delinquency of
account as hereinbefore stated shall forfeit the landfill-use permit.
      (4)   Eligible users may deposit garbage, refuse, or other waste material
on a time by time basis by remitting such fees for use as may be set by the
Town Council to the attendant each time such person uses the sanitary landfill.
      (5)   Any person using the sanitary landfill shall deposit or place any
garbage and refuse in a manner and location as directed by the attendant.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for any person to deposit, place, or cause to be
deposited or placed the carcass of any animal or poultry or vehicle bodies in
the sanitary landfill operated by the town.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to discharge firearms or
fireworks in the sanitary landfill facility operated by the town.
      (2)   It shall be unlawful for any person to deposit, place, or cause to
be deposited or placed any hazardous waste in the sanitary landfill.
      (3)   It shall be unlawful for any person to set a fire within the
sanitary landfill, with the exception of authorized town personnel for the
purpose of proper operation and maintenance.
      (4)   No person shall deposit, place, or cause to be deposited or placed
garbage, refuse, or waste material in unauthorized areas.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987) Penalty, see §
54.99
§ 54.22 AMENDMENTS; FUTURE REGULATIONS.
   The Town Council may make by resolution further rules and regulations as
deemed necessary for the provision of collection service and governing the use
and operation of the sanitary landfill.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987)
§ 54.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person, firm, or corporation violating any of the provisions of §§
54.15 through
54.22 shall be charged with a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof before a
Municipal Judge, shall be fined in an amount not exceeding $750, plus costs of
prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state or by rules of the Supreme
Court.
(Ord. 129, passed 9-8-1987)
CHAPTER 55: NATURAL GAS
Section
   
55.01   Gas Department; Commissioner
§ 55.01 GAS DEPARTMENT; COMMISSIONER.
   (A)   There is created the town’s Department of Natural Gas and the office
of Natural Gas Commissioner. The Department shall be under the control of the
governing body. The Commissioner shall be appointed by the Mayor with the
consent of the Town Council.
   (B)   The town shall have the power to:
      (1)   Construct, purchase, or lease pipelines for the transportation and
distribution of natural gas to the inhabitants of the town;
      (2)   Purchase or lease real estate and all other property necessary for
the transportation and distribution of natural gas to the inhabitants of the
town;
      (3)   Purchase natural gas supplies or reserves for providing natural gas
to the inhabitants of the town;
      (4)   To do all other acts necessary for the purchasing, transportation,
distributing, and administering the distribution and sale of natural gas to the
inhabitants of the town; and
      (5)   Enter into any agreement with any other governmental agency which
the governing body feels is appropriate and beneficial for carrying out any of
the powers herein granted.
(Ord. 171, passed 5-7-1996)
TITLE VII: TRAFFIC CODE
   Chapter

CHAPTER 70: GENERAL PROVISIONS
Section
   
70.01   Adoption of Uniform Act Regulating Traffic on Highways

§ 70.01 ADOPTION OF UNIFORM ACT REGULATING TRAFFIC ON HIGHWAYS.
   The “Uniform Act Regulating Traffic on Highways” in Wyo. Stat. Title 31, as
amended, is adopted in its entirety as applicable, and:
   (A)   Wyo. Stat. § 24-1-110 “Exhibition of Acceleration”;
   (B)   Wyo. Stat. § 6-3-107 “Throwing Burning Substance from Vehicle:
Penalties”; and
   (C)   Wyo. Stat. § 6-3-204 “Littering”.
(Ord. 229, passed 10-5-2010)
§ 70.02 UNNECESSARY ENGINE/BRAKE NOISE.
   (A)   It is unlawful for the operator of any motor vehicle in town limits to
race his or her engine, backfire said engine, or cause any other unnecessary
engine noise or excessive smoke to be produced as to cause a public nuisance or
disturbance.
   (B)   It is unlawful for a person to operate a motor vehicle in town limits
if the vehicle has a defect in the exhaust system which affects sound reduction
of the motor, is not equipped with a muffler or other noise dissipative device,
or is equipped with a cutout, bypass, amplifier, or similar device to limit
noise.
   (C)   No person shall operate or use any un-muffled vehicle compression
brake within the town limits. The use of compression brakes with straight pipes
is prohibited within town limits.
(Ord. 315, passed 9-8-2021) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.03 RECREATIONAL VEHICLES.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      RECREATIONAL VEHICLE. A vehicle-type unit designed for temporary living
quarters for recreational, camping, or travel use, which either has its own
mode of power or is mounted on or drawn by another vehicle. RECREATIONAL
VEHICLE shall include but not be limited to the following.
         (a)   CAMPER. A structure designed to be mounted on a truck chassis
for use as a temporary dwelling for travel, recreational, and vacation uses.
         (b)   CAMPING TRAILER. A folding structure mounted on wheels and
designed for travel, recreational, and vacation uses.
         (c)   MOTOR HOME. A portable, temporary dwelling to be used for
travel, recreational, and vacation uses, constructed as an integral part of a
self-propelled vehicle.
         (d)   TRAVEL TRAILER. A vehicular, portable structure built on a
chassis and drawn by a motorized vehicle, and which is designed to be used as a
temporary dwelling for travel, recreational, and vacation uses.
   (B)   (1)   Any person may live in or continuously occupy a recreational
vehicle, as defined in division (A) above, within the property owner’s lot as
long as the recreational vehicle is legally connected to the town’s sewer,
water, and electricity.
      (2)   Those visiting are allowed to occupy their RV or trailer for a
period of 14 days without being hooked up to the town utilities. Any days
beyond the 14 days will require notification to the town.
   (C)   Nothing herein this section shall prevent recreational vehicles being
stored on residential properties that are in compliance with all applicable
laws or within commercial storage facilities.
(Ord. 314, passed 7-14-2021) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 70.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this traffic code for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   The violation of §
70.02 is considered to be a misdemeanor offense that carries a fine of up to
$750, and the vehicle could be impounded.
(Ord. 315, passed 9-8-2021)

SCHEDULE I. SPEED LIMITS.
   Fifteen mph on Pioneer Court is the maximum speed, and no person shall drive
a vehicle or motorized vehicle in excess of 15 mph.
(Ord. 239, passed 5-1-2012) Penalty, see §
70.99
SCHEDULE II. PARALLEL PARKING.

Merriam Street and Custer Street shall be designated as parallel parking only.
Vehicles must park in a southerly direction with their wheels adjacent to the
curb.
   (B)   At no time shall any vehicle be parked in a northerly direction on
North Laramie Avenue in the designated area in division (A) above.
(Ord. 240, passed 5-1-2012) Penalty, see §
70.99

CHAPTER 90: HEALTH AND SANITATION; NUISANCES
Section

90.01   Filth, offal, and rubbish; junk vehicles
Refuse, Garbage, and Weeds
   
90.15   Definitions

90.19   Placing garbage in containers

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 90.01 FILTH, OFFAL, AND RUBBISH; JUNK VEHICLES.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation to deposit,
leave, or cause to be deposited or left, the carcass of any dead animal in any
part of the town.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation to place or
cause to be placed in or upon any street, alley, park, or public land; or in or
upon his or her own premises; or upon the premises of another person in the
town: any filth, offal, rubbish, or other accumulation which is or may become
offensive, unwholesome, or detrimental to the public health and safety.
Further, it shall be unlawful for any person to allow such matter specified
herein to remain upon any premises owned or occupied by him or her in such a
manner as to become unwholesome or offensive to any person or persons adjacent
thereto.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation to place or
cause to be placed any abandoned, wrecked, or junked automobile or any part
thereof in or upon any street, alley, public premises, or upon any premises
owned or occupied by him or her within the town except as provided in division
(D) below.
   (D)   (1)   The following conditions shall apply to the placement of any
wrecked or junked automobiles or any parts thereof upon premises owned or
occupied by any person, firm, or corporation within the town.
         (a)   Persons, firms, or corporations engaged in the business of
maintenance or repair of automobiles shall place any wrecked automobile or part
thereof in a place which is away from the public view.
         (b)   Persons, firms, or corporations engaged in the business of auto
body work, repair, or restoration of automobiles shall store such wrecked,
junked, or damaged automobiles or any part thereof in buildings or in an
enclosure surrounded by a fence of at least eight feet in height constructed of
such material as to block view.
         (c)   Any person restoring or repairing his or her own wrecked,
damaged, or antique automobile shall do so upon his or her own premises or upon
premises occupied by him or her, and such restoration or repair shall be
completed within a reasonable amount of time so as to prevent creation of a
nuisance or an eyesore to the public view.
         (d)   Any person storing his or her own wrecked automobile or any part
thereof upon premises owned or occupied by him or her shall place such
automobile or part thereof so as to be away from the public view and in such a
manner as not to become offensive, unwholesome, or detrimental to any person or
persons adjacent thereto.
         (e)   Any person, firm, or corporation engaged in the business of a
salvage yard or auto wrecking yard shall have said premises enclosed by a fence
of at least eight feet in height constructed of such material as to block view
and keep such premises in a condition which shall not be offensive,
unwholesome, or a detriment to public health and safety.
      (2)   Any person, firm, or corporation violating the provisions of this
section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.
   (E)   (1)   When the Town Council shall have knowledge, upon complaint or
otherwise, that a nuisance or such offensive matter exists in violation of
divisions (A) through (D) above, or that any privy, stable, pigpen, butcher
shop, slaughterhouse, or other building, place, or thing is annoying to the
citizens of the town, or is likely to produce sickness or is a detriment to the
public health and safety, the Council shall forthwith direct the owner, agent,
or occupant of the premises on which such nuisances may exist to remove,
remedy, or abate the same within a reasonable time to be fixed by the Town
Council.
      (2)   If any owner, agent, tenant, or occupant neglects or refuses to
conform to the directions of the Town Council, the Town Council shall cause the
same to be removed or remedied at the expense of the town and such owner,
agent, tenant, or occupant convicted for violation of the provisions of this
section shall forfeit to the town double the amount of the expenses so incurred
by the town in addition to any fines and court costs which may be imposed.
(Ord. 137, passed 7-12-1988) Penalty, see §
90.99
**** REFUSE, GARBAGE, AND WEEDS ****
§ 90.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ABANDONED VEHICLE. A vehicle upon private property is an ABANDONED VEHICLE
when:
      (1)   Any of the four tires of the main wheels of such vehicle have been
removed or are deflated, other than for repair;
      (2)   Any of the main wheels of the vehicle have been removed, other than
for repair;
      (3)   Such vehicle is totally or partially suspended above the ground by
jacks, blocks, or any other lifting devices, other than for repair;
      (4)   Current license plates or valid temporary permits are not displayed
on such vehicle, provided that this shall not apply to vehicles in the
possession of licensed dealers for the purpose of sale at the place licensed
for such sale; and/or
      (5)   Any part of the mechanism of the vehicle has been removed so as to
render the vehicle inoperable, other than for repair.
   GARBAGE. Wastes resulting from the handling, preparation, cooking, or
consumption of foods; wastes from the handling, storage, or sale of produce;
and any other matter whatsoever that may decompose and become foul, offensive,
unsanitary, or dangerous to health.
   REFUSE. Combustible and noncombustible discarded materials including but not
limited to: paper wood, glass, metal and cloth products, yard trimmings, tree
branches, furniture, bedding, building materials, leaves, ashes, and solid
wastes resulting from industrial and manufacturing processes.
(Prior Code, § 13-1)
§ 90.16 MAINTAINING PREMISES; RESPONSIBILITY.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of any owner, agent, tenant, purchaser,
contractor, or lessee of any premises within the town, including places of
business, dwelling houses, apartments, tenements, construction sites, or other
establishments, at all times, to maintain the premises in a clean and orderly
condition, permitting no deposit or accumulation of materials other than those
necessary or ordinarily attendant upon the use for which such premises are
legally intended.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of any owner, occupant, or agent having control
of any lot or premises, as well as any portion of any alley adjoining the
premises, to:
      (1)   Mow, cut, destroy, or spray all weeds; and
      (2)   Remove all trash, garbage, junk, abandoned vehicles, unused
machinery, junk machinery, abandoned and discarded appliances, and all other
refuse.
   (C)   Any accumulation of trash, garbage, junk, abandoned vehicles, unused
vehicles, abandoned machinery, unused machinery, junk machinery, abandoned and
discarded appliances, and all other refuse is declared to constitute a nuisance
and a nonconforming use of the premises.
(Prior Code, § 13-2) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.17 NOTICE OF VIOLATION.
   (A)   (1)   Notice for violation of this section shall be a written notice
requiring the mowing, cutting, or destruction of weeds. The notice will require
the owner, occupant, or agent to comply with this violation removal within ten
days. Notice shall be deemed delivered when personally served or deposited in
the Unites States mail, with postage prepaid, and addressed to the owner,
occupant, or agent of the premises.
      (2)   Failure to comply will result in citation into Municipal Court for
resolution by fine and town cost for mowing, cutting, or spraying of said
property.
(Prior Code, § 13-2)
   (B)   The owner, agent, or occupant of any premises shall be liable for
compliance with this subchapter and §§
54.01 and
54.02, and if such person shall refuse to comply with the provisions of this
section, he or she shall be given written notice requiring removal of the
offending item or items within ten days. Notice shall be deemed delivered when
personally served or deposited in the United States mail, with postage prepaid,
and addressed to the owner, occupant, or agent of the premises.
(Prior Code, § 13-3)
(Ord. passed 8-2-2011)
§ 90.18 FAILURE TO COMPLY.
   (A)   Upon failure to comply with any written notice given pursuant to §
90.17(B), the town shall have the authority to remove any offending item and
charge the cost thereof as a special bill for refuse removal. In the event such
bill is not promptly paid for, it shall become a lien against the property and
collectible as such.
   (B)   The abatement of the nuisance by the town shall not relieve the person
in charge or control of such property of any penalty imposed for violation of
this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 13-4)
§ 90.19 PLACING GARBAGE IN CONTAINERS.
   All garbage and refuse shall be placed in the containers provided.
(Prior Code, § 13-5) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.20 TREE TRIMMING.
   (A)   An owner, lessee, occupant, or agent having control of any lot or
premises shall remove or trim any tree or portion thereof which creates an
unsafe condition for persons or property.
   (B)   An owner, lessee, or occupant of any property shall trim and remove
branches of any tree which interferes with any utility lines of the town.
   (C)   Any person who shall trim trees on any property within the town shall
be responsible for the removal from the streets, alleys, and other public ways
of the town, of all trash, limbs, and other debris created by such trimming.
   (D)   No person shall plant or place any tree on property belonging to the
town without prior approval of the governing body.
   (E)   The administrative officer or his or her designee shall require
compliance with this section, and the owner, lessee, agent, or occupant of any
premises shall be held liable for such compliance. If any person shall refuse
or fail to remove or trim any tree which is unsafe or interferes with any
utility lines upon any premises under his or her care and control, he or she
shall be served with written notice requiring removal or trimming of any tree
within three days from the receipt of such notice.
   (F)   (1)   Upon failure to comply with any notice give pursuant to division
(E) above, the town may have the tree or trees trimmed or removed, charging the
landowner the cost as a special bill for removal.
      (2)   Failure to pay the bill within five days of presentment to the
landowner shall be punishable as a misdemeanor.
(Prior Code § 13-7) (Ord. 225, passed 6-11-2014) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.21 PROHIBITED/RESTRICTED ITEMS IN DUMPSTERS.
   No person shall place any hazardous waters, rocks, cement, hot ashes, sod,
dirt, scrap iron, car batteries, motor oil, tires, or building materials in any
dumpster placed by the town or by any supplier of garbage disposal service for
the town.
(Prior Code, § 13-8) (Ord. 161, passed 5-7-1996) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating any of the provisions of §
90.01 shall be charged with a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof before a
Municipal Judge, shall be fined in an amount not exceeding $750 plus costs of
prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state, or by imprisonment in the
town’s jail for not more than six months, or both.
(Ord. 137, passed 7-12-1988)
