**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 91.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ABANDON. Relinquishing, withdrawing, or failing to provide for the care,
custody, and control of an animal without provisions for adequate food, water,
shelter, or someone to check the animal’s condition, in excess of 24 hours.
   ADULT. Any animal above the age of six months.
   ALTERED. Spayed or neutered.
   ANIMAL. Any live, vertebrate creature except human beings, domestic or wild.
   ANIMAL CONTROL DISTRICT. The town limits or other contracted area.
   ANIMAL CONTROL OFFICER. Any person designated by the town as a lawful
enforcement officer who is qualified to perform such duties under the laws of
the state.
   ANIMAL CONTROL SHELTER. Any facility operated by the municipal agency or its
authorized agents for the purpose of impounding or caring for animals held
under the authority of this chapter or state law.
   AT LARGE. A dog is deemed AT LARGE if it is off the premises of the owner
and the dog is not under restraint, nor is it under the control of the owner.
AT LARGE means and shall include any dog which:
      (1)   Molests people or passing vehicles (including bicycles);
      (2)   When off premises of the owner, attacks a human or other domestic
animal;
      (3)   Trespasses on school grounds;
      (4)   Damages public or private property;
      (5)   Is an unrestrained female dog in heat;
      (6)   Presents a traffic hazard; and/or
      (7)   Is on private property without the prior permission of the property
owner.
   CAT. A domesticated feline.
   CLEAN. Premises which do not have an offensive odor, do not have an
excessive number of flies or other insects, and/or do not otherwise cause an
unsanitary condition in the enclosure(s) of the surroundings where the animals
are kept.
   CONFINED. Within an enclosure designated to prevent intrusion or escape.
   CONTROL. An animal which is under verbal restraint which:
      (1)   Is physically capable of hearing normal commands and is obedient to
those commands; and
      (2)   Is, at the time under consideration, within reasonable proximity to
the controller so that shouting or excessively loud commands are not necessary.
   DANGEROUS DOG. Includes:
      (1)   Any animal with a known propensity, tendency, or disposition to
attack unprovoked; to cause injury or to otherwise endanger the safety of a
human being or domestic animal without provocation on public or private
property, or which has been deemed vicious by the Municipal Court;
      (2)   Is trained for dog fighting; and/or
      (3)   Is owned or harbored primarily or in part for the purpose of dog
fighting.
   DOG. A domesticated canine.
   DOMESTICATED ANIMAL. Individual animals which are tractable or tame.
“Tractable” means easily managed, taught, docile, or complaint.
   DWELLING UNIT. One or more rooms and a single kitchen designed for or
occupied as a unit by one family or individual for living and cooking purposes.
   FOWL. Any of the larger domestic birds used as food, such as chickens.
   GOOD REPAIR. Is capable of confining the animal(s) for which the enclosure
is being used.
   HYBRID. The offspring of wild animals bred with domestic dogs and cats.
   ISOLATION FACILITY. Any place specified by an animal control officer, police
officer, or county health officer which is equipped with a pen or cage which
isolates an animal from contact with other animals.
   LEASH. A thong, cord, rope, chain, or similar device which holds an animal
in restraint and which is not more than six feet in length.
   LICENSING AUTHORITY. The Town Animal Control Facility or its designees.
   LIVE TRAP. A device designed to capture or contain an animal without causing
injury.
   OWNER/KEEPER. Any person, partnership, corporation, or his or her agents,
owning, keeping, or harboring one or more animals. In the case of dogs bearing
a license issued by the town, the OWNER of such dog shall be presumed, in the
absence of direct evidence to the contrary, to be the registered applicant for
such dog license as disclosed by the records in the office of the Clerk. An
animal shall be deemed to be harbored if it is fed or sheltered for three
consecutive days or more.
   PREMISES OF THE OWNER OR KEEPER OF AN ANIMAL. Only the property over which
the owner or keeper has full possession and control. The unenclosed property of
a condominium or town house or the common passageway, parking facility, or
unenclosed common yard of an apartment building or trailer court are not the
PREMISES OF ANY OWNER OR KEEPER.
   PROPER SHELTER. Natural or artificial shelter appropriate to the local
climatic conditions for the species concerned, as necessary for the health of
the animals.
   PROVOCATION. Threatening, teasing, or striking an animal, or threatening or
striking the animal’s owner either on or off the animal owner’s property.
   PUBLIC NUISANCE. Any animal which:
      (1)   Trespasses on public or private property;
      (2)   Is at large;
      (3)   Damages, soils, urinates, or defecates on private or public
property;
      (4)   Causes garbage which has previously been placed in a garbage or
refuse container to be strewn or deposited on private or public property;
      (5)   Habitually, constantly, or frequently disturbs the sleep, rest,
tranquility, or peace of any neighborhood or person;
      (6)   Chases pedestrians, bicyclists, or motor vehicles;
      (7)   Attacks other domestic animals;
      (8)   Any dog in heat which is not confined;
      (9)   Any animal which is tethered in such a way that either it or its
tether obstructs any part of a public road, alley, or pedestrian walkway; and/
or
      (10)   Any animal which creates offensive odors disturbing to any
neighbor or person.
   RABIES CERTIFICATE. A certificate signed by a licensed veterinarian
verifying that an animal is vaccinated against rabies, and which includes the
date of immunization and the date that the immunization expires.
   RESTRAINT. An animal shall be considered under RESTRAINT if it is under the
physical control of a responsible person by a leash or lead no longer than six
feet in length, or fixed to a stationary object not within reach of a street or
sidewalk, alley, or trail by a tether not less than 15 feet in length, but
within the real property limits of its owner.
   SURRENDER. Forfeiture by the owner of ownership and possession to the town.
   VETERINARY HOSPITAL. An establishment maintained and operated by a licensed
veterinarian for the boarding of animals or the diagnoses and treatment of
diseases and injuries of animals.
   VICIOUS ANIMAL. Includes:
      (1)   Any animal that constitutes a physical threat to human beings or
other animals, or one that bites claws, or attempts to bite or claw any person
or other animal;
      (2)   Any animal which attacks, inflicts injury, assaults, bites, or
menaces other people or other animals in any public or private place without
just provocation;
      (3)   Any animal which, unrestrained or inadequately restrained without
provocation, approaches in an endangering or terrorizing manner, any person
upon the street, sidewalk, or any public ground or place within the animal’s
control district or other contracted area, whether or not the attack was
consummated; and
      (4)   Any animal owned or harbored primarily or in part for the purpose
of vicious animal fighting or any animal trained for vicious animal fighting.
   WILD ANIMAL or EXOTIC PET. Any live monkey (non-human primate), raccoon,
skunk, fox, wolf, poisonous snake, leopard, panther, lion, lynx, or any other
warm-blooded animal which can normally be found in the wild state.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.010)
§ 91.02 TRAPPING.
   (A)   No traps shall be used for capture of an animal within the animal
control district, other than humane live box traps. Also, no traps shall be set
when the temperature exceeds 80°F or falls below 32°F, except in an emergency
which shall be declared upon the sole discretion of the animal control officer.
   (B)   Animals captured must be returned to the owner (if known), taken to a
local veterinary clinic (if possible), relocated to another home, or humanely
disposed of.
   (C)   Traps must be checked every four hours unless they are set in such a
manner as to provide proper shelter once the animal is confined.
   (D)   Animal control officers may go onto private property, exclusive of
buildings, to remove a trapped animal which is not provided with proper
shelter.
   (E)   The final decision for placement of a trap shall be at the discretion
of the animal control officer.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.400) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.03 UNLAWFUL RELEASE OF ANIMALS.
   It is unlawful for any person who is not the owner of, or does not have
charge, care, custody, or control of, any animal to set free the animals from
any enclosed area within which the animal is properly restrained, or from any
restraining device such as a leash or chain which the animal is properly
restrained.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.350) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.04 REMOVING DEAD ANIMALS.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of the animal control officer to remove from the
streets, alleys, and public places within the animal control district all dead
dogs, cats, and other animals, and to further keep records to include location
and description of any such dead animal, and shall notify the owner if known.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the owner to dispose of his or her own animals
from his or her private property.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.360) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.05 RABBITS AND PIGEONS.
   Rabbits and pigeons are allowed within the animal control district, provided
that they meet the following requirements.
   (A)   The animals belong to the individuals owning or renting the property
upon which they are kept.
   (B)   The enclosure, fence, or pen in which the animals are kept shall be at
least 20 feet from all neighboring residences, unless the owners of said
residences waive the requirement in writing.
   (C)   The animals shall be confined, constrained, or in the supervision and
control of their owners or keepers at all times.
   (D)   The fence, pen, or enclosure shall be kept in good repair.
   (E)   The animals are kept for only the purpose of education, science,
companionship, exhibitions, or personal consumption.
   (F)   Animals shall not be allowed to make loud or incessant noise which may
be annoying or discomforting to neighbors in close proximity.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.340) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.06 WILD ANIMALS; WOLF HYBRIDS.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person to own, keep, harbor, or care for any
wild animals or exotic pets within the animal control district, except that
this section shall not apply to the keeping of small cage birds or aquatic
animals, amphibians or non-poisonous reptilian animals, or such small rodents
as hamsters guinea pigs or gerbils, solely as pets, and not to any legally
incorporated college, university, or school.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.450)
   (B)   It is unlawful for any person to own, keep, harbor, or care for any
wolf hybrid. If a wolf hybrid, vaccinated or not, bites someone, it shall be
considered a non-vaccinate.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.460)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.07 CATS.
   (A)   Cats running at large; nuisance.
      (1)   Cats are not prohibited from running at large unless they are
determined to be a nuisance animal by a Town Court Judge after the signing of a
formal complaint/citation with the Town Police Department.
      (2)   If a cat is determined to be a nuisance animal, it is prohibited
from running at large, and the owner or person in control or care of the cat
shall be subject to the same provisions as set forth in §
91.30(A) and all fines related thereto.
   (B)   Animal nuisances defined. Cat owners or those persons having the
control or care of a cat shall exercise the control over their animals
necessary to prevent the animals from becoming a public nuisance by any
excessive or continuous noise caused by the animal; any chasing of vehicles,
bicycles, or people; any attacking of any other domesticated animal; any
trespassing upon public or private property; and any cat that is free roaming
and is not altered (spayed or neutered), all of which shall be considered a
public nuisance for the purposes and intent of this section.
   (C)   Unlawful conduct; stray or homeless cat.
      (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to feed a stray or homeless cat
without taking full responsibility for the cat by providing the cat with
continued humane treatment, which shall include proper food, consumable
unfrozen water, shelter, veterinarian care, rabies vaccinations, and altering
the cat.
      (2)   It shall not be unlawful for a person to feed a stray or homeless
cat while using a humane trap or to sustain a cat for transport to a veterinary
clinic during those periods of time outside of normal business hours.
   (D)   Licensing for cats.
      (1)   (a)   It is unlawful for any person owning, keeping, harboring or
having the care, custody, and control of any animal over three months of age,
within the town, not to obtain a license as provided for in this chapter for
the animal.
         (b)   This requirement does not apply to animals whose owners are not
residents of the town if such animals are not kept in the town any longer than
30 days, and while in the town such animals are kept under restraint and
complete control.
      (2)   No license shall be issued under the provisions of this chapter
unless the applicant therefor has first provided to the licensing authority a
certificate of rabies vaccination issued by a currently licensed veterinarian
of the United States, showing that such animal sought to be licensed has,
within one year past and immediately preceding the date of application for the
license, been vaccinated for rabies.
      (3)   A license shall be issued after compliance with all provisions of
this chapter and the payment of a $5 fee for each animal.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.470) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.08 STOCK ANIMALS.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation to own, harbor, or
care for any stock within the animal control district. This section shall
include but not be limited to horses, cattle, swine, goats, or sheep.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.370)
   (B)   It is unlawful for any owner or keeper of any stock to allow or permit
such stock to be at large within the animal control district.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.380)
   (C)   Animal control officers have the authority to take possession of and
impound any stock found to be at large within the animal control district. Any
stock captured by the animal control officer may be impounded at the town’s
Animal Control Facility or may be placed in the custody of another agency or
person for the purpose of housing and caring for such animals. The owner of
such animals shall be required to pay all costs of housing and care prior to
the release of such animals back to his or her custody.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.390)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.09 FOWL.
   (A)   (1)   Restrictions on domestic fowl. It is unlawful for any person,
firm, or corporation to own, keep, harbor, or care for any domestic fowl
(except chickens and ducks) within the animal control district. This division
(A)(1) prohibits fowl to include but not be limited to turkeys, geese,
peacocks, or guinea fowl.
      (2)   Keeping of chickens/ducks and the amount.
         (a)   Roosters are allowed by this chapter and are counted in the
maximum number of chickens per lot size.
         (b)   Chickens/ducks (and the number of) shall be restricted by the
following guidelines:
 
Minimum Lot Size Number of Fowl
25 ft. x 140 ft. 4 fowl maximum
50 ft. x 140 ft. 5 fowl maximum
60 ft. x 120 ft. 6 fowl maximum
 
         (c)   Chickens and ducks as pets and for egg production in the town
limits shall be for noncommercial use. The keeping of chickens and ducks for
meat production and on-site slaughtering shall not be allowed.
            1.   If the applicant is not the owner of the property, the
applicant must acquire written permission from the owner for the intended use
and submit same with the required permit for having chickens and or ducks.
            2.   The application fee is $50, submitted with the application
permit, and there is a yearly license fee of $25 due on yearly renewal and
inspection of premises.
      (3)   Coops and enclosures. Chickens and ducks shall be kept within a
coop and enclosure at all times. Coops shall provide a minimum of two square
feet per animal, shall be lined with organic bedding material (e.g., hay,
litter, sawdust, and the like), and shall provide sufficient ventilation.
Enclosures must provide a penned area space of eight square feet per animal.
         (a)   Coops and enclosures must be located a minimum of 20 feet from
any inhabited building or any portion of a habitable building. Coops must also
be ten feet from property lines. Coops and enclosures must conform to all
regulations pertaining to accessory buildings.
         (b)   The coop and enclosure and chickens/ducks therein shall not
disturb neighboring residents due to noise, order, damage, or threats to public
health. Coops and enclosures shall be constructed and maintained in a safe and
sanitary condition.
      (4)   Additional requirements.
         (a)   Feed shall be stored indoors in secured containers to prevent
the intrusion of rodents and non-household animals.
         (b)   Refuse from the chickens and or ducks (includes feces, bedding
material, and associated debris) shall be disposed of on a weekly basis. The
use of chicken feces as a fertilizing material is allowed for the applicant’s
own use but is not allowed to be stored or accumulated on the property. The use
of chicken manure as a fertilizer shall be discontinued if noxious odors are
reported and substantiated by the Chief of Police or another town official.
Upon notification by the Chief of Police via citation, or any other town
official or resident complaint having concerns pertaining to noise, noxious
odors, or any other condition affecting the health and public safety of the
residents of the neighborhood, an investigation shall be done and, if
necessary, a citation shall be issued with Court action.
         (c)   No dog or cat that kills a chicken, hen, or duck will, for that
reason alone, be considered a dangerous or aggressive animal, and therefore not
liable for criminal nor civil damages.
      (5)   Preventative action. In order to avoid complaints being filed or
difficulties during the approval process and hearing with the Town Council for
the permit, it is required that anyone filing for this chicken permit provide
written letters of support from abutting property neighbors.
      (6)   Coop or henhouse building requirements.
         (a)   The materials used in the making a henhouse/coop shall be
uniform for each element of the structure, such that the walls are made of the
same material, the roof has the same shingles or other covering, and any
windows or openings are constructed using the same materials.
         (b)   All enclosures for the keeping of chickens shall be constructed
and maintained as to prevent rodents or other pests from being harbored
underneath, inside, or within the walls of the enclosure. The coop must be
impermeable to rodents, wild birds, and predators, including dogs and cats. All
enclosed pens must be kept dry, well ventilated, and in sanitary condition at
all times, and must be cleaned on a regular basis to prevent offensive odors.
         (c)   Adequate shelter, care, and control of the chickens are
required. Any person allowed to keep chickens under this division (A) shall
comply with all the provisions and definitions of the chapter regarding care,
shelter, sanitation, health, rodent control, cruelty, neglect, noise,
reasonable control, and any other requirements pertaining to adequate care and
control of animals in the town.
      (7)   Chicken litter and dead chickens. Chicken litter and waste shall be
disposed of. Also, any dead chickens shall not be deposited in any trash
dumpster (TDS).
(Prior Code, § 6.04.230)
   (B)   It is unlawful for any owner or keeper of any domestic fowl, or his or
her agents, to allow or permit such fowl to be at large within the animal
control district.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.240)
   (C)   Animal control officers have the authority to take possession of any
fowl found to be at large within the animal control district. Any fowl captured
by the animal control officer may be impounded at the town’s Animal Control
Facility or may be placed in the custody of another agency or person for the
purpose of housing and care prior to the release of such animals back to his or
her custody.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.250)
Penalty, see §
91.99
**** ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT ****
§ 91.20 ANIMAL ADOPTION; POLICY AND PROCEDURE.
   Adoption of any animal shall be according to the policy and procedure of the
impoundment facility, which is selected by the town.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.030)
§ 91.21 ANIMAL CONTROL OFFICERS.
   (A)   The animal control officer shall enforce this chapter and shall
provide all impounding animals with suitable care, feed, water, and veterinary
attention while so confined.
   (B)   The animal control officer shall not entice any animal off the owner’s
premises. The animal control officer may go upon any public property to take
into custody any animal suspected of biting a person. The animal control
officer may go upon private property when in fresh pursuit of an animal if the
officer observed the violation.
   (C)   The animal control officer shall keep an accurate record of all
impounded or surrendered animals confined in the Town Animal Shelter. Such
record shall include the date of impoundment and the following (if known): sex,
breed, color, age, inoculation and neutering history, and other pertinent facts
which may assist in identification or adoption of the animal. At the time of
disposal of the animal, the animal control officer shall enter the date, method
of disposal and fee charged, name, address, and phone number of the person
receiving such animal, and other information required by the current town form.
   (D)   The animal control officer may seize and impound any animal at large
which has committed, in the presence of the animal control officer, an act
which is defined as a public nuisance. If an animal is injured and the owner
cannot be immediately located, the animal control officer shall have authority
in the owner’s absence to contact a licensed veterinarian for all necessary and
reasonable care for such injured animal.
   (E)   Upon the veterinarian’s recommendation, the animal control officer may
authorize euthanasia. All reasonable costs incurred for the treatment of the
animal shall be reimbursed to the town by the owner. No animal may be
repossessed by the owner until such costs have been reimbursed to the town.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.040) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.22 ANIMAL CONTROL SHELTER.
   (A)   (1)   The Animal Control Shelter which is notified, or to which an
animal is delivered, shall keep a record of each animal, giving a description,
the date of impoundment or notification, and the disposition of the animal.
      (2)   The Town Animal Control Facility is not required to release the
names of the adopters of unclaimed animals.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.050)
   (B)   It is unlawful for any person to break into the Animal Control Shelter
or vehicle; or turn loose or otherwise release any animal impounded therein; or
hinder, obstruct, or prevent the animal control officer from impounding any
animal liable to be impounded.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.110)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.23 VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   (1)   In addition to or in lieu of impounding a dog found at large, or
in violation of any of the sections listed in this chapter, the animal control
officer or police officer may issue to the known owner, or his or her agent, of
such animal a citation or notice of ordinance violation.
      (2)   A criminal warrant may be initiated before the Municipal or County
Judge in the event the owner or his or her agent fails to appear as stated in
the citation or notice, or fails to post an appropriate bond in lieu thereof.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.070)
   (B)   Whenever an animal control officer or any police officer observes or
has reasonable cause to believe that a person has violated the provisions of
this chapter, such officer is authorized to prepare a written citation
containing a notice to appear in Municipal Court. The citation, at a minimum,
shall be written on a form which notifies the person of the offense with which
he or she is charged and the time and place where the person must appear to
answer the charge.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.430)
§ 91.24 CITATION; PROCEDURE.
   Every animal control officer, upon issuing a citation herein authorized,
shall deposit the original of the citation within the Municipal Court, and
shall issue a copy of the same to be given to the person against whom the
violation is charged. Upon deposit of the original citation with the Court, the
citation may be disposed of only by trial in Court or other official action by
the Judge of the Court, including forfeiture of the bail, or by the deposit of
sufficient bail with or payment of the fine to the Municipal Court by the
person to whom the citation was sworn to, as required under the general laws of
the state in respect to a complaint charging a commission of the offense
alleged in the citation to have been committed; then the citation, when filed
with the Municipal Court, shall be deemed a lawful complaint for the purpose of
prosecution under this chapter.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.120)
§ 91.25 ENFORCEMENT; AUTHORITY.
   The provisions of this chapter shall be enforced by the sworn officers of
the Town Police Department and animal control officer in the performance of his
or her duties, or to fail to obey the lawful order of any animal control
officer.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.210) Penalty, see § 31.99
§ 91.26 ABATEMENT OF CRUELTY.
   (A)   An animal control officer may lawfully interfere to prevent the
perpetration of any act of cruelty upon an animal in his or her presence.
   (B)   (1)   A law enforcement officer or animal control officer may remove,
shelter, and care for any animal(s) found to be cruelly exposed to the weather,
starved or denied water, neglected, abandoned, or otherwise treated in a cruel
manner, and may deliver the animal(s) to another person to be cared for and
given medical attention if necessary.
      (2)   In all cases, the owner (if known) shall immediately be notified,
and such officer or other person having possession of the animal(s) shall have
a lien thereon for its care, keeping, and medical attention, and expense of
notice. If the owner or custodian is unknown and cannot with reasonable effort
be ascertained, or does not within five days after notice redeem the animal by
paying the expenses incurred, it may be treated as abandoned and dealt with as
provided in §
91.32.
   (C)   (1)   Whenever in the opinion of any law enforcement officer, animal
control officer, or county health officer, any animal is suffering from
distemper, parvovirus, or other serious disease or is severely maimed and
suffering from injury, it shall be lawful for such officer to kill such animal
after consulting with a licensed veterinarian if the animal is licensed or
tattooed, and attempts to notify its owner shall be made before the animal is
killed.
      (2)   The officer or representative mentioned in division (C)(1) above
may kill an animal without consulting a licensed veterinarian or an owner if it
is considered an emergency situation to relieve the animal from suffering. The
owner thereof shall not recover damages for the killing of such animal unless
he or she shall prove that such killing was unwarranted.
   (D)   If an animal is restrained or confined without food, consumable water,
or proper attention, the town may enter into and upon any property where the
animal is restrained or confined and supply it with necessary food, water, and
attention, so long as it remains there. If necessary for the health of the
animal, the town may remove the animal and not be liable for any action for the
entry or for taking possession. If the town reasonably believed that a
violation of this section will continue, the town is authorized and empowered
to remove and impound the animal. Additionally, the town is authorized and
empowered to remove and impound any animal in plain view from immediate life
threatening, exigent circumstances.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.140)
§ 91.27 FEES.
   All licenses fees, impoundment fees, boarding fees, adoption fees, and other
revenue received by the Town Animal Control Shelter shall be deposited into the
town’s General Fund.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.220)
§ 91.28 LICENSING REQUIREMENTS; FEES.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person owning, keeping, harboring, or having
custody of any dog over six months of age within the animal control district,
to not obtain a license as provided for in this chapter for the dog. This
requirement does not apply to dogs whose owners are not residents of the animal
control district if such animal is not kept in town any longer than 30 days,
and while in town such animals are kept under restraint.
      (1)   No license shall be issued under the provision of this chapter
unless the applicant therefor has first exhibited to the licensing authority a
certificate of rabies vaccination issued by a licensed veterinarian of this
state or another state, showing that such dog sought to be licensed has, within
one year past and immediately preceding the date of application for license,
been vaccinated for rabies.
      (2)   Dogs must wear license tags at all times when off the premises of
the owner.
      (3)   The licensing authority shall maintain a public record of all tags
issued.
      (4)   A license shall be issued after payment of a $10 fee for all
unaltered dogs and a $5 fee for all altered dogs.
      (5)   A replacement tag may be obtained upon payment of a $1 replacement
fee after proof has been shown that a current license was purchased previously.
If there is a change in ownership of the dog during the license year, the new
owner may have the current license transferred to his or her name upon payment
of $1.
      (6)   No person shall use any license for any dog other than the dog for
which it was issued.
      (7)   All licenses issued under the provisions of this chapter shall
expire on the January 1 of each odd-numbered year. The effective date of any
license issued shall be January 1 of the preceding odd- numbered year.
Notwithstanding the definite term of the license, the same may be issued at any
time of the year.
      (8)   No person other than the owner shall remove the collar or license
tag from the licensed animal.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.290)
   (B)   The license fee shall be waived under the following circumstances:
      (1)   The applicant is the owner or keeper of an authorized police dog;
and
      (2)   The applicant is totally or partially blind, totally or partially
deaf, or otherwise physically disabled and is the owner of a guide dog or
service dog.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.300)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.29 KENNELS.
   (A)   License expiration and renewal.
      (1)   Any license issued under the provisions of this section shall be
issued at any time during the year and shall expire one year after the date of
issue, or on July 1 in the year after the date of issue, whichever period is
shorter, at which time said license shall be renewed upon payment of the
required fees.
      (2)   Any person obtaining a kennel license under this provision of this
section shall pay the sum of $75 to the Town Clerk’s office.
   (B)   Enclosures.
      (1)   Any person holding or who is required to obtain a kennel license
shall provide each adult dog over the age of six months with a structurally
sound, moistureproof and windproof enclosed shelter large enough to accommodate
and keep the animal reasonably clean, dry, and comfortable, and which provides
adequate drainage such that the animal is free to walk or lie down without
coming in contact with standing water.
      (2)   The enclosure shall be constructed of materials and in a manner to
minimize the risk of injury to the animal and the risk of the animal’s escape
in accordance to §
91.52.
   (C)   Maintenance and inspection. The premises and enclosures of all persons
holding or required to obtain a kennel license shall be maintained in a
reasonably sanitary condition which shall minimize the risk of any animal
contracting diseases or parasites, or of causing unreasonable disturbance or
offense to persons within the town. The premises shall be subject to inspection
by the town at all reasonable times without prior notice.
   (D)   Revocation of license. Upon the revocation of any person’s kennel
license or kennel license privilege, the Court may order the impoundment of
such person’s dogs at such person’s expense, pending compliance with the
Court’s orders relating to removal of any one or more of said dogs from the
town.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.330) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.30 DOGS AT LARGE; IMPOUNDMENT.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any owner of any dog, or his or her agent, to allow
or permit such dog to be at large, whether licensed or unlicensed.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.170)
   (B)   Animal control officers have the authority to take possession of and
impound any dog at large.
      (1)   When in pursuit of any dog at large, the officer may go onto
private property, exclusive of buildings, and take such animal into possession
for impoundment. Such officer may utilize a tranquilizer dart if necessary in
order to capture an animal that cannot be captured by any other means.
      (2)   No person may capture, restrain, harbor, or take possession of a
dog not owned by such person, unless the person shall notify or deliver the
animal to the Animal Control Shelter within 24 hours. This does not apply to
persons who take possession of a dog at the owner’s request.
      (3)   If a dog is impounded at the Animal Control Shelter with a valid
license attached to its collar, the animal control officer will notify the
owner by phone, by mail, or in person to the address currently listed on the
license receipt.
      (4)   Dogs impounded at the Animal Control Shelter shall be held no less
than five days from the date of impoundment, unless euthanasia prior to that
time is deemed necessary or appropriate by the shelter manager or veterinarian
advising the animal shelter personnel, before being placed for adoption, and no
less than ten days from the date of impoundment before being humanely disposed
of.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.180)
   (C)   Any impounded female, while in heat, shall be placed in a segregated
enclosure.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.280)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.31 IMPOUNDMENT RELEASE; RECLAMATION.
   (A)   In the event the owner does not claim a dog within ten days, the owner
will lose all rights of ownership to such dog.
      (1)   Any dog impounded and the owner being known to the Police
Department shall be billed for the impoundment and boarding fees until such dog
is reclaimed or destroyed.
      (2)   Any dog not reclaimed by the owner within the period prescribed in
this division (A) shall be deemed abandoned and shall be placed for adoption in
a suitable home or humanely destroyed. In disposing of unclaimed animals, it
shall be lawful for the town or Animal Shelter to surrender them to the
hospital or reputable institution of learning for use in education.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.260)
   (B)   (1)   The owner of an impounded animal shall pay the Town Animal
Control Facility as follows:
 
An impound fee for apprehending and impounding any dog           $5 fee
If the same dog or another dog from the same household should be -
apprehended and impounded:
Within one year of the first dog                                 $20 fee
Within one year of the second dog                                $50 fee
Notes to Table:
The owner of an impounded animal shall pay the Town Animal Control
Facility a boarding fee of $5 per day, or any portion of a day, that the
animal is impounded.
 
      (2)   The Town Animal Control Facility may release an impounded dog to
its owner or representative of the owner if:
         (a)   The owner or representative presents a valid form of
identification;
         (b)   The owner or representative presents evidence that the dog is
licensed. If the animal is not licensed, the owner must purchase a license only
after proving evidence that the dog has been vaccinated against rabies as
prescribed in §
91.53(A);
         (c)   If the owner or keeper does not have a current rabies
vaccination certificate, the owner or keeper may be required to place a cash
deposit of $50 with the Animal Control Facility to be refunded upon presenting,
within five business days thereafter, proof of current rabies vaccination and
license; and
         (d)   The owner or representative pays the boarding and impoundment
fees or fines for the animal.
      (3)   The Town Animal Control Facility may release a dog to a person
other than the owner if:
         (a)   The owner does not claim an impounded dog within five days; and/
or
         (b)   The person to whom the dog is to be released has filled out the
necessary adoption application, returned it for approval by the Town Animal
Control Facility, has been approved to adopt by the Animal Control Facility,
and abides by the Facility’s rules.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.270)
§ 91.32 FAILURE TO CONTROL DOG; DISPOSAL OF ANIMAL.
   (A)   No owner shall fail to exercise proper care and control of his or her
dog(s) to prevent them from becoming a public nuisance as defined in §
91.01.
   (B)   (1)   Any owner which is the subject of three violations of §§
91.30(A) and
91.50(A) within a two-year period shall be, on the third time, declared a
habitual public nuisance, and upon determination thereof by the Court, the
owner shall be required to have the dog destroyed or relocated outside the
animal control district.
      (2)   (a)   The owner shall have ten days from the date of notification
to relocate such dog outside the animal control district and provide proof of
such relocation.
         (b)   At the end of this period, if the dog is not relocated, the dog
shall be humanely disposed of by the law enforcement authority or the animal
control officer at the owner’s or keeper’s expense.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.160) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.33 SURRENDERING ANIMALS.
   (A)   Any dog (animal) surrendered by its owner to the town’s chosen animal
shelter shall be held no less than five days, providing the animal is healthy,
adequate cage space is available, and a surrender fee (if required) has been
paid.
   (B)   As a condition of acceptance of a surrendered animal, the animal
control officer shall require identification of the person surrendering the
animal.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.100)
**** CONDITIONS AND REGULATIONS ****
§ 91.45 MOTOR ACCIDENTS; RESPONSIBILITY.
   The driver of any motor vehicle involved in an accident resulting in injury
to a domestic animal shall immediately and safely stop and render such
assistance as may be possible and shall take reasonable steps to notify the
owner of such animal or give notice of such accident to the local Police
Department or Animal Control Department.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.020) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.46 WASTE.
   (A)   It shall be the responsibility of the owner of a dog to keep his or
her property free and clear of animal waste so as not to present a potential
danger to the public health, as determined by the county health officer. If the
determination is made that a situation exists which is potentially dangerous to
the public health, a notice of ordinance violation may be issued.
   (B)   The owner of every dog shall be responsible for the removal of any
excreta deposited by his or her animal on public walks, recreation areas, or
private property.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.060) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.47 ANIMALS IN VEHICLES.
   (A)   (1)   No person shall leave a dog unattended in the bed of a pickup
truck in a public parking area unless the dog is restrained in such a manner as
to prevent the dog from making physical contact with a pedestrian who is
passing the truck in a place where the pedestrian is legally entitled to be.
      (2)   It is considered cruel and therefore unlawful for a person to leave
a dog, cat, or any animal unattended in a motor vehicle when the temperature is
70°F or above unless, in the opinion of the officer, adequate ventilation and
water is provided.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.080)
   (B)   (1)   If a police officer or animal control officer observes a vehicle
parked within the animal control district in which domestic or wild animals are
confined with no means of ventilation or water, and circumstances are such that
the health or safety of the animal(s) are threatened, the police officer or
animal control officer shall immediately attempt to locate the vehicle owner or
person responsible to provide ventilation and water, or remove such animal from
the vehicle.
      (2)   If unable to locate the vehicle owner or person responsible within
a reasonable amount of time, the police officer or animal control officer shall
be empowered to use whatever means are reasonably necessary to enter the
vehicle to preclude serious harm to the animal. No officer or agent taking
action under this division (B) shall be liable for damage reasonably necessary
to rescue the confined animal.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.090)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.48 CRUELTY; UNLAWFUL ACTS.
   (A)   Cruelty to animals shall be prohibited within the animal control
district.
   (B)   No person shall override, overload, drive when overloaded, overwork,
torture, or torment an animal or deprive an animal of necessary sustenance.
   (C)   No person shall unnecessarily or cruelly beat, mutilate, or kill an
animal unless specifically authorized by law.
   (D)   No person shall cause, instigate, be a spectator at, or permit a
dogfight, cockfight, bullfight (bloodless or otherwise), or other combat
involving animals.
   (E)   No person shall abandon any animal. All animals which are deemed
abandoned shall be considered surrendered to the town for impoundment,
adoption, or disposal.
   (F)   No person shall fail to provide his or her animal with sufficient good
and wholesome food and water that is able to be consumed and is unfrozen,
proper shelter to protect it from the weather (including sunlight), veterinary
care when needed to prevent suffering, and with humane care and treatment.
   (G)   It is unlawful to annoy, bait, harass, torment, or tease any confined
or chained animal.
   (H)   Unless specifically authorized by law, no person shall willfully:
      (1)   Maim or disfigure any domestic or wild animal; or
      (2)   Administer poison, cause to be ingested any foreign object to any
such animal, or expose a poisonous substance with the intent that it shall be
taken by any animal, except pests of public health concern.
   (I)   The provisions of this section do not and will not in any way limit
the right of a police officer or animal control officer to kill any domestic or
wild animal if such officer determines that there is a reasonable danger to the
public safety.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.130) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.49 DANGEROUS DOGS.
   Upon conviction of an owner, by a court of competent jurisdiction, of a
vicious dog violation, such dog shall be considered a “dangerous dog”. When a
dangerous dog is allowed to remain within the animal control district, the
following requirements shall be enforced.
   (A)   No owner of a dangerous dog shall permit such dog to go beyond the
property of the owner or keeper unless such dog is securely muzzled and
physically restrained with a chain having a minimum tensile strength of 300
pounds and not exceeding three feet in length.
   (B)   When off the premises of the owner or keeper, such dog shall be
accompanied by a competent person physically capable of handling such dog.
   (C)   While unattended on the owner’s property, a dangerous dog shall be
securely confined, in a humane manner, indoors or in a securely enclosed and
locked pen or structure suitably designed to prevent the entry of young
children and to prevent the dog from escaping. The pen or dangerous animal run
area must have all sides six-feet high and a secure top. If the pen structure
has no bottom secured to the sides, the sides must be embedded into the ground
no less than one foot.
   (D)   The owner of a dangerous dog shall post warnings signs on the property
where the dog is kept that are clearly visible and that inform persons that a
dangerous dog is on the property.
   (E)   No person shall possess with the intent to sell, offer for sale,
breed, or buy or attempt to buy within the animal control district, any
dangerous dog.
   (F)   (1)   Any dangerous dog may be immediately seized and impounded by an
animal control officer or police officer if the owner is in violation of this
section.
      (2)   The owner shall be responsible for the reasonable cost incurred by
the town for the care of a dangerous dog confiscated by the animal control
officer or for the destruction of any dangerous dog if the action by the town
is pursuant to law and if the owner violated this section. Any such dog shall
be retained by the town pending the order of an appropriate court.
   (G)   In addition to any other penalty, a court may order the town to
dispose of a dangerous dog in an expeditious and humane manner.
   (H)   Any dangerous dog found upon any property, public or private, not the
premises of the owner may, if such animal cannot be safely taken up and
impounded by reasonable means, be destroyed by the animal control or police
officer.
   (I)   The owner of a dangerous dog shall notify the town, within one hour of
learning of the animal’s escape or release, that the animal is loose,
unconfined, has attacked another animal or a human being, or has died or been
given away.
      (1)   If the dangerous dog has been given away, immediately upon the
conveyance, the owner or keeper shall provide the Animal Control Shelter with
the name, address, and telephone number of the new owner.
      (2)   Upon conveyance, the new owner, if he or she resides within the
animal control district, will be obligated to comply with the requirements of
this chapter and section.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.150) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.50 DISTURBING THE PEACE.
   (A)   It is unlawful for an owner or keeper of a dog to allow or permit such
dog to cause serious annoyance or disturbance to any person or persons by
frequent and habitual howling, yelping, barking, or otherwise.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.190)
   (B)   (1)   If the owner or keeper of a dog declared to be disturbing the
peace, as provided in division (A) above, refuses to quiet the dog after being
duly notified of the same, it shall be the duty of the Police Department to
apprehend the dog and to cause such dog to be impounded in the Animal Control
Shelter.
      (2)   Upon the capture and impounding of any dog, it shall be the duty of
the officer to notify the owner thereof, if such owner can be ascertained,
which notice may be given.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.200)
Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.51 MAINTAINING YARDS; PENS.
   (A)   It is unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation to keep, use, or
maintain any yard, pen, or place on his or her premises or premises under his
or her control, within or near the animal control district, in which a number
of cattle, swine, or other animals may be kept in such a manner as to become
offensive to any person residing within the vicinity or annoying to the public.
If the determination is made that a situation exists which is offensive to
someone residing within the vicinity, a notice of ordinance violation may be
issued.
   (B)   An enclosure is unclean when it contains more than one day’s
eliminations of each animal enclosed therein.
   (C)   An enclosure is unhealthy when it is likely to cause illness or injury
to the animals.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.310) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.52 PROPER FOOD, SHELTER, AND CARE.
   (A)   Each animal shall, at suitable intervals and a least once every 24
hours, receive a quantity of wholesome foodstuff suitable for the species’
physical condition and age, sufficient to maintain an adequate level of
nutrition for the animal.
   (B)   Each animal shall, at suitable intervals and at least once every 24
hours, receive an adequate supply of clean, fresh, consumable water for the
species’ physical condition and age, and such water shall be provided either
free flowing or in a receptacle. If water pans or dishes are used, the pans or
dishes shall have weighted bottoms or be mounted or secured in a manner to
prevent tipping and in a manner to prevent the water from freezing and being
unable to be consumed.
   (C)   Each animal shall have convenient access to shelter throughout the
year. Any artificial shelter shall be structurally sound and maintained in good
repair to protect the animal from injury from the elements, and shall be of
sufficient size to permit the animal to enter, stand, turn around, and lie down
in a natural manner.
      (1)   Any shelter which does not protect the animal from temperature
extremes or precipitation, or which does not provide adequate ventilation or
drainage, shall not comply with this section.
      (2)   This shelter, any other spaces accessible to the animal, and all
bedding for the animal shall be maintained in a manner which minimizes the risk
to the animal for contracting disease, being injured, or becoming infested with
parasites.
   (D)   Each animal shall receive care and medical treatment for debilitating
injuries, parasites, and disease, sufficient to maintain the animal in good
health and minimize suffering.
   (E)   The living area for each animal being kept, confined, or restrained
shall have adequate drainage such that the animal shall be free to walk or lie
down without coming in contact with standing water.
   (F)   If any animal is restricted by a chain, leash, wire cable, electronic
collar, underground electronic fences, or similar restraint, such restraint
shall be designed and placed to prevent choking or strangulation or
entanglement with other objects or other animals. Such restrain shall be no
less than ten feet in length.
   (G)   (1)   If any animal is kept in an enclosure, that enclosure shall be
constructed of material and in a manner to minimize the risk of injury to the
animal, and shall encompass sufficient usable space to keep the animal in good
condition.
      (2)   When a dog is confined outside a residence, the following minimum
requirements shall be used:
 
Size of Dog Pen Size of Dog                         Measurements
Extra large     Over 25 in. at withers or over 75   6 ft. x 8 ft. (48 sq. ft.)
                lbs.
Large           Over 20 in. up to 25 in., not over  5 ft. x 8 ft. (40 sq. ft.)
                75 lbs.
Medium          Over 12 in. up to 20 in., not over  5 ft. x 7 ft. (35 sq. ft.)
                50 lbs.
Small           12 in. or less at withers, not over 5 ft. x 6 ft. (30 sq. ft.)
                20 lbs.
Notes to Table:
An additional 16 square feet shall be required for each dog sharing the pen
with another animal.
The minimum pen size excludes the shelter.
 
   (H)   The Town Animal Control Shelter shall be exempt from the pen size
requirements under this section since the intent is only to hold animals for a
maximum of ten days.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.320) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.53 VACCINATIONS; RABIES CONTROL.
   (A)   The owner of any dog shall have the dog vaccinated against rabies
within 30 days after the dog reaches the age of six months.
      (1)   If the owner obtains the dog or brings the dog in to the animal
control district after the dog reaches six months of age, the owner of the dog
shall have the dog vaccinated against rabies within 30 days after the dog was
obtained or brought into the town animal control district, unless the dog has
been vaccinated as evidenced by a current certificate of rabies vaccination
from this state or another state.
      (2)   The owner of a dog shall have a dog vaccinated against rabies by a
veterinarian before the date that the immunization expires, as stated on the
certificate of vaccination, or if no date is specified, within two years after
that previous vaccination.
   (B)   The owner shall pay the cost of the rabies vaccination.
   (C)   A law enforcement officer, animal control officer, county health
officer, or his or her designee may order a dog quarantined if the officer has
reason to believe the animal bit a person, is infected with rabies, or has been
in contact with a rabid animal.
      (1)   Any dog, if not currently vaccinated against rabies, which inflicts
upon any person a wound breaking the skin, shall be impounded within 24 hours
at the isolation facility at the owner’s choosing for a period of no less than
ten days. If a quarantine cannot be imposed because the dog cannot be captured,
the officer may kill the animal. The officer may kill a dog as a last resort or
if the owner agrees. The officer shall attempt to kill the animal in a humane
manner and in a manner which avoids damage to the head. After the animal is
dead, the head is to be severed from the body and sent to the state laboratory
for examination.
      (2)   In addition, a law enforcement officer, animal control officer,
county health officer, or his or her designee may kill a dog and have it
examined for rabies if the dog has not been vaccinated as provided in division
(A) above, if he or she has reason to believe the dog has been exposed to or in
physical contact with a rabid animal, and he or she has reason to believe the
dog has been in physical contact with humans.
   (D)   An officer who orders the dog to be quarantined shall deliver the
animal or shall order the animal to be delivered to an isolation facility as
soon as possible, but no longer than 24 hours after that original order is
issued. If the animal is currently immunized against rabies and was not off the
premises of the owner at the time of the bite, the officer may order the animal
quarantined on the owner’s premises. If such animal is found outside the
owner’s residence, it shall be taken and confined at an isolation facility of
the owner’s choosing for the remainder of the confinement period.
   (E)   The custodian of an isolation facility shall keep the dog which is
ordered to be quarantined in strict isolation under the supervision of a
veterinarian. Supervision of a veterinarian for an animal ordered quarantined
on the owner’s premises shall include examination by a veterinarian within 24
hours of the bite and on the tenth day of quarantine. If the observation period
has not been extended and if the veterinarian certifies that the dog has not
exhibited any symptoms of rabies, the animal may be released from quarantine at
the end of ten-day observation period.
   (F)   If the veterinarian determines that a dog exhibits symptoms of rabies
during the original or extended observation period, the veterinarian shall
notify the county health officer and the officer who ordered the animal
quarantined, and the officer or veterinarian shall humanely destroy the animal
and have the head examined. If the dog has bitten a person, the County Health
Department shall notify the person’s physician.
   (G)   The owner of an animal is responsible for any expenses incurred in
connection with the keeping of the animal quarantined in an isolation facility,
supervision and examination of the animal by a veterinarian, preparation of the
carcass for laboratory examination, and the fee for examination. If the owner
is unknown, the town is responsible for these expenses.
   (H)   Any person having knowledge that an animal has bitten a human shall
immediately report that incident to the animal control officer or the county
health officer, together with the name and address of the person or persons
bitten (if known).
(Prior Code, § 6.04.410) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.54 VICIOUS ANIMALS.
   (A)   Any animal that constitutes a physical threat to any human being or to
any other animal by biting, clawing, attacking, lunging toward, growling,
barking, baring teeth, or otherwise acts in a menacing manner toward or at any
human being or other animal, or if any of the aforementioned acts or conduct is
attempted toward or at any human being or other animal, it is a violation of
this section.
   (B)   No animal may be declared a vicious animal if the animal was
protecting or defending a human being within the immediate vicinity of the
animal from unjust attack or assault.
   (C)   No person shall own or harbor any vicious animal for the purpose of
fighting, or use any animal for the purpose of causing or encouraging
unprovoked attacks upon human beings or domestic animals.
   (D)   This section shall not apply to dogs owned by law enforcement
agencies.
   (E)   The owner of any dog deemed vicious by a court of competent
jurisdiction shall have that animal spayed or neutered, at the owners expense,
within 30 days of the determination that the dog is vicious (if such animal is
not currently spayed or neutered), and shall show proof of such to the court
within 45 days of the determination.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.420) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   (1)   For the purpose of this division (A), a CONVICTION means a
finding of guilty by a court after trial, or a plea of guilty or no contest to
the offense charged.
      (2)   If any violation is continuing, each day’s violation shall be
deemed to constitute a separate offense.
      (3)   Upon a conviction for a violation of any of the provisions of this
chapter, a person shall be fined not more than $750, plus any applicable court
costs.
      (4)   All previous records of the violator shall be considered by the
court in regard to determination of the penalty.
      (5)   In addition to the penalties cited above and in this chapter, the
Municipal Judge shall be empowered to order any dog destroyed after the dog has
been deemed vicious by the court. If the judge orders the specific disposition
of the animal, such order shall be carried out immediately.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.440)
   (B)   The penalty and violation for §
91.01(A)(7) shall be a misdemeanor, a fine of $750, and revocation of permit
and license to have chickens.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.230)
   (C)   (1)   Any person who shall violate any provision of §
91.29, or who shall attempt to evade any of the provisions of §
91.29, shall be deemed guilty, be fined any sum not to exceed the sum of $100,
and such person’s kennel license be revoked for not more than two years from
the date of conviction.
      (2)   Upon the second conviction, such person shall be fined any sum not
less than $100 and not greater than $300, and such person’s kennel license and
privilege to obtain any kennel license shall be revoked for not less than one
year and not more than two years from the date of conviction.
      (3)   Upon the third or any subsequent conviction hereof, such person
shall be fined any sum not less than $150 and not greater than $750, and such
person’s kennel license and privilege to obtain any kennel license shall be
revoked for not less than two years and not more than four years from the date
of conviction.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.330)
   (D)   (1)   An owner who refuses to have a dog vaccinated against rabies as
required in §
91.53 may, in addition to the other penalties, be required to forfeit not less
than $25 nor more than $100.
      (2)   An owner who refuses to comply with an order issued under §
91.53 to deliver an animal to an officer, isolation facility, or veterinarian,
or who does not comply with the conditions of an order that an animal be
quarantined, shall be fined not less than $100 and not more than $750, plus
court costs.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.410)
   (E)   (1)   The owner or custodian of said vicious animal described in §
91.54 shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and fined no more than $750 plus $10 in
court costs if convicted.
      (2)   Upon conviction of a person for harboring a vicious dog in
violation of §
91.54, if it shall appear to the court that the dog is living, the court may,
in addition to punishment provided for violation of §
91.54, order the animal control officer or any officer to forthwith cause the
dog to be humanely destroyed, and for this purpose any such officer charged
with such duty shall have the right to enter upon any premises within the town.
(Prior Code, § 6.04.420)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 110.01 LICENSE REQUIRED GENERALLY.
   (A)   Any person or persons, partnership, corporation, or other entities
engaged in any of the following businesses named in this division (A) shall be
liable to the town for the sum of $25 per year, to-wit: general merchandise,
hardware, furniture, drugs and sundries, lumberyards, banks, hotels, feed
stores, meat markets, coal yards, telephone companies, printing offices,
garages, secondhand stores, bowling alleys, moving pictures, elevators,
taxicabs, undertakers, embalmers, and dray lines.
   (B)   Any person or persons, partnership, or corporation engaged in the
following businesses named in this division (B) shall be liable to the town for
the sum of $25 per year or part thereof as a license tax to be paid by May 1 of
each year to-wit: doctors, lawyers, dentists, real estate firms and dealers,
loan offices, abstractors or real estate titles, insurance agents, tire repair
shops, confectioners, barbers, photographers, boardinghouses, restaurants,
contractors and builders, tractors, retail ice sellers, chiropractors,
osteopaths, and all sales agencies exclusive of automobiles, motortrucks,
aircraft, motorcycles, tractors, adding machines, typewriters, farm lighting
plants, and other personal property of any kind, character, and description.
   (C)   Any person or persons, partnership, or corporation engaged in any of
the following businesses shall pay to the town, as a license tax, the amount
set forth after each respective business as follows:
 
Carnivals                             The sum of $50 per day payable in advance
Circuses and other exhibitions in the $25 per day payable in advance, provided
open air or tents                     that nothing herein contained shall apply
                                      to Chatauquas or charitable benefits
Itinerant or street peddlers          The sum of $25 per day payable in advance
Transient merchants                   The sum of $25 per day for the first day
                                      and $5 per day for each day thereafter
 
   (D)   The license tax herein provided shall be for the purpose of raising
revenue for the town.
   (E)   Where more than one business or occupation are combined in one
business, then one license tax imposed by this section shall be imposed, which
said tax shall be the highest tax levied against any of the businesses, trades,
and the like. Taxes levied by this section shall be in addition to license fees
and taxes levied heretofore and existing at the time the ordinance codified
herein took effect.
(Ord. 8, passed 5-19-1925) Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.02 PAWNBROKERS; SECONDHAND DEALERS.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      PAWNBROKER. Any person who advances or loans money or other valuable
things on the deposit of personal property on the condition of selling the same
back at a stipulated price.
      SECONDHAND DEALER. Any person who buys, sells, exchanges, or deals in
personal property that has been previously used, broken, or disfigured, and is
not classed as antique; provided that merchants who deal in new furniture,
dishes, or other regular household furnishings accept such articles for credit
on the purchase price of new or used articles sold contemporaneously which are
of similar character to the goods so exchanged shall not be considered
SECONDHAND DEALERS.
   (B)   No person shall at any time carry on the business of a pawnbroker or
secondhand dealer without obtaining a license therefor.
   (C)   The license fees required by this section shall be as set by the
governing body from time to time.
   (D)   Pawnbroker’s licenses shall not be transferable.
   (E)   Persons holding a pawnbroker’s license and also engaged in business as
a secondhand dealer shall not be required to be licensed as a secondhand dealer
in addition to that of a pawnbroker.
   (F)   Any applicant for a pawnbroker’s license shall execute to the town a
bond in the penal sum of $1,000 conditioned that, in the conduct of such
business, he or she will respect and obey the provisions of this section and
other ordinances of the town applicable thereto, and will pay all damages that
may accrue to any person by reason of any fraud or misconduct in managing such
business.
   (G)   (1)   Every pawnbroker shall keep at his or her place of business an
accurate description of:
         (a)   All personal property, bonds, notes, and other securities
received on deposit or purchased;
         (b)   The time when they were received or purchased;
         (c)   Mentioning particularly any descriptive or identifying marks
that may be on such property, bonds, notes or other securities, together with
the name, residence, and accurate description of the person by whom they were
left; and
         (d)   Such other information as may be required by the governing body.
      (2)   Such entries must be made on the day such property is taken in,
using ink or indelible pencil, and no entry shall be erased or obliterated. All
such records shall be clean and legible.
   (H)   Every pawnbroker shall, upon request during the ordinary business
hours, submit and exhibit the records required by division (H) above to the
inspection of any law enforcement officer and permit any officer to make a copy
thereof. He or she shall also, upon request, exhibit for the inspection of any
law enforcement office any goods, personal property, bonds, notes, or other
securities that may be so received by him or her.
   (I)   No pawnbroker shall transact any such business with any person under
the age of 18 years.
(Prior Code, § 7-11) Penalty, see §
110.99
**** UTILITY WORKERS ****
§ 110.15 WATER TREATMENT CONTRACTOR; DEFINITION.
   For purposes of this subchapter, a WATER TREATMENT CONTRACTOR shall be
deemed to be any person engaged in the business of making any connections to
any water lines within the town for the purpose of installing water softeners,
underground lawn sprinkler systems, fire extinguishing sprinkler systems, or
any other connection made to any water line other than those made by a licensed
plumber.
(Prior Code, § 7-1)
§ 110.16 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   No person shall engage in business as a gas pipe fitter, plumber,
electrician, or water treatment contractor in the town without first obtaining
a license.
(Prior Code, § 7-2) Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.17 APPLICATION; FEES AND BONDS.
   (A)   (1)   Every person desiring to enter into business as a gas pipe
fitter, plumber, electrician, or water treatment contractor shall make
application to the Town Clerk for a license. Every application shall set forth
the name of the applicant and his or her qualifications, together with such
other information as may be required by the town.
      (2)   Each applicant shall submit to an examination as prescribed by the
town to determine the fitness to hold any license; provided however, that the
applicant will not be required to submit to an examination if the applicant can
produce a valid license from any of the following towns:
         (a)   Torrington, Wyoming;
         (b)   Douglas, Wyoming;
         (c)   Lingle, Wyoming;
         (d)   Wheatland, Wyoming;
         (e)   Cheyenne, Wyoming; and
         (f)   Casper, Wyoming.
(Prior Code, § 7-3)
   (B)   Each application shall be accompanied by a fee of $50 and a bond in
the penal sum of $50,000 or more, executed in favor of the town, conditioned
for the faithful performance of the duties for which the license is issued.
(Prior Code, § 7-4)
Penalty, see §
110.99
§ 110.18 TERM; RENEWAL.
   (A)   Upon meeting the requirements set forth in §
110.17, every successful applicant shall receive a license. All licenses issued
hereunder shall expire on December 31 of the year of issue.
(Prior Code, § 7-5)
   (B)   Any license may be renewed yearly by payment of an annual fee of $50
and a current bond in the penal sum of $50,000, executed in favor of the town,
conditioned for the faithful performance of the duties for which the license is
issued. Every licensee who desires to renew a license shall make application
for renewal to the Town Clerk within 30 days prior to the date of expiration of
the license to be renewed.
(Prior Code, § 7-6)
§ 110.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person or persons, partnership, or corporation violating any of
the provisions of §
110.01 shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof,
shall be fined in a sum of not more than $750 plus costs of prosecution as set
forth by the laws of the state or by rules of the Supreme Court.
(Ord. 8, passed 5-19-1925; Ord. 127, passed 8-4-1987)
CHAPTER 111: PEDDLERS AND SOLICITORS
