Editor’s note:
Appeal to United States Supreme Court dismissed, see case of J.L. Bunger v.
Town of Green River, Wyoming, 300 U.S. 638, 57 S. Ct. 510
Case holding that only undue or discriminatory burdens upon commerce are
forbidden by the Commerce Clause of the United States Constitution, see Breard
v. City of Alexandria, Louisiana, 341 U.S. 622, 91 L.E. 1223, 71 S. Ct. 920
Case holding that ordinances barring nonresidents of state from selling by
sample and taking orders for goods without first procuring license is in
contravention of the Commerce Clause of the United States Constitution, see
Clements v. Town of Casper, 4 Wyo. 494, 35 P. 472 (1894)
Case holding that taxation which leaves out-of-state sellers in the same
position as in-state sellers does not violate the Commerce Clause of the United
States Constitution, see Breard v. City of Alexandria, Louisiana, 341 U.S. 622,
91 L.E. 1223, 71 S. Ct. 920
Case holding that when local police regulation has a real relation to suitable
protection of the people and is reasonable in its requirement, it is not
invalid because it may incidentally affect interstate commerce, provided that
it does not conflict with federal statutes, see Town of Green River v. Bunger,
50 Wyo. 52, 58 P. 2d 456 (1936)
§ 111.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CANVASSER or SOLICITOR.
      (1)   Any individual, whether a resident of the town or not, traveling
either by foot or any type of conveyance from place to place, from house to
house, or from street to street, taking or attempting to take orders for sale
of goods, wares, and merchandise; personal property of any nature whatsoever
for future delivery; or for services to be furnished or performed in the
future, whether or not such individual has, carries, or exposes for sale a
sample of the subject of such sale, or whether he or she is collecting advance
payments on such sales or not.
      (2)   Such definition shall include any person who, for himself, herself,
or for another person, hires, leases, uses, or occupies any building,
structure, tent, hotel room, lodging house, apartment, shop, or any other place
within the town for the sole purpose of exhibiting samples and taking orders
for future delivery.
   PEDDLER. Any person, whether a resident of the town or not, traveling by
foot, wagon, automobile, vehicle, or any other type or conveyance from place to
place, from house to house, or from street to street carrying, conveying, or
transporting goods, wares, merchandise, meats, fish, vegetables, fruits, garden
truck, farm products or provisions, offering and exposing the same for sale or
making sales, and delivering articles to purchasers; provided that one who
solicits orders and as a separate transaction makes deliveries to purchasers as
a part of a scheme or design to evade the provisions of this chapter shall be
deemed a PEDDLER subject to the provisions of this chapter. The word PEDDLER
shall include the words HAWKER and HUCKSTER.
   TRANSIENT MERCHANT.
      (1)   Any person, whether as owner, agent, consignee, or employee and
whether a resident of the town or not, who engages in a temporary business of
selling and delivering goods, wares, and merchandise; and who, in furtherance
of such purposes, hires, leases, uses, or occupies any building, structure,
motor vehicle, tent, public rooms in hotels, lodging houses, apartments, shops,
or any streets, alleys, or other places for the exhibition and sale of such
goods, wares, and merchandise, either privately or at public auction; provided
that such definition shall not be construed to include any person who, while
occupying such temporary location, does not sell from stock but exhibits
samples only for the purpose of securing orders for future delivery.
      (2)   The term TRANSIENT MERCHANT shall include the terms ITINERANT
MERCHANT and ITINERANT VENDOR.
(Prior Code, § 15-1)
§ 111.02 SIGNS PROHIBITING PEDDLERS AND SOLICITORS.
   It shall be unlawful for any peddler, solicitor, or canvasser to ring the
door bell or knock on the door of any residence, dwelling, or apartment whereon
a sign bearing the words “no peddlers” or other words of similar import is
painted, affixed, or exposed to public view, or to attempt to gain admittance
thereto, provided that the provisions of this section shall not apply to any
solicitor or canvasser who knocks at any door or rings any bell at the
invitation or with the consent of some member of the household at which he or
she so applied for admission.
(Prior Code, § 15-2) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 111.03 EXEMPTIONS.
   (A)   Nothing in this chapter shall apply to any farmer or producer who
sells or disposes of his or her own agricultural products, including fruits or
vegetables or of beef, mutton, or pork; provided that such meats are produced
in accordance with all health regulations and laws if such merchandise is
produced by him or her in the state.
(Prior Code, § 15-3)
   (B)   No license shall be required of drummers, traveling salespersons, or
other persons engaged in soliciting or taking orders exclusively from the trade
or established wholesale or retail dealers for the delivery of goods, wares, or
merchandise by wholesale.
(Prior Code, § 15-4)
§ 111.04 ASSOCIATION WITH LOCAL DEALERS.
   Persons engaged in any business regulated by this chapter shall not be
relieved from complying with the provisions of this chapter merely by reason of
associating temporarily with any local dealer, trader, merchant, or auctioneer
or by conducting such transient business in connection with, as part of, or in
the name of any local dealer, trader, merchant, or auctioneer.
(Prior Code, § 15-5)
§ 111.05 PRIVATE RESIDENCES.
   No person shall engage in the practice of going in and upon private
residences into the town as a solicitor, agent or representative, peddler,
hawker, itinerant merchant, and transient vendor of merchandise, not having
been requested or invited to do so by the owners or occupants of such private
residences, for the purpose of soliciting orders for the sale of goods, wares,
and merchandise; or for the purpose of disposing of, peddling, or hawking the
same; or soliciting orders for goods or services; or for taking orders for any
type of contract for present or future completion or continuation.
(Prior Code, § 15-6) Penalty, see §
10.99
Editor’s note:
   Case discussing the definition of peddlers, hawkers, and the like, see
Clements v. Town of Casper, 4 Wyo. 494, 35 P. 472 (1894)
   Case holding that an ordinance forbidding the going on private premises by
solicitors, peddlers, hawkers, itinerant merchants, and transient vendors in
the absence of an invitation by the owners or occupants does not contravene the
Due Proves Clause where reasonable bases exist for the ordinance, see Breard v.
City of Alexandria, Louisiana, 341 U.S. 622, 91 L.E. 1223, 71 S. Ct. 920
   Case holding that ordinances declaring uninvited visitation of private
residences by solicitors, peddlers, hawkers, itinerant merchants, and transient
vendors a nuisance punishable as a misdemeanor is valid exercise of police
power to prevent disturbances and is not repugnant to the Commerce Clause of
the United States Constitution, see Town of Green River v. Bunger, 50 Wyo. 52,
58 P. 2d 456 (1936)
§ 111.06 REQUIRED LICENSE.
   It shall be unlawful for a transient merchant, peddler, solicitor, or
canvasser to engage in any such business without first obtaining a license
therefor in compliance with the provisions of §§
111.07 through
111.11.
(Prior Code, § 15-7) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 111.07 FILING; CONTENTS OF APPLICATIONS.
   Applicants for a license under §§
111.07 through
111.11 shall file within the Town Clerk’s office a sworn application in writing
which shall give the following information:
   (A)   The name and description of the applicant;
   (B)   The permanent home address and full local address of the applicant,
and the phone and cell number;
   (C)   A brief description of the nature of the business and the goods to be
sold;
   (D)   If employed, the name and address of the employer, together with
credentials establishing the exact relationship;
   (E)   The length of time for which the right to do business is desired;
   (F)   The place where the goods or property proposed to be sold, or orders
taken for the sale thereof, are manufactured or produced, where such goods or
products are located at the time such application is filed, and the proposed
method of delivery;
   (G)   A photograph of the applicant or photo of driver’s license filed
immediately with the filing of the application; and
   (H)   A statement as to whether the applicant has been convicted of any
crime or violation of any town law, the nature of the offense, and the
punishment or penalty assessed therefor.
(Prior Code, § 15-8)
§ 111.08 FEES.
   The license fee, which shall be paid to the town, within the office of the
Clerk, upon the issuance of a license hereunder, shall be as established from
time to time by the Town Council, provided that:
   (A)   If any person has more than one person engaged in the business of
peddling or uses more than one motortruck, wagon, or other vehicle in such
business of peddling, the license fees shall be paid for each of such persons,
motortrucks, wagons, or vehicles used in such business.
   (B)   If any person has more than one person engaged in the business of
soliciting or canvassing, the foregoing license fee shall be paid for each
person engaged in such business.
(Prior Code, § 15-9)
§ 111.09 EXHIBITION.
   Any person licensed under the provisions of this chapter shall exhibit his
or her license at the request of any citizen.
(Prior Code, § 15-10) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 111.10 TRANSFER.
   No license issued under the provisions of this chapter shall be transferred.
(Prior Code, § 15-11) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 111.11 REVOCATION.
   (A)   Licenses issued under the provisions of this chapter may be revoked by
the Town Council, after notice and hearing, for any of the following causes:
      (1)   Fraud, misrepresentation, or false statements contained in the
application for license;
      (2)   Fraud, misrepresentation, or false statements made in the course of
carrying on the business;
      (3)   Any violation of this chapter;
      (4)   Conviction of any crime or misdemeanor involving moral turpitude;
and/or
      (5)   Conducting the business in an unlawful manner, or in such a manner
as to constitute a breach of the peace, or to constitute a menace to the
health, safety, or general welfare of the public.
   (B)   Notice of the hearing for revocation of a license shall be given in
writing, setting forth specifically the grounds of complaint and the time and
place of hearing. Such notice shall be mailed, postage prepaid, to the licensee
at his or her last known address at least three days prior to the date set for
hearing.
(Prior Code, § 15-12)

   Alcoholic beverages generally, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 12-1-101 et seq.
   Authority of town to regulate, prohibit, or license the sale of alcoholic
and/or malt beverages, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 12-2-201 et seq.

§ 112.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR. Any spirituous or fermented fluid intended for beverage
purposes containing more than 1% of alcohol by volume.
   BOTTLE CLUB. An operation or enterprise whereby space is given or rented to
any person upon the club premises for the keeping or storage of alcoholic or
malt beverages for consumption upon the club premises, or in other rooms nearby
used for consumption by the owner of the beverages or by the guests; the
income, profits, or fees of the operator of the BOTTLE CLUB being secured from
sales of furnishing mixes, ice, food, or glasses or from dues, charges,
contributions, membership cards, or assessments.
   CLUB. Any of the following organizations:
      (1)   A post, charter, camp, or other local unit composed only of
veterans and its duly organized auxiliary chartered by the Congress of the
United States for patriotic, fraternal, or benevolent purposes, and as the
owner, lessee, or occupant, operates an establishment for these purposes within
the town;
      (2)   A chapter, lodge, or other local unit of an American national
fraternal organization, and as the owner, lessee, or occupant, operates an
establishment for fraternal purposes within the town. As used in this
definition, an “American Fraternal Organization” means an organization actively
operating in not less than 36 states or having been in active, continuous
existence for not less than 20 years, but does not mean a college fraternity;
and
      (3)   A social club with more than 100 bona fide members who are
residents of the county; owning, maintaining, or operating club quarters;
incorporated and operating solely as a nonprofit corporation under the laws of
the state and qualified as a tax exempt organization under the Internal Revenue
Service Code; and having been continuously operating for a period of not less
than one year.
         (a)   The club shall have had during this one-year period a bona fide
membership paying dues of at least $25 per year as recorded by the secretary of
the club, quarterly meetings, and actively engaged membership carrying out the
objects of the club.
         (b)   A social club shall, upon applying for a license, file with the
Town Council and the State Liquor Commission a true copy of its bylaws and
shall further, upon applying for a renewal of its license, file with the Town
Council and the State Liquor Commission a detailed statement of its activities
during the preceding year which were undertaken or furthered in pursuit of the
objects of the club, together with an itemized statement of amounts expended
for such activities.
         (c)   Club members, at the time of application for the limited retail
liquor license, shall be in good standing by having paid at least one full year
in dues.
   LIMITED RETAIL LIQUOR LICENSE. The authority under which clubs shall be
permitted to sell alcoholic and malt beverages.
   MALT BEVERAGE. Any fluid intended for beverage purposes manufactured from
malt, wholly or in part, or from any substitute therefor containing more than
1% of alcohol by volume.
   RESTAURANT. Space in a building maintained, advertised, and held out to the
public as a place where individually priced meals are prepared and served
primarily for on-premises consumption, and where the primary source of revenue
from the operation is from the sale of food and not from the sale of alcoholic
or malt beverages. The building shall have a dining room or rooms, a kitchen,
and the number and kinds of employees necessary for the preparing, cooking, and
serving of meals in order to satisfy the licensing authority that the space is
intended for use as a FULL-SERVICE RESTAURANT.
      (1)   FULL-SERVICE RESTAURANT means a restaurant at which waiters or
waitresses deliver food and drink offered from a printed food menu to patrons
at tables or booths.
      (2)   The service of fry-only orders or such food and victuals as
sandwiches, hamburgers, or salads shall not be deemed a RESTAURANT for the
purposes of this chapter.
   RETAIL LIQUOR LICENSE. The authority under which a retailer shall be
permitted to sell alcoholic or malt beverages for use or consumption, but not
for resale.
   RETAILER. A person who sells or offers for sale any alcoholic or malt
beverage for use or consumption and not for resale.
   SELL or SALE. Offering for sale, trafficking in, bartering, delivering for
value, exchanging for goods, or acting in any way other than purely
gratuitously. Every delivery of any alcoholic or malt beverage made otherwise
than by gift shall constitute a SALE.
(Prior Code, § 3-1) (Ord. 224, passed - -)
Statutory reference:
   Definitions, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 12-1-101 and 12-4-302
§ 112.02 APPLICATIONS.
   (A)   Any person desiring a license under this chapter shall apply to the
Town Council for the same. Such application shall be made upon a form prepared
by the Attorney General and shall meet the requirements prescribed by
applicable state statutes.
   (B)   Each applicant shall, at the time of filing an application, make
payment within the office of the Clerk to the town in an amount sufficient to
cover the costs of publishing the required statutory notice.
(Prior Code, § 3-2)
Statutory reference:
   Duty of Attorney General of state to prepare and furnish forms, see Wyo.
Stat. § 12-4-701
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-101
§ 112.03 PROCEDURE UPON APPLICATION.
   The procedure to be followed when an application has been filed shall be as
prescribed by applicable state statutes.
(Prior Code, § 3-3)
§ 112.04 APPEAL OF DENIALS; RENEWAL APPLICANTS.
   No applicant for a license under this chapter shall have a right of appeal
from the decision of the governing body denying an application, except
applicants holding a license which is expiring and who are applying for a
renewal.
(Prior Code, § 3-4)
Statutory reference:
   Appeal procedures, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-104
§ 112.05 TERM.
   A license shall be a personal privilege and good for one year unless sooner
revoked.
(Prior Code, § 3-5)
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-106
§ 112.06 RENEWALS.
   All renewals for a license shall be controlled by applicable state statutes.
(Prior Code, § 3-6)
Statutory reference:
   Appeal procedures, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-104
§ 112.07 HOURS OF OPERATION.
   (A)   All persons licensed under this chapter shall:
      (1)   Close the dispensing room and cease the sale of both alcoholic and
malt liquors promptly at the hour of 2:00 a.m.;
      (2)   Clear the dispensing room of all persons other than employees by 2:
30 a.m.; and
      (3)   Keep the same closed until 6:00 a.m. the same day.
   (B)   The Town Council may designate a total of four days each calendar year
when all licensees may operate without restriction as to closing hours.
(Prior Code, § 3-7) (Ord. 170, passed - -2002) Penalty, see §
112.99
Statutory reference:
   Hours of operation, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-5-101
§ 112.08 PLACES OF SALE.
   (A)   Location.
      (1)   The place in which alcoholic and malt beverages are sold under a
retail liquor license shall be located in one room at such location upon the
premises for which the retail liquor license is issued, as shall be approved by
the Town Council; except that the licensee thereof may have and maintain one
additional dispensing room in the same building, which additional dispensing
room shall be operated under the authority of the same license as authorizes
the licensee to operate a dispensing room as herein provided. The additional
dispensing room may be on any floor.
      (2)   The licensee shall pay an additional fee equal to two-thirds of the
fee paid for the retail license for authority to operate an additional
dispensing room. Alcoholic and malt beverages secured in the licensed room by a
server may be served only in the building in which the licensed room is
located.
   (B)   Items which may be sold. Only alcoholic and malt beverages,
nonalcoholic beverages, food, and tobacco may be sold and served in the
licensed room.
   (C)   Inspections. All places licensed under this chapter shall be open for
inspection by any peace officer at any time, and such officers may enter
therein without a search warrant for such purpose.
   (D)   Separation of certain facilities. The retail licensee may maintain an
area for the sale of alcoholic and malt beverages for off-premises consumption,
which shall be located adjoining the facility for making sales for on-premises
consumption. The two facilities may be separated by a glass or other suitable
partition if they are connected by a doorway to permit persons to pass freely
between the two facilities. No addition fee shall be assessed against a
licensee who separates the licensed premised in this manner.
   (E)   Additional rooms for convention facilities. If the licensee is engaged
in a business operation with convention facilities, the licensee may maintain
more than one additional dispensing room for the same fee required by §
112.11(A)(1).
(Prior Code, § 3-8) Penalty, see §
112.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-5-201
§ 112.09 CLUBS.
   (A)   Clubs shall be licensed under a limited retail liquor license.
   (B)   No club holding a limited retail license shall sell alcoholic or malt
beverages for consumption anywhere except in the licensed premises and for
consumption by its members and guests. It shall be the duty and obligation of
each club to check and regulate the sale to members and guests, and any and all
alcoholic or malt beverages sold by it shall be consumed in the licensed
premises.
(Prior Code, § 3-9) Penalty, see §
112.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-301
§ 112.10 RESTAURANTS.
   (A)   Restaurants may be issued restaurant liquor licenses upon application
and upon meeting all requirements prescribed by state statutes. Any restaurant
license issued shall be subject to all restrictions imposed by state statutes.
   (B)   (1)   There shall be one bar-and-grill liquor license available within
the town.
         (a)   The applicant for a bar-and-grill liquor license shall provide a
valid food-service permit with the application.
         (b)   The license shall be allowed to be transferred, assigned, or
sold by the holder pending approval of the Council.
         (c)   An applicant for a bar-and-grill liquor license shall satisfy
the town that the primary source of revenue from the operation of the
restaurant to be licensed will be derived from food services and not from the
sale of alcoholic or malt beverages.
         (d)   When renewing a bar-and-grill liquor license, the town shall
condition renewal upon a requirement that not less than 60% of gross sales from
the preceding 12 months of operation of a licensed restaurant be derived from
food services.
         (e)   Upon application for license renewal, a license holder shall
submit an annual report to the town on the sales of the licensed restaurant.
The report shall contain the annual gross sales figures of the restaurant and
shall separate the gross sales figures into two categories:
            1.   Food-service sales; and
            2.   Alcoholic and malt beverage sales.
      (2)   The bar-and-grill licensee shall satisfy the appropriate
requirements for a restaurant liquor license and all other applicable liquor
statutes.
(Prior Code, § 3-11) (Ord. 224, passed - -; Ord. 275, 8-10-2016) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.11 FEES.
   (A)   (1)   Every person licensed as a retail liquor dealer shall pay an
annual fee of $1,000.
      (2)   Every club licensed under a limited retail liquor license shall pay
an annual fee of $1,000.
      (3)   Every restaurant licensed under a restaurant license shall pay an
annual fee of $1,000.
   (B)   Every applicant for a bar-and-grill liquor license shall pay a fee of
$1,500.
(Prior Code, § 3-12) (Ord. 224, passed - -)
Statutory reference:
   Disposition of fees received, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-105
   Fee for operation of additional dispensing room, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-5-201
   Minimum and maximum license fee which municipality may charge, see Wyo.
Stat. § 12-4-201
   Refund of fees, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-4-105
§ 112.12 PROHIBITED ACTS.
   (A)   (1)   Except as provided in division (E) below, restaurant and bar-
and-grill liquor licensees shall not sell alcoholic or malt beverages for
consumption off the premises owned or leased by the licensee.
      (2)   Except as provided in divisions (B) and (E) below, alcoholic or
malt beverages shall be served for on-premises consumption only in dining areas
which are adequately staffed and equipped for all food services offered by the
restaurant.
   (B)   Alcoholic and malt beverages shall be dispensed and prepared for
consumption in one room upon the licensed premises separated from the dining
area in which alcoholic and malt beverages may be served or restaurant liquor
license is operational.
      (1)   No consumption of alcoholic or malt beverages shall be permitted
within the dispensing room, nor shall any person other than employees over 18
years of age be permitted to enter the dispensing room.
      (2)   If a restaurant has a dispensing room separate from the dining area
which is licensed prior to February 1, 1979 for purposes of alcoholic or malt
beverage sales and consumption, the restaurant may dispense alcoholic or malt
beverages in the separate dispensing room under a restaurant liquor license,
and any person over 18 years of age is permitted to enter the separate
dispensing room.
   (C)   All sales of alcoholic and malt beverages authorized by a restaurant
or bar-and-grill liquor license shall cease at the time food sales and services
cease, or at the hours specified by Wyo. Stat. § 12-5-101(a) if food sales and
services extend beyond the hours specified therein.
   (D)   No restaurant or bar-and-grill liquor licensee shall promote the
restaurant or bar-and-grill as a bar-and-lounge, nor shall the licensee compete
with a retail liquor licensee in activities other than dinner functions
including but not limited to dances, receptions, and other social gatherings.
   (E)   A restaurant or bar-and-grill liquor licensee may permit a patron to
remove one unsealed bottle of wine for off-premises consumption provided that
the patron has purchased a full-course meal and consumed a portion of the
bottle of wine with the meal on the restaurant premises.
      (1)   For purposes of this division (E), the term FULL-COURSE MEAL shall
mean a diversified selection of food which is ordinarily consumed with the use
of tableware and cannot conveniently be consumed while standing or walking.
      (2)   A partially consumed bottle of wine that is to be removed from the
premises pursuant to this division (E) shall be securely sealed by the licensee
or an agent of the licensee and placed in a tamper-proof transparent bag, which
shall also be securely sealed prior to removal from the premises, so that it is
visibly apparent that the resealed bottle of wine has not been tampered with.
The licensee or agent of the licensee shall provide a dated receipt for the
bottle of wine to the patron.
(Prior Code, § 3-13) (Ord. 224, passed - -) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.13 OPEN CONTAINERS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to have in his or her possession or
automobile, any open container which contains any alcoholic beverage in any
public place including streets within the town, except those establishments
licensed to serve alcoholic beverages. The Town Council, by resolution from
time to time, can designate places and times during which this section shall
not apply.
(Prior Code, § 3-14) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.14 SERVER TRAINING.
   (A)   Alcohol server training required. All holders of any type of liquor
license issued by the town shall require all employees that have been employed
for at least 60 days to attend alcohol server training approved or recognized
by the State Liquor Division.
      (1)   The employees shall attend and complete said alcohol server
training within 90 days of employment.
      (2)   Alcohol server training will be renewed every three years.
(Prior Code, § 3-17)
   (B)   Interim training. All holders of any type of liquor license issued by
the town shall have new employees trained by a current employee whom has
previously attended and completed an alcohol server training class until said
new employee complies with division (A) above.
(Prior Code, § 3-18)
   (C)   Record of training due with renewal. Annually, with the liquor license
holder’s application for a new license, the license holder shall provide a list
of all current employees together with the date of hire, date of alcohol server
training, or date of scheduled alcohol server training.
(Prior Code, § 3-20)
(Ord. 255, passed 6-11-2014) Penalty, see §
112.99
Editor’s note:
   Current website for tips training, see http://gettips.com
§ 112.15 VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   In addition to any other penalty which may be imposed pursuant to the
provisions of this code or state law, the violation of any provision of this
chapter by any licensee may be sufficient cause for the suspension or
revocation of the license.
   (B)   An action to revoke any license or permit for the reason that the
license was unlawfully granted; or that the licensee or permittee was not
entitled to receive the same; or that the place of sale in which alcoholic or
malt liquors are sold under such license is not a proper place for sale of the
same; or that the licensee or permittee is permitting activities in such place
of sale forbidden by ordinance or which are detrimental to the welfare, health,
morals, and safety of the people of the town; or that the licensee or permittee
has violated any of the provisions of this chapter, may be brought in the name
of the town. Such action shall be civil in nature and commenced as are civil
actions.
(Prior Code, § 3-16)
Statutory reference:
   Procedures to revoke or suspend licenses, see Wyo. Stat. § 12-7-101
§ 112.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   The violation of any provision of this chapter shall be a misdemeanor.
(Prior Code, § 3-15)
   (B)   All holders of any type of liquor license issued by the town that
fails to comply with §
112.14(A) or (B) shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and fined in an amount
not to exceed $750 plus court costs.
(Prior Code, § 3-19) (Ord. 255, passed 6-11-2014)

§ 113.01 CIRCUSES, EXHIBITIONS, AND THE LIKE.
   (A)   No person shall conduct or manage any caravan, circus, street show,
merry-go-round, or other exhibition, show, or amusement; or shall exhibit any
natural or artificial curiosities, or panoramic, or other show or device of any
kind; or shall give any concert or musical entertainment without first
obtaining a license therefor.
(Prior Code, § 7-7)
   (B)   (1)   Any person conducting or managing any caravan, circus, street
show, merry-go-round, or other exhibition, show, or amusement; or who shall
exhibit any natural or artificial curiosities, or panoramic, or other show or
device of any kind; or who shall give any concert or musical entertainment,
shall make application to the office of the Clerk for a license.
      (2)   Every application shall set forth the name of the applicant and
qualifications, together with such other information as may be required by the
town.
(Prior Code, § 7-8)
   (C)   Each application shall be accompanied by a fee as established by the
governing body.
(Prior Code, § 7-9)
Penalty, see §
113.99
§ 113.02 FIREWORKS.
   (A)   No person shall offer for sale, sell, or give away any fireworks
within the town without first obtaining a license therefor.
   (B)   Any person applying for a license to offer for sale, sell, or give
away fireworks shall file with the office of the Clerk an application in
writing which shall contain the following information:
      (1)   The name of applicant;
      (2)   The permanent home address and local address of the applicant and
the representative thereof filing the application;
      (3)   A description of the nature of the business to be conducted; and
      (4)   The place where the sale of fireworks will take place, which place
must be approved by the governing body prior to issuance of the license.
   (C)   (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to sell any fireworks
without a license, or to discharge or detonate any fireworks within the town
except during the time and in a place the Town Council designates.
      (2)   Persons may discharge fireworks on July 3, 4, and 5 only.
   (D)   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons to discharge any
fireworks south of the Burlington Northern Railroad tracks from the hours of 7:
00 p.m. to 11:00 p.m. on July 4 on the years that an organized display is
presented for the general public.
   (E)   FIREWORKS shall mean any article, device, or substance prepared for
the primary purpose of producing a visual or auditory sensation by combustion,
explosion, deflagration, or detonation, including without limitation: articles
and devices commonly known and used as fireworks, toy cannons or toy canes in
which explosives are used, blank cartridges, firecrackers, torpedoes,
skyrockets, and Roman candles. FIREWORKS shall not include any item which may
be sold or offered for sale under 15 U.S.C. § 1261, 21 U.S.C. § 371, and 16
C.F.R. part 1507.
(Prior Code, § 7-10) (Ord. 167, passed 10-1-1994) Penalty, see §
113.99
§ 113.03 MARTIAL ARTS PARAPHERNALIA.
   It is unlawful for any person or legal entity to sell any type of martial
arts paraphernalia within the town.
(Ord. 120, passed 3-22-1985) Penalty, see §
113.99
§ 113.04 BILLIARDS; BOWLING; POOL HALLS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any firm, corporation, association, or person
to maintain or operate any public pool hall, billiard hall, or bowling alley
for pay in the town without first having procured from the town a license as
hereinafter provided.
   (B)   Any person desiring to open, maintain, and operate any public pool
hall, billiard hall, or bowling alley for pay in the town shall file with the
office of the Clerk a written application for a license to maintain and operate
such hall, shall designate the location by lot and block where such hall is to
be maintained and operated, and shall designate the number of tables or alleys
to be operated therein.
      (1)   The application so filed with the Town Council at its regular
meeting next ensuing, and if approved by the Council, shall direct the office
of the Clerk to issue a license to such person so applying upon the payment by
such person to the Town Treasurer of a license fee of $5 per quarter for each
table or alley to be used or operated in such hall.
      (2)   It shall be a condition of said license that it may be revoked at
any time by the Mayor and Town Council for a violation of any of the provisions
of this section; and that upon such revocation, the unearned portion of the
license fee provided in division (B)(1) above shall be deemed and held to be
forfeited to the town.
   (C)   The town, through its Mayor and Town Council, reserves the right under
the terms of the provisions of this section, in passing upon an application for
any such license, to consider the character and responsibility of the applicant
and any objections that may exist against the granting of the license at the
proposed location.
   (D)   Any license granted under the provisions of this section shall extend
for the year ending on May 1 following the date thereof, unless revoked prior
thereto as provided in division (B)(2) above; provided that the license fee of
$5 per table shall be payable quarterly in advance.
   (E)   In case the licensee desires to operate additional tables or alleys,
he or she shall present to the Mayor and Town Council in application therefor,
setting out the date of his or her license, the place of his or her location,
the number of tables or alleys operated therein, and the number of additional
tables or alleys which he or she desires to operate in said place, and the
Mayor and Town Council shall have the power to direct and authorize the
issuance of a further license, for the unexpired portion of the year, for the
operation of such additional tables or alleys upon the payment of the required
license fee as provided in division (B)(1) above.
   (F)   No license shall be issued to any firm, corporation, or to any person
as agent or manager for any other person firm or corporation, and no license
shall be transferrable by sale, assignment, or in any other manner.
   (G)   It shall be unlawful for any person maintaining and operating any pool
hall, billiard hall, or bowling alley for pay in the town to permit any person
under 18 years age to frequent, remain in, or patronize any such hall or alley.
   (H)   Any person maintaining and operating any public pool hall, billiard
hall, or bowling alley for pay in the town may require any person suspected by
him or her to be under 18 years of age, before permitting such person to
frequent and remain in or patronize such hall or alley, to sign a statement in
writing of his or her true age and name.
   (I)   It shall be unlawful for any person under 18 years of age to frequent
or remain in any public pool hall, billiard hall, or bowling alley maintained
and operated for pay in the town, or to play in any such hall or alley.
   (J)   It shall be unlawful to permit therein any disturbance of the peace,
drunkenness, fighting, the presence of or the drinking of any intoxicating
liquor, any lewd or obscene pictures or practices, and/or gambling of any
nature or character.
(Ord. 22, passed 4-7-1930) Penalty, see §
113.99
§ 113.05 DANCES.
   (A)   All public dances shall close not later than 1:00 a.m.
   (B)   Any person or persons operating a dance in the town must first obtain
from within the office of the Town Clerk a dance license (good for one night
only).
(Ord. 28, passed 5-1-1933; Ord. 41, passed 7-29-1938; Ord. 48, passed 6-5-1944)
Penalty, see §
113.99
§ 113.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person or legal entity violating §
113.03 shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof,
shall be fined a sum not to exceed $100.
   (C)   (1)   Any person maintaining or operating any public pool hall,
billiard hall, or bowling alley for pay in the town without first having
applied to the Mayor and Town Council for a license therefor, and without
having procured such license as provided in §
113.04, shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof
before a Law Enforcement Officer of the town, shall be fined in a sum of not
more than $750 plus costs of prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state
or by rules of the Supreme Court. The operation of any such public pool hall,
billiard hall, or bowling alley upon any one day shall be deemed a separate
offense.
      (2)   Any such person violating the provisions of §
113.04(G) shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction before a
Law Enforcement Officer of the town, shall be fined in a sum of not more than
$750 plus costs of prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state or by
rules of the Supreme Court.
      (3)   Any person under 18 years of age violating the provisions of §
113.04(I) shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof
before a Law Enforcement Officer of the town, shall be fined a sum of not more
than $750 plus costs of prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state or by
rules of the Supreme Court.
      (4)   Any person under 18 years of age who shall, for the purpose of
securing permission to frequent, remain, in or play in any such hall or alley,
falsely represent to the manager or any employee thereof that his or her age is
18 years or more, shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction
thereof before a Law Enforcement Officer of the town, shall be fined a sum of
not more than $750 plus costs of prosecution as set forth by the laws of the
state or by rules of the Supreme Court.
      (5)   Any person violating the provisions of §
113.04(J) shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof
before a Law Enforcement Officer of the town, shall be fined a sum of not more
than $750 plus costs of prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state or by
rules of the Supreme Court.
   (D)   (1)   Violation of any of the provisions of §
113.05 shall constitute a misdemeanor, and any person convicted of such
violation and misdemeanor shall be punished by a fine of a sum of not more than
$750 plus costs of prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state or by
rules of the Supreme Court.
      (2)   Should there be a violation of the provisions of §
113.05 by a firm or partnership, each member or copartner of such firm or
partnership shall be deemed guilty as an individual.
(Ord. 22, passed 4-8-1930; Ord. 28, passed 5-1-1933; Ord. 120, passed 3-22-
1985; Ord. 127, passed 8-4-1987)

130.99   Penalty
**** OFFENSES AGAINST PEACE AND SAFETY ****
§ 130.01 AIDING, ABETTING, AND ATTEMPTING.
   (A)   No person shall aid, abet, or assist another person in an act that is
a violation of this code.
   (B)   Any person attempting any act which is prohibited by this code shall
be guilty of a misdemeanor and may be punished in the same manner as if the
attempted act was actually committed.
(Prior Code, § 10-1) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.02 ASSAULT.
   A person is guilty of simple assault if, having the present ability to do
so, he or she unlawfully attempts to cause bodily injury to another.
(Prior Code, § 10-2) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 6-2-501
§ 130.03 BARRICADES; EXCAVATIONS.
   No person shall leave any excavation in an open or uncovered condition, or
in any such condition as may cause danger to life or property upon any premises
of which such person is the owner, agent, or other representative, unless the
ground upon which excavation is situated is enclosed by substantial barricades.
(Prior Code, § 10-3) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.04 BATTERY.
   (A)   A person is guilty of simple assault if, having the present ability to
do so, he or she unlawfully attempts to cause bodily injury to another.
   (B)   A person is guilty of battery if he or she intentionally, knowingly,
or recklessly causes bodily injury to another person by use of physical force.
   (C)   A person is guilty of unlawful contact if he or she:
      (1)   Touches another person in a rude, insolent, or angry manner without
intentionally using sufficient physical force to cause bodily injury to
another; or
      (2)   Recklessly causes bodily injury to another person.
(Wyo. Stat. § 6-2-501) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.05 COIN MACHINES.
   No person shall break, open, or enter a coin machine with intent to commit
larceny.
(Prior Code, § 10-5) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.06 CRIMINAL ENTRY; TRESPASS.
   (A)   No person shall, without authority, knowingly enter a building,
occupied structure, vehicle, or cargo portion of a truck or trailer, or a
separately secured or occupied portion of those enclosures.
(Prior Code, § 10-6)
   (B)   No person shall enter or remain on or in the land or premises of
another person knowing he or she is not authorized to do so or after being
notified to depart or to not trespass. For the purposes of this division (B),
notice is given by:
      (1)   Personal communication to the person by the owner or occupant, his
or her agent, or by a peace officer; or
      (2)   Posting of signs reasonably likely to come to the attention of
intruders.
(Prior Code, § 10-7)
Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 6-3-302 and 6-3-303
§ 130.07 DESTRUCTION OF PROPERTY.
   A person is guilty of property destruction and defacement if he or she
knowingly defaces, injures, or destroys the property of another without the
owner’s consent.
(Prior Code, § 10-9) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 6-3-201
§ 130.08 DISORDERLY CONDUCT.
   No person shall:
   (A)   Commit an act in a violent and tumultuous manner toward another
whereby the property of any person is placed in danger of being destroyed or
damaged;
   (B)   Cause, provoke, or engage in any fight, brawl, or riotous conduct;
   (C)   Incite, attempt to incite, or be involved in attempting to incite a
riot. For the purposes of this division (C), the term RIOT shall mean a
tumultuous disturbance of the peace by persons assembled and acting with a
common intent to the terror of the people of the town, either in executing a
lawful enterprise in a violent or turbulent manner or in executing an unlawful
enterprise in a violent or turbulent manner;
   (D)   Obstruct, whether singly or together with other persons, the flow of
vehicular or pedestrian traffic or refuse to clear such public way when ordered
to do so by the police or other lawful authority known to be such;
   (E)   Damage, befoul, or disturb public property or the property of another
so as to create a hazardous, unhealthy, or physically offensive condition;
   (F)   Fail to obey a lawful order to disperse by a police officer, when know
to be such an official, when one or more persons are committing acts of
disorderly conduct in the immediate vicinity and the public health and safety
imminently threatened;
   (G)   Resist or obstruct the performance of duties by police or any other
authorized official of the town when known to be such an official;
   (H)   Commit an act in a violent and tumultuous manner toward another
whereby that other is placed in danger of his or her life, limb, or health;
   (I)   Interfere with another’s pursuit of a lawful occupation by acts of
violence;
   (J)   Use abusive, profane, or obscene language or gestures in any public
place; and/or
   (K)   Interrupt or disturb the peace of the town or any of its inhabitants:
      (1)   By any loud talking;
      (2)   By threatening abusive, profane, or obscene language or violent
actions; or
      (3)   By any other rude behavior.
(Prior Code, § 10-10) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to prohibit profane, and the like, language, see Wyo.
Stat. § 15-1-103
   Authority of town to restrain, prohibit, and the like, disorderly conduct,
see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103
   Authority of town to suppress, and the like, riots, and the like, see Wyo.
Stat. § 15-1-103
   Breach of peace, see Wyo. Stat. § 6-6-102
   Offenses against public peace generally, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 6-6-101 to 6-6-
307
§ 130.09 DISTURBING GATHERINGS.
   (A)   No person shall disturb any lawful assemblage of people by rude,
boisterous, or indecent behavior or otherwise.
   (B)   No person shall annoy or disturb any congregation or assembly,
gathered together for religious worship, by making a noise or by rude or
indecent behavior or profane discourse within the place wherein such
congregation or assembly is gathered together or so near the same as to be
heard by or tending to disturb the persons so engaged or assembled.
(Prior Code, § 10-11) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to prevent and the like, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103
   Authority of town to suppress disorderly conduct, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103
§ 130.10 FILING FALSE REPORTS OF CRIME.
   No person shall knowingly make or file any false, misleading, or unfounded
statement or report concerning any emergency or the commission or alleged
commission of any crime.
(Prior Code, § 10-12) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.11 DISCHARGING FIREARMS.
   No person shall carry a concealed deadly weapon; fire or discharge any
cannon, gun, fowling piece, pistol, or firearm of any description; or fire,
explode, or set off any squib, cracker, or other thing containing powder or
other combustible or explosive material without written permission from the
Town Council, which permission shall limit the time of such firing and shall be
subject to revocation by the Town Council at any time after being granted.
(Prior Code, § 10-13) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Carrying weapons, see Wyo. Stat. § 6-8-104
§ 130.12 GLUE SNIFFING AND OTHER INHALANTS.
   (A)   No person shall, for the purpose of causing or so as to result in a
condition of intoxication, euphoria, excitement, exhilaration, stupefaction, or
dulling of the senses or nervous system, intentionally smell or inhale the
fumes from any glue or similar toxic vapor or consume or use any otherwise
legal substance for a purpose not in accordance with its legitimate use or
purpose as in generally known, used, and advertised; provided that this section
shall not apply to the following:
      (1)   The inhalation of anesthesia for medical or dental purposes when
administered or directed by a licensed physician or dentist; or
      (2)   The use of tobaccos.
   (B)   No person shall, for the purpose of violating or aiding another to
violate any provisions of this section, intentionally possess, buy, sell,
transfer possession or receive possession of any substances herein prohibited.
(Prior Code, § 10-18) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.13 INTERFERING WITH POLICE; RESISTING ARREST.
   (A)   No person shall resist any police officer or member of the Police
Department in the discharge of any duty or shall in any way interfere with or
hinder or prevent an officer from discharging the duties as such officer.
   (B)   No person shall in any manner assist any person in custody of any
police officer to escape or attempt to escape from such custody or shall rescue
or attempt to rescue any person so in custody.
(Prior Code, § 10-19) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Interference with officer, see Wyo. Stat. § 6-5-204
§ 130.14 INTERFERING WITH MUNICIPAL EMPLOYEES.
   No person shall:
   (A)   Interfere in any way with any employee of the town in the performance
of his or her work;
   (B)   Displace any stakes or landmarks deposited or installed by any
employee;
   (C)   In any way molest any tools, instruments, or equipment of any
employee; nor
   (D)   In any way molest any tools, instruments, or equipment used by such
employee in the duties assigned to him or her.
(Prior Code, § 10-21) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.15 NARCOTIC PARAPHERNALIA.
   No person shall possess any device, contrivance, instrument, or
paraphernalia designed or used for smoking a controlled substance or possess
any hypodermic needle or syringe or any other device, contrivance, instrument,
or paraphernalia designed or used for injecting a controlled substance, except
for the following persons:
   (A)   Licensed drug manufacturers, wholesale drug jobbers, pharmacists,
physicians, dentists, podiatrists, veterinarians, and nurses;
   (B)   Hospital research, teaching, and clinical laboratory personnel;
   (C)   Funeral directors and embalmers;
   (D)   Persons specifically authorized by a licensed physician, dentist, or
podiatrist to use a hypodermic needle or syringe for medical treatment
purposes, while so using the needle or syringe; and/or
   (E)   Persons using a hypodermic needle or syringe for the treatment of
livestock.
(Prior Code, § 10-22) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Narcotics generally, see Controlled Substances Act, Wyo. Stat. §§ 35-7-1001
to 35-7-1057
§ 130.16 OPEN BURNING.
   (A)   No person shall, without permission of the owner and acting with
criminal negligence, set fire to any grounds or to anything on the grounds
which is the property of another, or allow a fire to pass from the owner’s
grounds to the injury or destruction of any property of another.
(Prior Code, § 10-23)
   (B)   (1)   The open burning of garbage, refuse, and rubbish is prohibited
within the town limits.
      (2)   Notwithstanding the provisions of division (B)(1) above, the
controlled open burning of bothersome weeds and leaves may be permitted.
      (3)   A separate container will be provided upon request for stove ashes,
which will not be picked up unless cool for five days.
      (4)   Incinerators shall not be allowed except those incinerators in
place prior to January 21, 1985. Only paper, weeds, and leaves may be burned in
said incinerators.
(Ord. 119, passed 1-21-1985; Ord. 128, passed 9-8-1987) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.17 FAILURE TO EXTINGUISH/CONTAIN FIRE.
   No person shall light a fire and leave the vicinity of the fire without
extinguishing it or containing it so it does not spread and is not likely to
spread.
(Prior Code, § 10-24) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.18 THROWING MISSILES.
   No person shall throw any missile at or against any house, building,
vehicle, or at or toward any person or persons.
(Prior Code, § 10-25) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.19 OVERNIGHT CAMPING.
   (A)   No person shall remain overnight in any tent, sleeping bag, vehicle,
camper, or in any other manner whatsoever on any property belonging to the town
except in those areas specifically designated for overnight camping.
   (B)   No person shall remain in any area specifically designated for
overnight camping for a period in excess of three days in any 30-day period.
(Prior Code, § 10-29) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.20 REFRIGERATORS, ICEBOXES, AND THE LIKE.
   No person shall leave or permit to remain outside of any dwelling, building,
or other structure or within any unoccupied or abandoned building, dwelling, or
other structure under his or her control in a place accessible to children: any
abandoned, unattended, or discarded icebox, refrigerator, or other container
which has an airtight door or lid, snap lock, or other locking device which may
not be released from the inside, without first removing the door or lid, snap
lock, or other locking device from such icebox, refrigerator, or container.
(Prior Code, § 10-32) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.21 SHOPLIFTING.
   No person shall willfully conceal or take possession of any goods offered
for sale by wholesale or retail stores or other mercantile establishments
without the knowledge or consent of the owner thereof and with intent to
convert the goods to his or her own use without paying the purchase price.
(Prior Code, § 10-33) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 6-3-404
§ 130.22 UNLAWFUL ACTS THROUGH TELEPHONES.
   No person shall:
   (A)   Telephone another and use obscene, lewd, or profane language or
suggest a lewd or lascivious act with intent to terrify, intimidate, threaten,
harass, annoy, or offend;
   (B)   By repeated anonymous telephone calls, disturb the peace, quiet, or
privacy of persons where the calls were received; and/or
   (C)   Telephone another and threaten to inflict injury or physical harm to
the person or property of any person.
(Prior Code, § 10-34) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.23 THEFT OF PROPERTY.
   No person shall steal, take and carry, or lead or drive away property of
another with intent to deprive the owner or lawful possessor of the property.
(Prior Code, § 10-35) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.24 THEFT OF SERVICES.
   (A)   No person shall:
      (1)   Obtain services which he or she knows are available only for
compensation without paying for the services; or
      (2)   Tamper or otherwise interfere with or connect to by any means,
whether mechanical, electrical, acoustical, or otherwise: any cables, wires, or
other devices used for distribution of service.
   (B)   As used in this section, SERVICES includes but is not limited to
electric, telephone, cable television, gas, water, or sewer services.
(Prior Code, § 10-36) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.25 UNLAWFUL POSTING.
   No person shall paint, print, paste, stencil, or otherwise mark upon or in
any manner place upon or affix to any building, fence, wall, tree, or vehicle
without the consent of the owner thereof: any word, letter, character, figure,
sentence, or device; or any handbill or notice.
(Prior Code, § 10-37) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.26 NOISE.
   (A)   No person shall act in such a loud or disorderly manner as to disturb
the general peace and order of this town regardless if the behavior is being
committed on public or private property. Nor shall any person operate or use
any item or equipment which causes the same results.
   (B)   The owner or immediate caretaker of any residence or place in which
the offense in division (A) above has been committed will be held responsible
for enforcement of this section after official warning has been given. Under
another disturbance, the owner or immediate caretaker shall be guilty of this
section regardless if he or she is participating in the illegal behavior or
not.
   (C)   No person shall keep or allow any animal upon one’s premises to in
such manner as to be offensive or annoying to others. It will be the
responsibility of the owner to control and prevent such animals from violation
of this division regardless if the owner is at home at the time of the
violation or not.
   (D)   The provisions of this section shall be in effect during all hours of
the day and night.
   (E)   For the purpose of this section, a person is considered to be in
violation of this section only if:
      (1)   A complaint is filed with the Police Department and the responding
officer feels such conduct is indeed unreasonable for the circumstances; or
      (2)   A police officer has not received a complaint yet feels the noise
or behavior is to an extend to disturb the peace of the town or disturb others
under the circumstances.
   (F)   Violation of any division or divisions of this section shall be
considered a misdemeanor.
(Prior Code, § 10-40) (Ord. 179, passed 10-7-1997) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.27 DISORDERLY GATHERINGS.
   (A)   No person who owns, rents, leases, subleases, occupies, possesses,
trespasses, or has control of any residence, hotel or motel room, public or
private building, premises or any portion thereof, shall: sponsor, conduct,
host, invite, or permit a social gather or party on said premises which is or
becomes a public nuisance as defined below, where such nuisance is either the
intentional result of or could be reasonably anticipated by the person or
persons have control of the premises. “Reasonably anticipated” shall be
adjudicated using a reasonable person standard.
   (B)   A NUISANCE GATHERING OR PARTY shall be defined as a social gathering
or party occurring on any premises within the town which, by reason of the
conduct of the persons in attendance, results in any one or more of the
following acts, conditions, events, or violations that occur at the premises of
said social gathering or party or on neighboring public or private property:
      (1)   Illegal open container;
      (2)   Urination outside on the premises or on the property of another;
      (3)   Unlawful sale, furnishing, dispensing, consumption, or possession
of alcohol or intoxicating liquor;
      (4)   Unlawful use of any controlled substance;
      (5)   Indecent exposure;
      (6)   Assault, battery, or fighting;
      (7)   Vandalism;
      (8)   Use of profane language or gesturing;
      (9)   Obstructing a peace officer;
      (10)   Resisting arrest;
      (11)   Unlawful parking;
      (12)   Disturbing the peace; and/or
      (13)   Any other unlawful act or action.
   (C)   All persons present at a nuisance gather or party shall cease upon the
order of a law enforcement officer and shall leave the premises immediately,
excluding the residents of said premises.
(Ord. 230, passed - -) Penalty, see §
130.99
**** OFFENSES AGAINST PUBLIC MORALS ****
§ 130.40 GAMBLING.
   (A)   (1)   GAMBLING means risking any property for gain contingent in whole
or in part upon lot, chance, the operation of a gambling device, or the
happening or outcome of an event (including a sporting event) over which the
person taking a risk has no control, but does not include:
         (a)   Bona fide contests of skill, speed, strength, or endurance in
which awards are made only to entrants or the owners of entries;
         (b)   Bona fide business transactions which are valid under the law of
contracts;
         (c)   Other acts or transactions now or hereafter expressly authorized
by law;
         (d)   Raffles or bingos conducted or pull tables sold by charitable or
nonprofit organizations where the tickets for the raffle or bingo are sold only
in this state and the pull tabs are sold only on the premises owned or occupied
by the charitable or nonprofit organization;
         (e)   Any game, wager, or transaction which is incidental to a bona
fide social relationship, is participated in by natural persons only, and in
which no person is participating (directly or indirectly) in professional
gambling; or
         (f)   Calcutta wagering on contests or events conducted by bona fide
nationally chartered veterans’, religious, charitable, educational, or
fraternal organizations or nonprofit, local civic, or service clubs organized
or incorporated under the laws of this state, provided that:
            1.   The contest or event is conducted solely in this state;
            2.   Any rules affecting the contest or requirements for
participants are clearly posted;
            3.   The total prizes or prize money paid out in any one contest or
event does not exceed 90% of the total wagers;
            4.   A minimum of 10% of the total wagers of each contest or event
is donated within one year by the sponsoring organization to a bona fide
charitable or benevolent purpose;
            5.   No separate organization or professional person is employed to
conduct the contest or event or assist therein; and/or
            6.   The sponsoring organization, before conducting the contest or
event, gives 30 days’ written notice of the time and place thereof to the
governing body of the county or municipality in which it intends to conduct the
contest or event, and the governing body does not pass a resolution objecting
thereto.
         (g)   The display or private use of antique gambling devices in the
owner’s residence.
      (2)   No person shall engage in gambling.
(Prior Code, § 10-15)
   (B)   No person shall knowingly permit gambling in or upon the premises
owned, managed, or controlled by him or her.
(Prior Code, § 10-16)
   (C)   It shall be the duty of the police to seize any games, devices, cards,
tools, and implements or other articles or things designed for the purpose of
gambling, employed or used by or in the possession of parties arrested for
gambling, and upon conviction of the parties so arrested and upon order of the
judge, to confiscate and destroy the same.
(Prior Code, § 10-17)
Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to restrain, prohibit, and the like, gambling, see Wyo.
Stat. § 15-1-103
   Gambling generally, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 6-7-101 to 6-9-104
§ 130.41 OBSCENITY.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      AVAILABLE TO THE PUBLIC. The matter or performance may be purchased or
attended on a subscription basis, on a membership-fee arrangement, or for a
separate fee for each item or performance.
      DISSEMINATE. To transfer possession of, with or without consideration.
      KNOWINGLY. Being aware of the character and the content of the material.
      MATERIAL. Any book, magazine, newspaper, or other printed or written
material; or any picture, drawing, photograph, motion picture, or other
pictorial representation; or any statue or other figure; or any recording,
transcription, or mechanical, chemical or electrical reproduction or any other
articles, equipment, or machines.
      NUDITY. The showing of the human male or female genitals or pubic area
with less than a fully opaque covering, or the depiction of covered male
genital in a discernible turgid state.
      OBSCENE. To the average person applying contemporary community standards:
         (a)   The predominant appeal of the matter taken as a whole is to
prurient interest; i.e., a shameful or morbid interest in sexual conduct,
nudity, or excretion;
         (b)   The matter depicts or describes in a patently offensive manner
sexual conduct; and
         (c)   The work, taken as a whole, lacks serious literary, artistic,
political, or scientific value.
      PERFORMANCE. Any preview, play, show, skit, film, dance, or other
exhibition performed before an audience.
      PROMOTE. To cause, permit, procure, counsel, or assist.
      SERVICE TO PATRONS. The provision of service to paying guests in
establishments providing food and beverages including but not limited to
hosting, hat checking, cooking, bartending, serving, table setting and
cleaning, waitering, and entertaining.
(Prior Code, § 10-26)
   (B)   No person shall:
      (1)   Knowingly disseminate, distribute, or make available to the public
any obscene material;
      (2)   Knowingly engage or participate in any obscene performance made
available to the public;
      (3)   Knowingly engage in commerce for commercial gain with materials
depicting and describing explicit sexual conduct, nudity, or excretion
utilizing displays, circulars, advertisements, and other public sales efforts
that promote such commerce primarily on the basis of the prurient appeal;
      (4)   Provide service to patrons in such a manner as to expose to public
view:
         (a)   His or her genitals, public hair, buttocks, perineum, anal
region, or pubic hair region;
         (b)   Any device, costume, or covering which gives the appearance of
or simulates the genitals, pubic hair, buttocks, perineum, anal region, or
pubic hair region; or
         (c)   Any portion of the female breast at or below the areola thereof.
      (5)   Appear in a state of nudity in any public place; or
      (6)   Knowingly promote the commission of any of the above listed
unlawful acts.
(Prior Code, § 10-27)
   (C)   (1)   Expert affirmative evidence that the materials or activities are
obscene is not required when the materials or activities themselves are
presented as evidence.
      (2)   It shall be an affirmative defense in any prosecution under
division (B) above that allegedly obscene material was disseminated or
presented for a bona fide scientific, medical, educational, governmental, or
judicial purpose of a physician, psychologist, teacher, clergyperson,
prosecutor, or judge.
(Prior Code, § 10-28)
Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Authority of town to prohibit obscene language, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-103
   Obscenity generally, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 6-4-301 to 6-4-302
§ 130.42 PROSTITUTION.
   No person shall:
   (A)   Keep, set up, maintain, or operate any place, structure, or building
or conveyance for the purpose of prostitution; or with knowledge or reasonable
cause to know that the same is or is to be used for such purpose; or receive or
offer to agree to receive any person in any place, structure, or building or
conveyance for the purpose of prostitution; or permit any person to remain
therein for such purpose;
   (B)   Direct, take, transport, or offer or agree to take or transport any
person to any place, structure, or building or to any other person with
knowledge or reasonable cause to know that the purpose of such directing,
taking, or transporting is prostitution;
   (C)   Reside in, enter, or remain in any place, structure, or building or
conveyance for the purpose of prostitution;
   (D)   Engage in or solicit prostitution or aid or abet prostitution by
solicitation or by any means whatsoever; and/or
   (E)   (1)   Run or maintain a disorderly house within the town.
      (2)   For the purpose of this section, a DISORDERLY HOUSE shall include
any place kept in such a manner as to disturb, annoy, or scandalize the public
generally or persons within the particular neighborhood; and any place kept for
the purpose of public resort with drunkards or public or private resort with
prostitutes, or other idle or vicious people, any place of public resort where
illegal practices are habitually carried on to the corruption of the public
morals or safety.
(Prior Code, § 10-30) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. § 6-4-101
§ 130.43 PUBLIC DRUNKENNESS.
   No person shall be drunk or in a state of intoxication in any public place
within the town.
(Prior Code, § 10-31) Penalty, see §
130.99

   Power of towns to regulate, and the like, intoxication, see Wyo. Stat. § 15-
1-103
§ 130.44 WINDOW PEEPING.
   No person shall look, peer, or peep into any residential home window which
is not his or her home.
(Prior Code, § 10-38) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.45 URINATING/DEFECATING IN PUBLIC.
   A person is guilty of disorderly conduct if he or she urinates or defecates
on public or private property in the view of the public.
(Prior Code, § 10-41) (Ord. passed 9-11-2013) Penalty, see §
130.99
**** OFFENSES CONCERNING MINORS ****
§ 130.60 CURFEW.
   (A)   No minor shall remain, idle, wander, stroll, or play in any public
place either on foot or to cruise about without a set destination in any
vehicle in, about, or upon any place in the town between the hours of 9:30 p.m.
and 5:00 a.m. unless:
      (1)   Accompanied by a parent, guardian, custodian, or other adult person
having custody or control of such minor or unless the minor is on an emergency
errand or specific business or activity directed or permitted by his or her
parent, guardian, or other adult person having the care and custody of the
minor; or
      (2)   Where the presence of such minor is connected with or required by
some legitimate employment, trade, profession, or occupation.
   (B)   For purposes of this section, any person who has not attained the age
of 18 years is declared to be a MINOR.
(Prior Code, § 10-8) (Ord. 189, passed 12-4-2001) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.61 TOBACCO.
   (A)   No person shall sell, offer for sale, give away, or deliver tobacco
products to any person under the age of 18 years.
   (B)   No person under the age of 18 years shall purchase tobacco products,
misrepresent his or her identity or age, or use any false or altered
identification for the purpose of purchasing tobacco products.
   (C)   It is unlawful for any person under the age of 18 years to possess or
use any tobacco products.
(Prior Code, § 10-39) (Ord. 177, passed 1-7-1997) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see Wyo. Stat. §§ 14-3-301 through 14-4-305
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Simple assault is a misdemeanor punishable by a fine of not more
than $750.
      (2)   Battery is a misdemeanor punishable by imprisonment for not more
than six months, a fine of not more than $750, or both. Notwithstanding any
other provision of law, the term of probation imposed by a judge under §
130.04 may exceed the maximum term of imprisonment established for the offense
under§
130.04 provided the term of probation, together with any extension thereof,
shall in no case exceed one year.
      (3)   An unlawful contact under §
130.04(C) is a misdemeanor punishable by imprisonment for not more than six
months, a fine of not more than $750 or both.
   (C)   Any person, firm, or corporation violating any of the provisions of §
130.16 shall be charged with a misdemeanor and, upon conviction thereof, shall
be fined in an amount not less than $10 nor more than $750, plus the costs of
prosecution as set forth by the laws of the state or by the rules of the
Supreme Court.
(Prior Code, § 10-23)
   (D)   All persons identified in §
130.27(A) violating §
130.27 shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and shall be fined a minimum of
$350 plus court costs for the first offense. Convictions for a second offense
shall be a minimum of $500 plus court costs. Convictions for the third or
subsequent offenses shall be fined up to a maximum of $750 plus court costs.
   (E)   The penalty for §
130.45 is as follows.
      (1)   The first offense upon conviction is a fine of up to $250.
      (2)   The second offense upon conviction is a fine up to $450.
      (3)   The third offense upon conviction is a fine up to $750.
(Prior Code, § 10-41)

Ord. passed 9-11-2013)
